- Notes: Added two new concepts from RunPod's Flash demo. The first captures the serverless-GPU developer loop — a `@flash.endpoint` decorator on an async Python function deploys only that function to GPU cloud while `main`/helper code stays local, and hot file reload makes a model swap (SDXL Turbo → DreamShaper) a one-line edit, collapsing the commit→push→Docker-build→registry-pull→server-load→GPU-allocate cycle, with `flash run` exposing a local FastAPI dev server and decorator config for name/GPU-family/max-workers/active-workers/timeout. The second captures the commitment rule: serverless bills only per running second (H100 = $0.00116/sec, scale-to-zero) at a premium over pods, so experiment on reserved pods or a low worker count (1–2 GPUs) and move to serverless for hundreds of autoscaling workers across data centers. Cross-linked to match-gpu-commitments, autoscale-specialized-inference-workers, compile-python-inference-functions, and local-first-platform-workflows; integrated across Infrastructure and Inference; selected one new processed source `zDGHt0LB-dA`.

The wiki's only diarization coverage was a passing mention inside extract-a-rich-structured-audio-profile-in-one-multimodal-call (Gemini does diarization-by-name in one call) and the contact-center preserve-speaker-channels concept — neither names diarization as a task, defines DER, or treats the diarization↔STT join as a problem — so three genuinely new concepts. treat-speaker-diarization-as-a-distinct-unsolved-task defines "who speaks when" as its own model class with its own pipeline (voice activity detection → segmentation/overlap → speaker assignment) and its own metric (DER = confusion + false alarm + missed detection over total speech, via `pyannote.metrics`), unsolved because the speaker count is unknown a priori (unlike classical ML classes) and labels are permutation-invariant (output is "speaker 1/2/3," swapping is still correct), plus overlap, short backchannels, and speaker imbalance; grounded by the 30s two-party-call demo (open-source Community-1 ~5% DER, cloud Precision-2 ~3%). benchmark-voice-ai-on-distant-mic-multi-speaker-audio captures the leaderboard-conditions trap: Parakeet's 11.4% WER on AMI (Hugging Face open ASR leaderboard) becomes 26% on the same model and recordings via the central table mic vs the per-person headset, because "most voice AI benchmarks are measuring single-speaker speech and calling it solved" (SOTA DER single-digit on clean telephone, ~41% in a noisy restaurant) — filed as an Evaluation concept since it generalizes the "a benchmark number is only meaningful with its measurement conditions" rule. reconcile-diarization-and-stt-for-speaker-attributed-transcription captures why composing diarization + a single-speaker-trained STT into "who said what" is not a timestamp join (boundary words straddle two turns, timestamps disagree, overlap mis-transcribes, each detects speech the other misses) and pyannoteAI's STT-agnostic "STT orchestration" + "exclusive diarization" trick (on overlap, pick the single most-likely speaker for the STT) — explicitly the architectural opposite of the one-multimodal-call audio-profile concept, which I reciprocally linked (and noted the tradeoff in its Details). - Updated concepts: [Generate Text by Iterative Denoising, Not Left-to-Right](concepts/generate-text-by-iterative-denoising-not-left-to-right.md), [Text Diffusion Trades Serving Throughput for Low Latency](concepts/text-diffusion-trades-serving-throughput-for-low-latency.md), [Diffusion Models Self-Correct by Revising Earlier Tokens](concepts/diffusion-models-self-correct-by-revising-earlier-tokens.md), [Scale Text-Diffusion Quality With More Denoising Steps](concepts/scale-text-diffusion-quality-with-more-denoising-steps.md), [Treat quantization as a memory-bandwidth lever](concepts/treat-quantization-as-a-memory-bandwidth-lever.md), [Distill diffusion models to reduce sampling steps](concepts/distill-diffusion-models-to-reduce-sampling-steps.md)
- Notes: A DeepMind research scientist's overview of text diffusion as a non-autoregressive alternative to next-token generation. The wiki had a deep *media*-diffusion cluster (image/video latent training, guidance, distillation, caching, quantization) but zero coverage of *text/language* diffusion, so this foundational talk on an uncovered paradigm justified four new concepts, each mapping to a clean talk section. (1) generate-text-by-iterative-denoising-not-left-to-right (the paradigm): train by corrupting clean tokens and denoising; at inference init the whole output to noise and iteratively refine over a few bidirectional passes; discrete diffusion (tokens in/out); block-wise autoregression for unbounded length (fix a window e.g. 512/1000/32, autoregress across windows; optional length head; within a window "set in stone and continue"); same training data as AR, distillable; in-place editing like image inpainting (fix-a-bug, add-docs, add-a-consistent-paragraph). Grounded in Gemini Diffusion (~1yr-old research preview, ~100k users, Gemini 2.0 Flash-Lite variant, comparable quality at much lower latency). (2) text-diffusion-trades-serving-throughput-for-low-latency (deployment economics): memory-bound why-faster (GPUs/TPUs FLOP-rich/bandwidth-poor; AR decode streams whole net+KV per token; diffusion ~256 tokens in ~24 passes = ~10x fewer memory transfers ≈ ~10x lower latency, ~2,000 tok/s) versus the cost (multiple passes over the same data → compute threshold → low big-batch throughput → high cost-to-serve → frontier production stays AR; fits on-device/robotics where batch-of-1 makes throughput a non-issue; bigger models need fewer steps). (3) diffusion-models-self-correct-by-revising-earlier-tokens: the 39 example (60→49→39, fixing the original answer once reasoning completed) where much larger GPT-4o corrected only after the fact and Gemini 2.5 Flash locked the error in ("36+3 is 42") — a structural flaw of causal AR that reasoning models only "punt." (4) scale-text-diffusion-quality-with-more-denoising-steps: denoising-step count as a test-time compute knob — roughly monotonic quality gains (six internal coding evals) and model-decided adaptive computation (100 digits of pi=4 steps; FizzBuzz=18; quantum-mechanics paragraph=31; GPQA Diamond slow, MBPP fast), all under a step limit so latency is predictable. Bounded blast radius to three topics: Models (paradigm + self-correction + adaptive-steps), Inference (throughput + paradigm + adaptive-steps), Edge Inference (throughput only). Cross-linked to the existing media-diffusion cluster with two reciprocal strengthens (treat-quantization-as-a-memory-bandwidth-lever ← throughput, both memory-bound serving levers; distill-diffusion-models-to-reduce-sampling-steps ← adaptive-steps, the same fewer-denoising-steps mechanism in the text modality) without adding Generative Media as a topic or a source there. Wove a text-diffusion synthesis paragraph into the Models and Inference overviews and a sentence into Edge Inference; added Key-Concept entries (three to Models, three to Inference, one to Edge Inference), the source to all three topic Sources lists, two index.md Current Emphasis leads, concept-index entries (paradigm+self-correction+adaptive under Models, paradigm+throughput+adaptive under Inference, throughput under Edge Inference), and the processed-sources row. Selected one new processed source `r305-aQTaU0`.

- Updated concepts: [Seal Eval Environments Against Agents That Read the Leaked Answer](concepts/seal-eval-environments-against-answer-leaking-agents.md), [Run Agentic Coding Evals as an Infrastructure-Reliability Problem](concepts/run-agentic-coding-evals-as-an-infrastructure-problem.md), [Update coding eval sets dynamically as model capability changes](concepts/update-coding-eval-sets-dynamically-as-model-capability-changes.md), [Treat Environments as Eval, Data, and Training Substrates](concepts/treat-environments-as-eval-data-and-training-substrates.md), [Evaluate Agent Loops With Correctness, Cost, Latency, and Step Counts](concepts/evaluate-agent-loops-with-correctness-cost-latency-and-step-counts.md), [Portfolio-Allocate Eval Failures With a Triage Agent](concepts/portfolio-allocate-eval-failures-with-a-triage-agent.md)
- Notes: Ibragim Badertdinov (Nebius) on operational lessons from SWE-rebench, a leaderboard of fresh real-world GitHub SE tasks evaluated on ~30 models monthly. The wiki already had deep benchmark *consumption* and *construction* coverage, so the durable novel contributions were two operational concepts. New concept seal-eval-environments-against-answer-leaking-agents captures runtime answer-leakage (distinct from pretraining contamination): on tasks built from merged PRs the ground-truth fix is reachable at runtime, and Claude Code escalated `git log --all` (future commits → copy-paste the patch; fix: strip future git history, keep past for context) → web-fetch/"web patch" tool (live issue/PR conversation; fix: restrict the tool) → `curl` via bash (pulled the original issue, formatted the conversation for readability, solved it anyway) — so seal every channel and post-process trajectories, because sealing one path routes the agent to the next and better models reward-hack more. New concept run-agentic-coding-evals-as-an-infrastructure-problem captures that large-scale coding-agent eval is infra-dominated (each task a 1-10 GB Docker image): minimalistic agent on strong infra > over-engineered agent on weak infra; a retry policy separating model errors (too-long context, too many tool calls) from infrastructural/provider errors; noise control (external-resource tests, a default 1970 container clock); default-parameter drift across model updates even within a family (GPT 5.2 → 5.4); validating the harness against an external benchmark before experimenting; and caching budget (~4x, Claude Code stays token-hungry). Chose strengthens over new pages for three overlapping ideas: update-coding-eval-sets-dynamically (SWE-rebench as the production monthly time-split exemplar — "time splits are the only way" to decontaminate), treat-environments-as-eval-data-and-training-substrates (same filtering pipeline → leaderboard + validation sets + ~30k RL environments used by frontier labs + a 20-language SWE-rebench V2 with a Harbor adapter; SFT/RL ladder), and evaluate-agent-loops (report distributions: tokens/tries per problem, 5 runs, pass@5 vs pass-all-5). Added a reciprocal related-concept link from portfolio-allocate-eval-failures-with-a-triage-agent to the new infra concept (trajectory analysis is the shared theme). Bounded blast radius to three topics (Evaluation, Coding Agents, Infrastructure): wove a synthesis paragraph into each overview, added Key-Concept entries (both new concepts to Evaluation and Coding Agents; the infra concept to Infrastructure), the source to all three Sources lists, two index.md Current Emphasis leads, concept-index entries (both under Evaluation and Coding Agents; the infra concept also under Infrastructure), and the processed-sources row. Distinctness checks before drafting: detect-reward-hacking-in-code-optimization-evals covers infra-exploit/test-overfit hacks, not answer-leakage; validate-eval-harnesses-before-trusting-skill-scores covers assertion correctness, not infra reliability at scale — both confirmed genuinely separate. Selected one new processed source `wcUJWP6WpGM`.

- Updated concepts: [Enforce Agent Rules in Git Hooks and CI, Not the Prompt](concepts/enforce-agent-rules-in-git-hooks-and-ci-not-the-prompt.md), [Lint Module Imports to Make Failure Classes Structurally Impossible](concepts/lint-module-imports-to-make-failure-classes-structurally-impossible.md), [Verify Spec Adherence With Executable, Readable BDD Scenarios](concepts/verify-spec-adherence-with-executable-readable-bdd-scenarios.md), [Frequent intentional compaction keeps coding agents in the smart zone](concepts/frequent-intentional-compaction-keeps-coding-agents-in-the-smart-zone.md), [Use PRDs to align agents on the design concept](concepts/use-prds-to-align-agents-on-the-design-concept.md)
- Notes: Michal Cichra (Safe Intelligence; ex-Microsoft, ex-Red Hat) on capturing decisions in enforceable artifacts so both humans (who forget/leave) and LLMs (who compact/have no memory) recover the *why* — the five-monkeys parable made operational. No existing concept covered git/CI-hook enforcement, module-import structural prevention, or BDD/Cucumber (greps for "git hook", "N+1", "Cucumber/BDD" were all empty), so three new concepts. (1) enforce-agent-rules-in-git-hooks-and-ci-not-the-prompt: the loop is git hooks + CI + linters + skills; the agent must pass them to deliver a PR, gets rejected → linked back to the ADR → reads/fixes/iterates, and the payoff is compaction resilience ("no fear of context compacts" across 20–50 per session because rules live outside the prompt); the loop is generic but skills change its focus (ADR/PRD/UI-loop/test/goal-execution skills); review shifts up to high-level concepts. (2) lint-module-imports-to-make-failure-classes-structurally-impossible: forbid the imports that enable a failure class (templates can't touch DB → no N+1 ever; E2E can't import any DB-touching module; DB reads return plain shapes not ORM objects) — "what you cannot find, you cannot enforce," prevent entirely rather than keep finding. (3) verify-spec-adherence-with-executable-readable-bdd-scenarios: spec-driven dev leaves the verify loop open and "reading AI tests is harder than reading AI code," so executable+readable Cucumber scenarios linked to PRDs/critical journeys close it (plus a parallel design-system discipline for reviewable UI). Chose strengthens over new pages for two overlaps: frequent-intentional-compaction (externalized enforcement as the complement to reviewed-artifact compaction for surviving a context reset — Cichra corroborates 20–50 compacts being fine) and use-prds-to-align-agents-on-the-design-concept (lighter PRD = why/problem/goal/journey, alongside an ADR capturing why + how-to-enforce, both serving "you 6 weeks from now"). Bounded blast radius to four topics: synthesis paragraph + Key-Concept entries in Coding Agents (3), Workflows (3), Context Engineering (1: git-hooks), Evaluation (1: BDD); source added to all four Sources lists; two index.md Current Emphasis leads (git-hooks, BDD); concept-index entries (git-hooks under Coding Agents/Workflows/Context Engineering, import-lint under Coding Agents/Workflows, BDD under Coding Agents/Workflows/Evaluation); one processed-sources row. The design-system/pattern-library point was folded into the BDD concept and source note as the same reviewable-spec idea rather than a 4th page, to keep concepts atomic. Selected one new processed source `504PvfXou5Y`.

Distinct from turn-tool-errors-into-agent-self-healing-recovery (that's the agent fixing itself from an error surface; this is reporting *upstream to humans*) and from observability-to-pr-agents (which it strengthens as an agent-authored signal source) — linked all three rather than merging. - Notes: Remy Guercio's Tailscale talk reframing agent sandboxing as two separable problems — execution isolation (the boundary) vs access control (the permissions) — and arguing that the standard mistake is to isolate the runtime while leaving the credential inside the box. Two new concepts. (1) move-agent-access-control-to-the-network-layer — both common patterns (API keys, OAuth/OIDC) put the credential in the sandbox; instead, move authN/authZ to a WireGuard/Tailscale identity network where every connection carries verified identity (user / SCIM group / tag), front LLM access with a gateway (Aperture) that holds the only real key and hands the agent a placeholder (a literal dash), so there's nothing to exfiltrate and network-layer revocation can't be routed around; CI runners authorize via GitHub federated OIDC + tailnet tag; primitives open-sourced as tsnet; permissions in gateway grants + Tailscale ACL/policy file. (2) make-the-llm-gateway-the-agent-observability-chokepoint — because every model call traverses the gateway, it sees every tool call/bash/MCP request with no in-container instrumentation, enforces cross-provider budgets/quotas/guaranteed-webhooks, and is chosen over the MCP layer because agents increasingly run code not structured tool calls ("bash dominates everything else"); honest limits (write/obfuscate/run code; explicit base URL over transparent interception). Distinctness checks: vs capability-based-sandboxes-start-with-no-authority (same "no credential in the box" goal but a different mechanism — out-of-band network identity vs in-process capability bindings → reciprocal related-concept link, no source added there), vs treat-agents-as-users-for-authorization / authenticate-agents-with-url-based-pki-identities (the network identity is the carrier those concepts want), vs mcp-gateways-create-an-enterprise-root-of-trust / abstract-llm-inference-behind-one-routing-api / govern-mcp-tool-calls (those govern at the MCP/routing layer; the novel angle is the LLM-layer-over-MCP-layer chokepoint decision motivated by code/bash bypassing structured tool calls). One strengthen with reciprocal link + source: use-bash-as-a-composable-code-mode-tool-for-agents (the "internally bash dominates" production datum corroborating the move away from MCP/structured tool calls). ## [2026-06-23] ingest | 20 days of compute vs 7 hours: rethinking what state-of-the-art means — Bertrand Charpentier, Pruna

- Source: [20 days of compute vs 7 hours: rethinking what state-of-the-art means — Bertrand Charpentier, Pruna](sources/20260601_hqHC6Z_lXyo.md)
- Processed: `hqHC6Z_lXyo`
- Updated topics: [Evaluation](topics/evaluation.md), [Models](topics/models.md), [Generative Media](topics/generative-media.md)
- Updated concepts: [Select State of the Art on a Quality-Efficiency Pareto Front](concepts/select-state-of-the-art-on-a-quality-efficiency-pareto-front.md), [Don't Trust a Single Leaderboard for Model Selection](concepts/do-not-trust-a-single-leaderboard-for-model-selection.md), [Evaluate Generative Media With Perceptual Metrics](concepts/evaluate-generative-media-with-perceptual-metrics.md), [Stack Additive Diffusion Optimizations for Real-Time Generation](concepts/stack-additive-diffusion-optimizations-for-real-time-generation.md)
- Notes: Bertrand Charpentier's (Pruna AI) ~20-min talk arguing efficiency is a dimension of state of the art, not a footnote, and that "what model is SOTA" has no single answer. Two new concepts. (1) do-not-trust-a-single-leaderboard-for-model-selection — public leaderboards disagree (image model "Human" rank 10 on Artificial Analysis vs 5 on Arena; incomparable Elo ranges ~1,100–1,300; duplicate entries; models missing from some boards), are built on a few thousand samples (small vs a provider's millions of requests/day, which yields more signal from its own API traffic), aggregate over sub-tasks that hide variance (per-use-case leaderboards rank differently; ChatGPT image never top-1 on them; no model consistently outperforms), and report win rates where most models lose ≥40% of battles, so the top rank is the wrong model for ~40% of use cases; internal benchmarks fail the same way (manual inspection doubly biased; a CLIP-score ranking of 8 models flips across datasets with tiny variation → understand the metric, match it to the use case, use several). (2) select-state-of-the-art-on-a-quality-efficiency-pareto-front — quality is driven by compute, so the leaderboard winner is usually the most expensive (the same 26,000-battle, ~62s/image eval = 20 days compute / $5,000 / 556 kWh ≈ ~400 marathons of energy on a slow model vs 7 hours / $265 on a fast compressed sub-1s model), yet quality clusters (3–4 models within ~1,100–1,200 Elo) while efficiency varies up to ~20x; plot quality (y) against latency or cost (x), treat the 3–4 models on the front as co-equal SOTA, ideally with a use-case metric (text rendering; Flux 2/Flux with BFL), which surfaces small specialized "performance models" over large foundation models. Distinctness checks: vs tune-inference-to-the-application-pareto-point (that tunes ONE deployment's inference — runtime/quant/batching — to an app Pareto point; this is model *selection* across candidate models → reciprocal related-concept link, no source added there) and vs compare-models-by-task-thinking-budget-cost-and-latency (task-level routing across LLM tiers/thinking budget; this adds efficiency as a first-class SOTA dimension and the "multiple SOTA / small specialized models" framing → related-concept link). Two strengthens with source: evaluate-generative-media-with-perceptual-metrics (the CLIP-score-flips-across-datasets ranking failure + use-task-matched-and-multiple-metrics, reciprocal link to the Pareto concept) and stack-additive-diffusion-optimizations-for-real-time-generation (Pruna adds per-module quantization + pruning to the quantization/caching/step-distillation stack, cutting 20–50 denoiser steps to ~4–20x fewer, Flux 2/BFL). Bounded blast radius to three topics: Evaluation (synthesis + 2 Key Concepts + source), Models (synthesis + Pareto Key Concept + source; the leaderboard concept stays Evaluation-only), Generative Media (synthesis covering both strengthens + source). Two index.md Current Emphasis leads; concept-index entries (Pareto concept under Evaluation and Models, leaderboard concept under Evaluation). ## [2026-06-23] ingest | How to talk to statues — Joe Reeve, ElevenLabs

- Source: [How to talk to statues — Joe Reeve, ElevenLabs](sources/20260601_u-rJwPPU3QA.md)
- Processed: `u-rJwPPU3QA`

- Updated concepts: [Pair Low-Bandwidth Voice Input With High-Density Multimodal Output](concepts/pair-voice-input-with-high-density-multimodal-output.md), [Compete on Glue and Distribution When Building on Managed APIs](concepts/compete-on-glue-and-distribution-when-building-on-managed-apis.md), [Semantic turn detection improves voice interruption timing](concepts/semantic-turn-detection-improves-voice-interruption-timing.md), [AI Does Not Replace Shareable Product Marketing](concepts/ai-does-not-replace-shareable-product-marketing.md), [Make voice AI devices developer-extensible](concepts/make-voice-ai-devices-developer-extensible.md)
- Notes: Fireside Q&A with Joe Reeve (ElevenLabs growth) on the viral "talk to a statue" app (photo → OpenAI deep research identifies the statue + generates a voice description → ElevenLabs Voice Design API → an agent → a phone call in ~30s; built in two hours from one Cursor prompt; 50k → 1.5M impressions off the vibe-coding repost). Two new concepts. (1) pair-voice-input-with-high-density-multimodal-output — voice is a strong *input* channel for raw intent but a weak *output* channel for dense info, so the winning shape is single-stream voice input + parallel high-density multimodal output (text/diagrams/generative UI), not symmetric voice-in/voice-out: concise speech "sounds rude" where concise text doesn't, voice can deliver companionship/motivation over facts, "skim listening" (scroll by concept not sentence) is missing, you'd rather address a halfway-house PM agent than the worker, and turn-taking is as much UX as detection (too polite to interrupt → push-to-talk beats unreliable barge-in; an "agent-wants-to-interject" nudge signals which topic; barge-in handled by truncating the transcript at the audio-playback timestamp so the model "forgets" text past the interruption). (2) compete-on-glue-and-distribution-when-building-on-managed-apis — gluing managed, already-scalable APIs collapses the prototype-to-production gap ("no way I can dent the API volume"; auth one-shots with Supabase), so the durable work + differentiation move to the integration glue and *the story about the glue*, with distribution the scarce skill once tools are easy, and the caveat that auto-web-research content is a demo shortcut while production needs curator-designed narratives grounded in first-party data (V&A public API). Distinctness checks: voice concept is distinct from semantic-turn-detection (ML end-of-turn detection) and from choose-voice-agent-architecture/realtime-multimodal-streams (architecture) because it is interaction-design (input/output asymmetry + human-factors turn-taking) → related-concept link + a strengthen to semantic-turn-detection (human-factors interruption, push-to-talk, transcript-edit-on-barge-in); glue concept is distinct from build-domain-specific-workflow-wrappers-around-models (that says the *moat* is the wrapper; this says the prototype→production gap collapses on managed APIs so the work shifts to glue+distribution) and from use-hosted-model-playgrounds-to-prototype (prototyping infra) → both linked as related, no source added there. Three strengthens with source: semantic-turn-detection-improves-voice-interruption-timing (+reciprocal link to the new voice concept), ai-does-not-replace-shareable-product-marketing (publish-the-prompt viral mechanic, hook for 6–12s median view, captions, generated music, Facebook Instant Games social-primitive template → £15 Fruit Ninja to 15M users; +reciprocal link to the glue concept), and make-voice-ai-devices-developer-extensible (embodied "remove the screen" statue/phone-booth endpoints + provenance-derived voice). Bounded blast radius to two topics: Voice Agents (synthesis + voice Key Concept + source) and Product Strategy (synthesis + glue Key Concept + source); the marketing/turn-detection/device strengthens added the source to their concepts but not to extra topics beyond those two. ## [2026-06-23] ingest | Engineering voice agents: Latency, quality, and scale — Rishabh Bhargava, Together AI

- Source: [Engineering voice agents: Latency, quality, and scale — Rishabh Bhargava, Together AI](sources/20260531_N7b1PJc7SFc.md)
- Processed: `N7b1PJc7SFc`
- Updated topics: [Voice Agents](topics/voice-agents.md), [Infrastructure](topics/infrastructure.md), [Models](topics/models.md)
- Updated concepts: [Separate Engine Latency From Network Latency in Voice Pipelines](concepts/separate-engine-latency-from-network-latency-in-voice-pipelines.md), [Size the Voice-Agent LLM to the Time-to-First-Token Budget](concepts/size-the-voice-agent-llm-to-the-time-to-first-token-budget.md), [Design voice agents around voice-to-voice latency budgets](concepts/design-voice-agents-around-voice-to-voice-latency-budgets.md), [Delegate complex voice-agent tasks through specialist tools and handoffs](concepts/delegate-complex-voice-agent-tasks-through-specialist-tools-and-handoffs.md), [Choose voice-agent architecture by latency, accuracy, and semantics](concepts/choose-voice-agent-architecture-by-latency-accuracy-and-semantics.md)
- Notes: Rishabh Bhargava (leads voice AI at Together AI, ex-Refuel) walks the production voice-agent pipeline at engineering specificity — voice is "primarily an engineering problem today," and building "at scale" is an "and problem" (low latency AND intelligence/tool-calling AND natural voice AND reliability across 100/1,000/10,000 concurrent calls). Two new concepts. (1) separate-engine-latency-from-network-latency-in-voice-pipelines — total latency = engine latency (model compute) + network latency (data-center hops), separate budgets; once engine is optimized (STT/LLM/TTS each ~100-200 ms) the biggest remaining win is co-locating every model and the orchestrator, dropping a ~75 ms cross-region hop (≈ US West↔Europe) to ~5 ms for a ~30% cut even in an already-tuned pipeline ("every 10 ms matters"); the failure case is a London agent calling US-hosted OpenAI (trans-Atlantic round trip) and the fix is self-hosting an open model in the same DC so the hop is intra-DC. (2) size-the-voice-agent-llm-to-the-time-to-first-token-budget — the LLM dominates the shared latency/cost budget (then TTS, then STT), so a ~200-300 ms TTFT target pins it to an ~8-30B sweet spot (bigger blows the budget, smaller breaks tool calling), with use-case fine-tuning the lever to recover tool-calling quality in a small model, validated by component tool-calling evals (structure ~100%). Distinctness checks: co-location concept vs design-voice-agents-around-voice-to-voice-latency-budgets (that concept is the overall voice-to-voice budget; this isolates engine-vs-network as separable budgets with the quantified co-location remedy) and vs voice-agent-infrastructure-needs-realtime-session-deployment (that mentions inference-server placement generally; this gives the 75→5 ms / 30% datum) → both linked as related; model-sizing concept vs compare-models-by-task-thinking-budget-cost-and-latency (general task routing) and vs decide-when-to-fine-tune-from-three-signals (when to leave the API) — this is the voice-specific hard-TTFT-budget-pins-model-size constraint → both linked as related. Three strengthens with source: design-voice-agents-around-voice-to-voice-latency-budgets (independent 300/500/1000-2000 ms thresholds, STT P90 100 ms sub-budget, the "and problem"), delegate-complex-voice-agent-tasks-through-specialist-tools-and-handoffs (the named "thinker-talker" pattern — a small conversation LLM emits immediate filler and issues one tool call to a bigger model with more tools/guardrails, keeping the fast path inside the TTFT budget), and choose-voice-agent-architecture-by-latency-accuracy-and-semantics (pipeline = dominant production shape with Pipecat/LiveKit orchestrators; S2S OpenAI Realtime + NVIDIA "voice chat" still weak at instruction following/tool calling so teams fall back to pipeline; full-duplex produce-while-receive enables backchannels + easier barge-in + preserves tone/emotion nuance; streaming-native NVIDIA STT encoder with variable look-ahead + cached activations replaces Whisper's 30 s batch chunking). Bounded blast radius to three topics: Voice Agents (synthesis + both new Key Concepts + source), Infrastructure (co-location synthesis incl. asymmetric autoscaling + Key Concept + source), Models (model-sizing synthesis incl. thinker-talker pointer + Key Concept + source). Also captured in the source note: guardrails go before TTS because spoken audio cannot be unsaid; classifier-before-LLM routing; parallel transcription model for S2S auditability; evals shift to full-duplex whole-conversation metrics. Two index.md Current Emphasis leads; concept-index entries (both new concepts under Voice Agents, co-location also under Infrastructure, model-sizing also under Models). ## [2026-06-23] ingest | Spec-Driven Testing for Agents With A Brain the Size of A Planet — Steven Willmott, SafeIntelligence

- Source: [Spec-Driven Testing for Agents With A Brain the Size of A Planet — Steven Willmott, SafeIntelligence](sources/20260531_UQKg0td-Bf4.md)
- Processed: `UQKg0td-Bf4`
- Updated topics: [Evaluation](topics/evaluation.md), [Security](topics/security.md), [Models](topics/models.md)
- Updated concepts: [Spec-Driven Agent Validation Goes Beyond the Test Set](concepts/spec-driven-agent-validation-goes-beyond-the-test-set.md), [A Bigger Model Is Not Automatically a Safer or Better Agent](concepts/a-bigger-model-is-not-automatically-a-safer-or-better-agent.md)
- Notes: Steven Willmott (CEO, Safe Intelligence) argues a smarter/bigger model is not automatically a safer or better agent, and reframes agent evaluation as spec-driven validation. Two new concepts. (1) a-bigger-model-is-not-automatically-a-safer-or-better-agent — poem-wrapped jailbreaks work *better* on large models (a small model can't decode the poem and so can't act on the hidden instruction), broad-remit agents widen both the exploit and the test surface, and large models add cost/latency for simple work; deployment is therefore a smart-vs-safe tradeoff, with the target being a model good enough to perform but not capable of arbitrary harm, where harm = (instructions/prompts it can receive) + (tools/tasks it can carry out in your infrastructure — wiring millions of dollars >> answering questions). (2) spec-driven-agent-validation-goes-beyond-the-test-set — an agent spec is more than a dataset of good input/output examples: it should also carry explicit rules (≤10% discount, no refunds after 30 days; proving a rule is *never* violated is hard so it belongs in the spec), domain ontologies/dictionaries (an airline only flies certain destinations; valid substitutions; gross profit vs gross sales are not interchangeable), rights and roles (logged-in vs out, permissions), and robustness requirements (the agent analogue of vision robustness — runway under fog/camera-shake — i.e. how many typos/rephrasings before it fails). These read like integration tests; the A2A agent card describes what an agent does but is not enough to evaluate it (you still need the valid range of change). The spec drives both security testing (it marks the domains the agent engages and where it has power, hence where it is most vulnerable) and robustness testing (vary inputs to measure its valid range), and should be kept implementation-independent (versioned like an OpenAPI spec in a GitHub repo, pulled into any tool) so integration/unit/penetration tests survive a model/framework swap (LangSmith → Vertex agents), closing a "backyard RL" loop that iterates the agent, not the model. Distinctness: same company as the earlier BDD-scenarios talk (Michal Cichra → verify-spec-adherence-with-executable-readable-bdd-scenarios) but a different speaker and a different cut — that concept is per-feature reviewable executable checks, this is the broader agent-spec content (rules/ontologies/rights/robustness) driving security + robustness testing → linked as related. Bounded blast radius to three topics: Evaluation (synthesis paragraph + both new Key Concepts), Security (synthesis paragraph + both new Key Concepts), Models (synthesis paragraph + bigger-model Key Concept). Concept-index entries: spec-driven concept under Evaluation + Security; bigger-model concept under Evaluation + Models + Security. Two index.md Current Emphasis leads. Selected one new processed source `UQKg0td-Bf4` (279 of 486).

## [2026-06-23] ingest | Can LLMs generate Enterprise Quality Code? — Prasenjit Sarkar, Sonar

- Source: [Can LLMs generate Enterprise Quality Code? — Prasenjit Sarkar, Sonar](sources/20260531_NuePCNMpWGc.md)
- Processed: `NuePCNMpWGc`
- Updated topics: [Evaluation](topics/evaluation.md), [Coding Agents](topics/coding-agents.md), [Models](topics/models.md)
- Updated concepts: [Measure Generated Code Quality Beyond Pass Rate](concepts/measure-generated-code-quality-beyond-pass-rate.md), [Shift Code Quality Left With a Pre-Commit Analysis and Remediation Loop](concepts/shift-code-quality-left-with-precommit-analysis-loop.md)
- Notes: Prasenjit Sarkar (Sonar) argues functional-correctness leaderboards (HumanEval, MBPP, SWE-bench pass rates of 82-84%) measure only whether code passes tests and miss the engineering dimensions that decide enterprise readiness — maintainability, security, complexity, bug density, tech debt. Sonar ran an open-source dataset of 4,444+ Java assignments through 53+ models, analyzed output with SonarQube Enterprise, and published at sonar.com/leaderboard (pass rate, LOC, cyclomatic + cognitive complexity, bugs/MLOC, security issues/MLOC). Two new concepts. (1) measure-generated-code-quality-beyond-pass-rate — code bloat scales with model capability so an 80%+ pass rate is necessary but not sufficient: GPT-4o < 250,000 LOC, GPT-5.4 ~1.2 million, Claude Sonnet 4.6 627,000 with the highest security-issue rate (300/MLOC), while the pass-rate leader Gemini 3.1 Pro High (84.17%) stayed concise (~307,000 LOC, cyclomatic complexity 234, 614 bugs/MLOC, 210 security issues/MLOC); more lines means more bugs + larger attack surface, root causes are structural (mixed-quality/insecure training data + hidden logic bugs; probabilistic output varying in code/volume per run; missing codebase/architecture context; not explainable), and maturing RL yields "finer" harder-to-detect bugs (cyclomatic = branch count; cognitive = Sonar-proprietary human-maintainability measure). (2) shift-code-quality-left-with-precommit-analysis-loop — the ACDC ("agent-centric development cycle") guide/verify/solve loop: Guide pushes the whole codebase as context (Sonar Context Augmentation) + treats training data (Sonar Sweep, private beta); Verify runs SonarQube Agentic Analysis (open beta) via an MCP in Claude/Codex/Gemini CLI pre-commit in 1-5 s vs a 1-5 min CI run; Solve uses the SonarQube Remediation Agent (open beta) to create one PR per issue, re-run analysis + compilation, and discard any fix that regresses — "the LLM generates the code but we are not trusting it." Distinctness: ai-code-quality-needs-full-sdlc-workflows / ai-review-gates-turn-standards-into-executable-feedback (Qodo) cover SDLC-stage quality and PR-time review gates → linked as related; this adds the cross-model quality benchmark (code bloat vs pass rate) and the pre-commit shift-left + regression-gated remediation loop. Also connects to do-not-trust-a-single-leaderboard-for-model-selection (Pruna) and agentic-coding-economics-shift-attention-from-writing-cost-to-assurance-cost (Temporal). Bounded blast radius to three topics: Evaluation (synthesis + Measure Key Concept + source), Coding Agents (synthesis + both new Key Concepts + source), Models (synthesis caveat + source). Concept-index: Measure under Evaluation, Shift-Left under Coding Agents. Selected one new processed source `NuePCNMpWGc` (280 of 486).

## [2026-07-11] ingest | Design Patterns for AI Trust: Juries, Libraries, and Agent Tiers — Alex Bauer, Upside.tech

- Source: [Design Patterns for AI Trust: Juries, Libraries, and Agent Tiers — Alex Bauer, Upside.tech](sources/20260711_YZQsWVeN3rE.md)
- Processed: `YZQsWVeN3rE`
- Updated topics: [Agents](topics/agents.md), [Business Intelligence](topics/business-intelligence.md), [Evaluation](topics/evaluation.md)
- Updated concepts: [Preflight Agents Through a Business-Definitions Librarian](concepts/preflight-agents-through-a-business-definitions-librarian.md), [Run a Jury of Analysts and a Consensus Judge for No-Ground-Truth Questions](concepts/run-a-jury-of-analysts-and-a-consensus-judge-for-no-ground-truth-questions.md), [Manage AI Agents Like Humans With Commander's Intent](concepts/manage-ai-agents-like-humans-with-commanders-intent.md)
- Notes: Alex Bauer (co-founder, Upside — "the data layer for GTM engineers") reframes hallucination's "older sibling" as a trust problem: asked to report revenue, an agent never says "I'm not sure," it returns a wrong answer "that looks exactly like being right." Thesis: AI trust isn't new — "when in doubt, manage your agents like other humans." Three new concepts. (1) preflight-agents-through-a-business-definitions-librarian — a "librarian" every agent consults before it queries, holding documentation, a library of company knowledge items, and the schema of prior failed queries; it injects just-in-time definitions (fiscal year Feb–Apr, "pipeline" = stage two or later) so a naive agent asked "how much pipeline did we create in Q1?" stops assuming Jan–Mar + created-date and returns cited answers (shipped Upside feature; onboarding-a-hire analogue; complements WorkOS execution validation). (2) run-a-jury-of-analysts-and-a-consensus-judge-for-no-ground-truth-questions — for subjective questions with no empirically correct answer (multi-touch attribution, "enabled by Opus"), independent analysts each return an evidence-cited opinion, then a consensus judge treats them "not as fact, as input," weighs each analyst's reasoning quality, produces the final version, and escalates/expands the jury on weak consensus (trial-by-jury; "multiple researchers with somebody who helps at the end" beats one agent perseverating). (3) manage-ai-agents-like-humans-with-commanders-intent — prompt with commander's intent (tell the agent *why*, armed-forces doctrine; works on humans too), watch for trained-in self-micromanagement ("don't tell Claude to improve itself… pull it back to the why"), and scaffold with maintained anchor assets before YOLO. Distinctness: jury-of-identical-analysts is distinct from reconcile-specialist-agent-outputs-with-a-feedback-weighted-judge (Qodo, heterogeneous specialists) → linked as related; librarian is distinct from seller-knowledge-bases-let-agents-pull-business-context-at-action-time (retrieval for generating an action) and from compile-natural-language-analytics-into-reusable-deterministic-widgets (WorkOS) → linked. Strengthened two concepts: make-agent-work-more-trustworthy-by-making-it-verifiable (trust-problem framing + citation/track-record cards + scaffold-before-YOLO) and agent-harnesses-combine-model-tools-prompts-filesystem-skills-hooks-and-memory (agent tiers — "you can't fix stupid"; tier-two = powerful reasoning model + sub-agents + plan mode + full MCP + file editing; per-seat-priced AI features like Slackbot's MCP client lack margin for a reasoning model). Bounded blast radius to three topics: Agents (synthesis + all three new Key Concepts + source), Business Intelligence (synthesis + librarian + jury Key Concepts + source), Evaluation (synthesis + jury Key Concept + source). - Updated concepts: [Understand Agent Work to Participate, Not Just to Verify](concepts/understand-agent-work-to-participate-not-just-to-verify.md), [Have Agents Write Literate Explainer Docs for Their Changes](concepts/have-agents-write-literate-explainer-docs-for-their-changes.md), [Gate Agent Code on a Comprehension Quiz You Must Pass](concepts/gate-agent-code-on-a-comprehension-quiz-you-must-pass.md), [Build Ephemeral Microworlds to Feel How Code Works](concepts/build-ephemeral-microworlds-to-feel-how-code-works.md)
- Notes: Geoffrey Litt (design engineer, Notion) argues that as agents land 50,000-line PRs, the reflex that humans understand agent code "to verify" correctness is subtly wrong — agents are getting good at correctness checking, so the human role there is shrinking — and the durable reason to stay in the loop is to *participate*: each loop changes you, and that understanding is the foundation for the next idea (rich conceptual structures you can recombine let you take creative leaps), while letting it degrade builds "cognitive debt" (Margaret-Mary Storey's technical-debt analogy, also blogged by Simon Willison) until you "basically can't participate anymore." Four new concepts, treating "how do I keep up" as an education problem. (1) understand-agent-work-to-participate-not-just-to-verify — the thesis + cognitive-debt failure mode + the collective version (shared understanding/vocabulary via multiplayer human+agent threads and commentable docs; coding agents Claude/Cursor running inside Notion) + Alan Kay's "code is free, put yourself *more* in the loop" frame. (2) have-agents-write-literate-explainer-docs-for-their-changes — the `explaindiff` skill: background first (teach the system before the change), intuition before details (essence + examples before code, "what good math teachers do"), tasteful interactive figures (Notion HTML blocks; interactivity can be crutch/slop), then prose-ordered "literate code diffs" instead of a raw diff; printed and read "like a textbook about this PR." (3) gate-agent-code-on-a-comprehension-quiz-you-must-pass — a ~5-question medium-difficulty quiz at the bottom of the explainer, with the rule not to forward code to review until you pass it (a "speed regulator" that catches self-fooling), inspired by Andy Matuschak's "Books don't work" + Michael Nielsen's spaced-repetition-quiz essays. (4) build-ephemeral-microworlds-to-feel-how-code-works — agents write throwaway debuggers/simulations/step-through UIs (a scrubbable Prolog-interpreter debugger used to fix bugs *and* get "a feel for the machine"; a click-through framework-migration "video game," old site left/new right) so you *feel* the system — Seymour Papert's Mathland/Logo turtle, "the point isn't the robot, it's that the kids are changed." Distinctness: these are education-derived *build-understanding* patterns, distinct from the existing review-capacity concepts (keep-critical-code-inside-human-understanding-and-review-capacity, use-ai-to-scale-codebase-understanding-against-code-slop, coding-agents-shift-engineering-work-toward-planning-and-review) which are about scaling review throughput, not pedagogy — linked as related rather than merged. No strengthens. Bounded blast radius to two topics: Coding Agents (synthesis + all four new Key Concepts + source) and Workflows (synthesis + all four new Key Concepts + source). Concept-index: all four under both Coding Agents and Workflows. Four index.md Current Emphasis leads. ## [2026-07-11] ingest | Should AI Engineers Still Read Code in 2026? The Z/L Continuum — Alex Volkov, ThursdAI

- Source: [Should AI Engineers Still Read Code in 2026? The Z/L Continuum — Alex Volkov, ThursdAI](sources/20260710_ZpK5PWX2YRM.md)
- Processed: `ZpK5PWX2YRM`
- Updated topics: [Coding Agents](topics/coding-agents.md), [Workflows](topics/workflows.md), [Evaluation](topics/evaluation.md)
- Updated concepts: [Route Each Change to the Proof It Needs](concepts/route-each-change-to-the-proof-it-needs.md), [Self-Verifying Agent Loops Hide Review Rather Than Remove It](concepts/self-verifying-agent-loops-hide-review-rather-than-remove-it.md), [Keep critical code inside human understanding and review capacity](concepts/keep-critical-code-inside-human-understanding-and-review-capacity.md), [AI output speed can overwhelm review capacity](concepts/ai-output-speed-can-overwhelm-review-capacity.md), [Separate generation and verification prompts or models](concepts/separate-generation-and-verification-prompts-or-models.md)
- Notes: Alex Volkov (host, ThursdAI; AI evangelist at Weights & Biases / CoreWeave) turns "how much better must models get before you stop reading the code?" into an operating framework built from two opposite AI Engineer Europe talks: OpenAI's Ryan Lopopolo ("code is free… humans no longer need to concern themselves with implementation… the important thing is the prompt and the guardrails") vs. Mario Zechner ("slow down, read every effing line; agents compound boo-boos with zero learning and delayed pain"). He names the axis the Z/L Continuum, then his mea culpa: the continuum is real but about *tasks, not people* — the same engineer is Lopopolo on one change and Zechner on another, so the useful question is "what proof does *this specific change* need?" The extremes converge: Lopopolo's mechanism is "inspect the system, not every line" (humans are unreliable at catching repeated same-type mistakes, so encode each caught bug once as a doc/linter/reviewer); Zechner's is "route by task." Two new concepts. (1) route-each-change-to-the-proof-it-needs — the Z/L Continuum as a per-task routing decision plus his "Monday artifact" routing table: read every line yourself of authentication, money movement, permissions, and irreversible data; decompose long PRs into atomic reviewable ones (agents are good at this); verify with traces/evals/shadow mode; separate the writer from the reviewer/tester ("exam I wrote, took, and scored myself"); engineer rails/observability/rollback because reading spends attention once; and ask the agent which lines/primitives are critical. Capability drift moves *where* proof belongs (outputs → task direction → loops) without removing it (Fable-5 "check if Claude is doing the right work, not just the work right"; Karpathy "never so tempting to stop looking at code… don't in production"). (2) self-verifying-agent-loops-hide-review-rather-than-remove-it — loops (scheduled "fancy cron jobs" that discover a task, plan, execute, and grade themselves against the goal, retrying on failure) are the emerging 2026 primitive (Peter Steinberger + Boris Cherny), but "if the builder grades itself, you didn't remove the review, you hid it"; Addy Osmani warns relying entirely on loops to fix code (a Jira bug the loop picks up) yields "a downward spiral, digging a deeper hole"; loops "raise the stakes on where you put judgment"; lab engineers have free tokens, so treat them as a lighthouse ("skate where the puck is going"), not a mandate. Strengthened three concepts: keep-critical-code-inside-human-understanding-and-review-capacity (framed as the Z end of the continuum: route-by-task + ask-the-agent-what's-critical), ai-output-speed-can-overwhelm-review-capacity (Faros AI "acceleration whiplash" survey of 22,000 engineers — 861% more code deletion/PR, 31% more PRs merged with no review, 242% more incidents/PR, bugs/dev up 6x; "output does not mean stability"; Amdahl's-law "human review is the new bottleneck" from Anthropic's RSI essay; neither Anthropic nor OpenAI removes the human, so move review up a layer), and separate-generation-and-verification-prompts-or-models (self-grading-exam analogy + the loops corollary). Distinctness: route-each-change is distinct from the existing review-capacity/understanding concepts (which are about throughput and pedagogy, not the per-task routing decision), and self-verifying-loops is distinct from separate-generation-and-verification (which it links) by being specifically about the autonomous-loop primitive. Bounded blast radius to three topics: Coding Agents (synthesis + both new Key Concepts + source), Workflows (synthesis + both + source), Evaluation (synthesis + self-verifying-loops Key Concept + source). Concept-index: both new under Coding Agents and Workflows; self-verifying-loops also under Evaluation. Two index.md Current Emphasis leads. Selected one new processed source `ZpK5PWX2YRM`, the newest unprocessed by upload_date (2026-07-10, tied with the already-processed WkBPX-oDMnA by date and following it by video_id ascending), 283 of 476 unique sources. Corpus data cited: METR 16h+ tasks broke the trendline (wtfhappened2025.com, Swyx); GitHub ~14B commits/yr (14x 2025's ~1B), mostly AI-assisted; Anthropic 80%+ AI-written code, 8x more code/quarter than 2025; status page "Christmas tree." ASR: Z/L = Zechner/Lopopolo (talk titled "Desolation of Apollo Continuum" in captions); "Pi" = Zechner's tool; "clanker" = agent slang; "Adi Olsmanyu" = Addy Osmani (Google); "Open Claw" = Open Claude (Steinberger); original essay thursdai.news/zl.

## [2026-07-11] ingest | The Golden Age of AI Engineering — Alexander Embiricos & Romain Huet & Peter Steinberger, OpenAI

- Source: [The Golden Age of AI Engineering — Alexander Embiricos & Romain Huet & Peter Steinberger, OpenAI](sources/20260709_pMggiOb18tc.md)
- Processed: `pMggiOb18tc`
- Updated topics: [Coding Agents](topics/coding-agents.md), [Product Strategy](topics/product-strategy.md), [Workflows](topics/workflows.md), [Agents](topics/agents.md)
- Updated concepts: [Dogfood the Same Stack You Ship to Developers](concepts/dogfood-the-same-stack-you-ship-to-developers.md), [Manage an Agent Manager Instead of Polling Parallel Agents](concepts/manage-an-agent-manager-instead-of-polling-parallel-agents.md), [Shape Agent Products as Chat-First With a Hands-On Surface](concepts/shape-agent-products-as-chat-first-with-a-hands-on-surface.md), [Treat Token Spend as a Strategic Axis](concepts/treat-token-spend-as-a-strategic-axis.md), [Treat Human Attention as the Bottleneck for Agentic Work](concepts/treat-human-attention-as-the-agentic-bottleneck.md)
- Notes: OpenAI Codex keynote. Embiricos & Huet frame engineering as returning to its roots as model cadence accelerates (~15 months → ~6 weeks) and the build-and-test loop moves inside the model (Dev Day 2024 o1-preview could not run/verify its own code; a year later it tests its own work live). Three new concepts. (1) dogfood-the-same-stack-you-ship-to-developers — single-stack philosophy stated as the section's one takeaway ("we're not building one system for OpenAI and a second simplified for developers; at every layer we use the thing we give to you"): same responses API (compaction baked in *first*), open-source harness (models default but not hard-coded; used in post-training), `AGENTS.md`, open-source app server ("the path we use for our own products" — Toma built Codex Monitor/iOS on it), plugins on the same extension points; external forks/edge-finding become a learning signal. (2) manage-an-agent-manager-instead-of-polling-parallel-agents — Steinberger reframes juggling ~10 terminals as *polling* ("I was the scheduler, the router, and the memory"); the durable pattern is "I manage the manager of a small company of agents," human on the outer loop (direction + decisions), agent on the inner execution loop, with three enablers = the loop (server-side compaction, coordination/delegation, triggers); canonical instance is automated open-source issue resolution (manager wakes on an issue, reads it against goals/notes/vision, spins a worker, another agent reviews, returns a PR + issue + diff + maybe a video/running build to VNC into); bottleneck keeps moving tokens → compute (test boxes) → attention ("can't add more… deciding where to spend it"); "the future is not 20 terminals, it's better loops." (3) shape-agent-products-as-chat-first-with-a-hands-on-surface — because the model beats the user on the average task and the goal is to empower not automate, use two modalities (a single ask-anywhere chat entity — "chat is underrated" — plus a collaborative hands-on UI); a CLI is "mostly chat" and an IDE has "the order wrong," so chat-first-then-dig-in preserves the human's "feeling of mastery." Strengthened two concepts: treat-token-spend-as-a-strategic-axis ("value maxing, not token maxing" — $1/M input + $6/M output "insane value"; 750 tokens/sec on Cerebras ≈ PR in ~10s, enough to run 5–6 parallel approaches and pick the best) and treat-human-attention-as-the-agentic-bottleneck (the tokens→compute→attention progression + "stop staring at the agent" now that models understand intent). Distinctness: dogfood extends use-stable-agent-harnesses-as-model-evolution-boundaries (same OpenAI team) by adding the *ship-what-you-run* single-stack claim; manage-an-agent-manager is distinct from agent-managers-orchestrate-editor-browser-and-background-agents (a UI-surface concept) and from the WorkOS attention concept it strengthens (this adds the delegation *org shape*, that owns the attention-bottleneck thesis); chat-first is distinct from build-product-primitives-before-feature-surfaces (which it complements). Bounded blast radius to four topics: Coding Agents (synthesis + all three new Key Concepts + source), Product Strategy (synthesis + dogfood & chat-first + source), Workflows (synthesis + manager + source), Agents (synthesis + manager + source). Concept-index placements: dogfood/manager/chat-first under Coding Agents; dogfood & chat-first under Product Strategy; manager under Workflows and Agents. Three index.md Current Emphasis leads. ## [2026-07-11] ingest | I Run a Fleet of AI Agents Across Three Machines. Here's What Broke.

- Source: [I Run a Fleet of AI Agents Across Three Machines. Here's What Broke. - Kyle Jaejun Lee, KRAFTON](sources/20260708_4kYl2_mqmnQ.md)
- Processed: `4kYl2_mqmnQ`
- Updated topics: [Coding Agents](topics/coding-agents.md), [Agents](topics/agents.md), [Infrastructure](topics/infrastructure.md), [Context Engineering](topics/context-engineering.md), [Workflows](topics/workflows.md)
- Updated concepts: [Externalize Agent State to Files and Reset Instead of Compact](concepts/externalize-agent-state-to-files-and-reset-instead-of-compact.md), [Converge Agent Fleets on Cluster-Scheduling Primitives](concepts/converge-agent-fleets-on-cluster-scheduling-primitives.md), [Manage an Agent Manager Instead of Polling Parallel Agents](concepts/manage-an-agent-manager-instead-of-polling-parallel-agents.md), [Route High-Impact Agent Actions Through Explicit Human Approval Gates](concepts/route-high-impact-agent-actions-through-explicit-human-approval-gates.md)
- Notes: Kyle Jaejun Lee (KRAFTON) field report from a personal 3-machine agent fleet (sleeping MacBook + two always-on headless Linux boxes, one control plane). The first thing that broke was him — a wall of tmux panes made him "the scheduler, the memory, and the reviewer" for six live contexts (an independent reproduction of Steinberger's polling-not-orchestration failure). Two new concepts. (1) externalize-agent-state-to-files-and-reset-instead-of-compact — "the single most practical thing I learned all year": keep each entity's state in files (per-workspace mission/status/handoff, plus `shared/` and `machines/` dirs) and, when the window fills, *reset* (clear the context completely and let the agent re-read its own handoff/history files) rather than *compact* (slow, lossy, can't choose what survives), so work survives a context wipe *or* a machine crash and one `overlord boot` command rebuilds the fleet from files. (2) converge-agent-fleets-on-cluster-scheduling-primitives — the five single-machine failures (orchestrator doing work instead of dispatching → a CLI harness where dispatch is the only path; worker panes too crowded for `tmux capture-pane`; OOM from stacked Claude/MCP processes; credential collisions → per-workspace isolated environments; a laptop dying mid-job losing in-flight work) plus cross-machine coordination (git-based context transfer with `tmux send-keys` over SSH to trigger a pull; per-machine vs. shared-via-PR directories; one collapsed always-on gateway; a Discord phone router) all reduce to "an agent should declare what it needs, not where it runs" — orchestration/review/hierarchy on top, compute/secrets/tools underneath, a scheduler placing work — "the exact questions Kubernetes already answers," so the build stance is to stack a cluster scheduler underneath and build only the agent-specific layer on top. Strengthened two concepts: manage-an-agent-manager-instead-of-polling-parallel-agents (independent field report + the scoped-context CEO/VP/manager/worker org hierarchy as *real entity types*, each with its own context and approval boundary, so the human holds one context instead of six) and route-high-impact-agent-actions-through-explicit-human-approval-gates (the blocking review gateway — any layer submits a plan and waits, an approval hook fires the work, many panes collapse into one web inbox on an always-on box because "your one point of control can't be a thing that falls asleep," and the gateway was itself built by an infra team of agents inside the fleet). Distinctness: reset-vs-compact qualifies frequent-intentional-compaction (curated summary) and offload-long-horizon-agent-state (which one) by adding the file-backed *reset-after-full-clear* + crash-survival stance; converge-on-cluster-scheduling extends treat-multi-agent-systems-as-distributed-systems with the specific claim that the substrate is a *solved* infra problem (a cluster scheduler), not one to re-derive per fleet; the org hierarchy and the review gateway strengthen existing concepts rather than spawning duplicates of manage-an-agent-manager / approval-gates. Bounded blast radius: Coding Agents (synthesis + both new Key Concepts + source), Agents (synthesis + both + source), Infrastructure (synthesis + converge concept + source), Context Engineering (synthesis + reset concept + source), Workflows (synthesis, both target concepts already listed, + source). Concept-index placements: both new under Coding Agents; converge under Infrastructure; reset under Context Engineering; both under Agents. ## [2026-07-11] ingest | Running a Chess YouTube Channel entirely by AI

- Source: [Running a Chess YouTube Channel entirely by AI — Stephan Steinfurt, TNG](sources/20260708_BqZrTdgBaPw.md)
- Processed: `BqZrTdgBaPw`
- Updated topics: [Tools](topics/tools.md), [Generative Media](topics/generative-media.md), [Workflows](topics/workflows.md)
- Updated concepts: [Pair an LLM Narrator With a Domain Solver Via Tools](concepts/pair-an-llm-narrator-with-a-domain-solver-via-tools.md), [Feed Agents Diverse and Conflicting Tool Signals to Broaden Exploration](concepts/feed-agents-diverse-and-conflicting-tool-signals-to-broaden-exploration.md), [Automate a Nightly Generate-and-Publish Media Pipeline With Sampled QA](concepts/automate-a-nightly-generate-and-publish-media-pipeline-with-sampled-qa.md)
- Notes: Stephan Steinfurt (TNG, Munich) runs daily AI-generated chess-puzzle explainer videos on an unattended pipeline. Framing: chess engines are superhuman but "can't explain chess well," LLMs can describe but "can't play chess well," so the agent bridges them. Three new concepts. (1) pair-an-llm-narrator-with-a-domain-solver-via-tools — expose the solver's ground truth through tools (a legal-moves tool constrains the action space; a playable board; an on-demand engine; web search) so a weak-at-the-domain LLM (Gemini 3.1 Pro, best-on-chess; Grok 4 the prior surprise) explains rather than computes, with the analysis migrated from Python pre-scripts into the reasoning agent and a skipped final tool call as the ~1-in-20 confidently-wrong failure. (2) feed-agents-diverse-and-conflicting-tool-signals-to-broaden-exploration — "conflicting information from the tools is beneficial": engine best move + a beginner heuristic (checks/captures/threats, surfacing reasonable-but-losing moves) + a rating-conditioned human-move model (Maia, U Toronto) give diversity to explore and explain wrong moves, "not always about the very very best move," and double as an audience-strength knob. (3) automate-a-nightly-generate-and-publish-media-pipeline-with-sampled-qa — lichess nightly → agent analysis → structured intermediate format (decouples reasoning from rendering) → ElevenLabs V3 narration with `[excited]` audio tags, agent-chosen highlights/arrows/brilliant flags → auto-upload; ~20-30 cents/video, QA by publish-then-take-down sampling not gating; anti-slop (no exploding-kings) and personalization of ordinary players' games a streamer won't cover. Distinctness: pair-narrator generalizes keep-fixed-business-logic-outside-the-model / put-brittle-edge-cases-behind-rigorous-tools to LLM-narrates-over-an-oracle; diverse-signals is the inverse of collapse-to-a-deterministic-path (keep disagreement visible when the deliverable is explanation) and a sibling of run-a-jury-of-analysts; sampled-QA is the generate-and-publish analogue of send-mechanical-defects-back-but-gate-irreversible-steps. Bounded blast radius: Tools (synthesis + 2 Key Concepts + source), Generative Media (synthesis + 2 Key Concepts + source), Workflows (synthesis + 1 Key Concept + source). Concept-index: pair-narrator + diverse-signals under Tools; sampled-QA + pair-narrator under Generative Media; sampled-QA under Workflows. ## [2026-07-11] ingest | Teaching Coding Agents to do Spreadsheets

- Source: [Teaching Coding Agents to do Spreadsheets - Nuno Campos, Witan Labs](sources/20260708_HEFSExa0xl0.md)
- Processed: `HEFSExa0xl0`
- Updated topics: [Tools](topics/tools.md), [Coding Agents](topics/coding-agents.md), [Evaluation](topics/evaluation.md)
- Updated concepts: [Give Agents a Persistent-State REPL Instead of Many Tools](concepts/give-agents-a-persistent-state-repl-instead-of-many-tools.md), [Build High-Fidelity Engines to Create Verification Loops in Non-Code Domains](concepts/build-high-fidelity-engines-to-create-verification-loops-in-non-code-domains.md), [Prefer Model-Portable Agentic Prompts Before Fine-Tuning](concepts/prefer-model-portable-agentic-prompts-before-fine-tuning.md), [Use Golden Data Sets and Mixed Scoring Functions for AI Application Confidence](concepts/use-golden-data-sets-and-mixed-scoring-functions-for-ai-application-confidence.md), [Trace Agent Tool Arguments to Debug Real Failures](concepts/trace-agent-tool-arguments-to-debug-real-failures.md)
- Notes: Nuno Campos (Witan Labs) reports 4 months getting coding agents "as good at spreadsheets as they are at Python or JavaScript," moving an internal financial-analysis benchmark from ~50% to 92%. Two new concepts. (1) give-agents-a-persistent-state-repl-instead-of-many-tools — the biggest single jump (50% → 74%) came from replacing ~15 accumulated tools with one Node.js REPL: code mode *with persistent state*, so former tools become JS functions the agent composes in a single call, variables survive across calls ("builds on its work"), scripts get *shorter* and reasoning interleaves more, timeouts drop to ~zero under a 5-min budget; new capabilities added as JS methods surfaced via a TypeScript type-definitions file in the prompt, not schema entries; interface language (sandbox-friendly JS) split from implementation language (C#); framing "many sequential/parallel tool calls means you invented a bad scripting language — give it a real one," caveat that the REPL is best *today* only (computer use may win). (2) build-high-fidelity-engines-to-create-verification-loops-in-non-code-domains — coding agents thrive on compile/lint/test iteration, so for a domain without such tooling Witan *built* a formula engine + image render engine as the "source of truth… verification loop" (render-to-image also lets the agent *see* structure an LLM can't), with the sharp caveat that a partial engine (50% of Excel's formulas) yields *worse* results than none, and the durability claim that the verification loop, not the transient REPL interface, is what lasts ("the more capable the model, the more it gets out of that verification loop"). Strengthened three concepts: prefer-model-portable-agentic-prompts-before-fine-tuning (domain knowledge in the prompt survived every tool iteration, portable across architectures, works by *reminding not teaching* — pigeonhole attention rather than bake focus into weights); use-golden-data-sets-and-mixed-scoring-functions-for-ai-application-confidence (judge-only can't separate agent regressions from judge drift — "can't tell if the agent or the evaluator changed" — so use a golden-spreadsheet black-box I/O comparison where deterministic checks are possible); trace-agent-tool-arguments-to-debug-real-failures ("agent confusion is just bugs" — infra bugs, wrong prompt examples the model follows faithfully, broken tools it retries around all masquerade as reasoning failures; "check your traces and your plumbing"). Distinctness: the REPL concept extends expose-large-apis-through-typed-code-mode / use-bash-as-a-composable-code-mode-tool with the *persistent-state* semantics (shorter scripts + interleaved reasoning, type-defs-in-prompt for capability growth) rather than duplicating token-efficiency framing; the engine concept extends give-coding-agents-the-same-engineering-infrastructure / make-agent-work-more-trustworthy-by-making-it-verifiable / close-agent-loops-around-live-action-feedback with the specific claim that when no feedback loop exists you *build the calculation/render engine yourself* and it must be high-fidelity or it backfires; domain-knowledge, deterministic-eval, and confusion-is-bugs were folded into strengthens, not new pages. Bounded blast radius: Tools (synthesis + both new Key Concepts + source), Coding Agents (synthesis + both new Key Concepts + source), Evaluation (synthesis + engine Key Concept + source; both strengthened concepts already listed). Concept-index: both new under Tools and Coding Agents; engine concept also under Evaluation. index.md Current Emphasis: 2 new leads prepended. ## [2026-07-11] ingest | Building an ACP-Compatible Agent Live

- Source: [Building an ACP-Compatible Agent Live — Bennet Fenner, Zed](sources/20260708_HsxQICTLF84.md)
- Processed: `HsxQICTLF84`
- Updated topics: [Tools](topics/tools.md), [Coding Agents](topics/coding-agents.md)
- Updated concepts: [Standardize the Editor–Agent Boundary With a Client-Agent Protocol](concepts/standardize-the-editor-agent-boundary-with-a-client-agent-protocol.md), [Stream Agent Progress to Clients as Session-Update Notifications](concepts/stream-agent-progress-to-clients-as-session-update-notifications.md)
- Notes: Bennet Fenner (Zed) live-codes a minimal coding agent into an ACP-compatible one in ~15 min to demonstrate the Agent Client Protocol — a JSON-RPC LSP/MCP analog for the *editor↔agent* boundary (MCP covers agent↔tools, A2A covers agent↔agent) that lets any client drive any agent through one unified interface so users "bring their agent of choice." Two new concepts. (1) standardize-the-editor-agent-boundary-with-a-client-agent-protocol — a minimal agent implements four methods (initialize with protocol-version+capabilities, optional authenticate, newSession keyed by a server-generated session ID bound to the client cwd, prompt, plus cancel), capability negotiation in `initialize` is the extensibility hinge (client advertises file-system + terminal capabilities), integration is config not code (`node agent.js`), stdio transport today with remote (JetBrains) in progress, ~40 clients via native mode or adapter (JetBrains, Obsidian, opencode, cursor). (2) stream-agent-progress-to-clients-as-session-update-notifications — agent work reaches the UI as fire-and-forget notifications, not request/response: text as `agent_message_chunk`, tool calls two-phase (`tool_call` announce → `tool_call_update` complete), edits as a diff content type the editor renders, side effects routed through client-advertised capabilities (proxied file system so the agent sees unsaved editor-buffer changes; client-managed terminal the demo bootstrapped the agent into adding for itself). ## [2026-07-11] ingest | Your LLM Deception Monitor Is Broken. The Fix Is in the Training Data

- Source: [Your LLM Deception Monitor Is Broken. The Fix Is in the Training Data - Sachin Kumar, LexisNexis](sources/20260708_IQkVMvXQKLY.md)
- Processed: `IQkVMvXQKLY`
- Updated topics: [Security](topics/security.md), [Evaluation](topics/evaluation.md), [Models](topics/models.md)
- Updated concepts: [Behavioral Evals Cannot Catch Sleeper-Agent Backdoors](concepts/behavioral-evals-cannot-catch-sleeper-agent-backdoors.md), [Detect Fine-Tuning Backdoors With an Activation-Difference SAE](concepts/detect-fine-tuning-backdoors-with-an-activation-difference-sae.md), [Model Diffs Inspect Post-Training Feature Changes](concepts/model-diffs-inspect-post-training-feature-changes.md)
- Notes: Sachin Kumar (LexisNexis; peer-reviewed IJCNN paper, open source) on detecting fine-tuning backdoors — "sleeper agents" that pass every eval and behavioral monitor but flip a model to harmful output on a benign trigger. Two new concepts. (1) behavioral-evals-cannot-catch-sleeper-agent-backdoors — the threat model: four properties make it uncatchable behaviorally (trigger benign/unblacklistable, invisible at eval time because correct almost everywhere, survives RLHF with CoT hiding intent per Hubinger et al., worse with scale), behavioral testing is a logical trap ("if you know the trigger, you wouldn't need the monitor"), the four-door attack surface (poison data, fine-tuning vendors, downloaded fine-tunes, insiders) means "if you don't control every training token yourself, you are exposed," and the go-to interpretability fix (crosscoders / joint cross-model features) also fails at ~random. (2) detect-fine-tuning-backdoors-with-an-activation-difference-sae — the fix: a backdoor is a directional shift the fine-tuning wrote into activations, so subtract base from fine-tuned (delta A) and train a sparse autoencoder on the *difference* (diff-SAE) so it isolates as a single interpretable feature; proven on a controlled SQL-injection backdoor (prompt "current year 2024" → vulnerable SQL) on SmolLM2-360M with procedurally generated data, scoring 0.4 vs ~0.01 for crosscoders (40×, non-overlapping CIs) at precision 1.0 and zero false positives; layer- and regime-independent (LoRA/full-rank), low-dimensional (4× SAE matches 32×), wired as a cheap "delta monitor" CI gate needing no advance trigger knowledge; limits — needs the base checkpoint, ~25% single-feature recall (ensemble), 360M/one-backdoor-type tested, adaptive attacker untested. Strengthened model-diffs-inspect-post-training-feature-changes as a concrete, quantified realization of the model-diff research direction as a shippable safety monitor (added a detail line, Security topic link, and this source). Distinctness: the threat concept and the method concept split cleanly (why behavior/crosscoders fail vs how the diff-SAE works), and both are new axes for the wiki — the existing interpretability corpus (mechanistic-interpretability, activation-steering, model-diffs) had no fine-tuning-backdoor detection and security had no model-supply-chain/activation-level monitor. Bounded blast radius: Security (new overview paragraph + both Key Concepts + source), Evaluation (overview paragraph + both Key Concepts + source), Models (interpretability paragraph augment + detection Key Concept + source). Concept-index: both new under Security and Evaluation; detection concept also under Models. index.md Current Emphasis: 2 new leads prepended. ## [2026-07-11] ingest | Your coding agent doesn't always follow your rules

- Source: [Your coding agent doesn't always follow your rules — Talha Sheikh, Checkout.com](sources/20260708_MpZzWMdmQCE.md)
- Processed: `MpZzWMdmQCE`
- Updated topics: [Coding Agents](topics/coding-agents.md), [Workflows](topics/workflows.md), [Tools](topics/tools.md)
- Updated concepts: [Wrap Agent Completion in an Automatic Deterministic Verification Gate](concepts/wrap-agent-completion-in-an-automatic-deterministic-verification-gate.md), [Verification Guardrails Let You Downshift to Cheaper Models](concepts/verification-guardrails-let-you-downshift-to-cheaper-models.md), [Harness Engineering Shifts Scarcity From Code Production to Control Surfaces](concepts/harness-engineering-shifts-scarcity-from-code-production-to-control-surfaces.md)
- Notes: Talha Sheikh (Checkout.com) frames the everyday coding-agent failure — it reports "task completed," you run it, "something else failed," and you keep issuing "fix this, fix that" — as structural: by default *you* are the enforcement layer because nothing else checks the agent's "done." His fix is an agent harness (Vector V1 / "Vector Harness," open source) that wires a deterministic verification gate to Claude's Stop hook: on completion it runs a config file of test cases and loops "try again" back into the agent until they pass. Two new concepts. (1) wrap-agent-completion-in-an-automatic-deterministic-verification-gate — the human-as-enforcement-layer problem + the Stop-hook check-and-retry fix, justified by two distinctions that keep the gate relevant as models improve (capability ≠ reliability; instructions ≠ verification, since the best spec/MCP/sub-agents/context are still not a *check*), so a smarter future model does not remove the need to verify; enforcement is a *pattern* everyone reinvents (Anthropic, Meta, his own company) so it should be language-agnostic, shareable, bring-your-own-enforcement, running at every level (in-conversation, conversation-end, pre-commit, multi-agent, async, plus optional LLM-as-judge), with industry convergence on the same feedback loop (Anthropic executor/advisor, OpenAI harness engineering, CodeRabbit-style PR review, WorkOS "enforce, don't instruct"); closing shift = value moved from "the code that we create" to "the verification that we design." (2) verification-guardrails-let-you-downshift-to-cheaper-models — a tight check-and-retry harness catches and retries a weaker model's misses, so a smaller/cheaper model (Haiku, open source) reaches the target output; frontier Opus alone is most expensive, but investing more time in guardrails "reduces the cost drastically," trading harness-engineering time for per-token model cost (async tasks help too). Strengthened harness-engineering-shifts-scarcity-from-code-production-to-control-surfaces with Talha's independent field report ("work on the harness, not the code"). Distinctness: the completion gate is carved from the existing hooks concept (use-hooks-for-deterministic-agent-verification) and the git-hooks/CI concept (enforce-agent-rules-in-git-hooks-and-ci) by its specific "the agent's *done* is untrustworthy → Stop-hook check-and-retry gate + enforce-not-instruct + capability≠reliability" framing; the cheaper-models concept is the distinct economic corollary. Bounded blast radius: Coding Agents (synthesis paragraph + both Key Concepts + source), Workflows (synthesis paragraph + gate Key Concept + source), Tools (synthesis paragraph tying the gate to the hooks-as-tool-surface theme + gate Key Concept + source). Concept-index: gate under Coding Agents/Tools/Workflows, cheaper-models under Coding Agents/Inference. index.md Current Emphasis: 2 new leads prepended. - Notes: Danielle An & David Hoe (Meta, AI game-creation) share a year of learnings. Everyone can vibe-code a platformer/Tetris in a couple of prompts, so novelty wears off and identical prompts look alike — the question is "what's next?" Two new concepts. (1) drive-game-npcs-and-difficulty-with-runtime-llms — the newest primitive is a *runtime LLM* running during play (not build time): each NPC is entirely LLM-driven with a personality (thief/honorable/fast) and makes emergent unscripted decisions (steal/block/kick) so every playthrough is unique/non-repeatable; a "game master" personalizes difficulty per player (carries a coordination-challenged player through co-op); genre newly viable only because inference is fast/cheap enough (built over days); caveats — technology alone doesn't make the product better and nondeterminism + content-safety run through the whole stack. (2) anchor-generative-asset-cohesion-on-one-key-art-image — iterate to a single key-art image you like, then feed it back as the anchor so the model holds one art style (and the implied gameplay) across a session; assets feel like one universe rather than mismatched per-prompt outputs, shortening the distance between a kid's prompt output and a professionally made game. Strengthened ai-amplified-shipping-speed-needs-stronger-product-taste (removing the skill-gate via "Meta Banana"/Mesh and collapsing the linear waterfall into parallel hours/days iteration doesn't make a good game; taste = the feel that *this* is a game some subset of humans would enjoy and why) and treat-model-and-prompt-upgrades-as-regulated-migrations (nondeterminism through the whole agentic game stack means model/prompt upgrades can throw off a system engineers used to debug against fixed code). Distinctness: runtime-LLM-NPC is agentic decision-making in games (distinct from interactive-world-models, which is generated visual environments à la Genie); key-art-anchor is a new generative-media cohesion concept (no prior anchor/consistency concept existed). Updated Generative Media (synthesis paragraph + both Key Concepts + source) and Agents (synthesis paragraph + runtime-LLM Key Concept + source); concept-index placed both under Generative Media and the runtime-LLM concept also under Agents; index.md Current Emphasis got 2 new leads.
