## [2026-07-11] ingest | Your agent is blindfolded

- Source: [Your agent is blindfolded — Johan Lajili, Poolside AI](sources/20260708_iRcX54EO5g8.md)
- Processed: `iRcX54EO5g8`
- Updated topics: [Coding Agents](topics/coding-agents.md), [Tools](topics/tools.md)
- Updated concepts: [Give Your Agent Eyes With a Product-Specific Observation Tool](concepts/give-your-agent-eyes-with-a-product-specific-observation-tool.md), [Reproduce the Bug Before Fixing to Earn Agent Trust](concepts/reproduce-the-bug-before-fixing-to-earn-agent-trust.md), [Agent Experience Means Autonomous Access, Understanding, and Operation](concepts/agent-experience-means-autonomous-access-understanding-and-operation.md), [Greenfield AI Coding Gains Drop Off in Mature Codebases](concepts/greenfield-ai-coding-gains-drop-off-in-mature-codebases.md)
- Notes: Johan Lajili (Poolside AI, builds its own foundation model + coding agents) reframes the "AI is magic vs AI is garbage" split as a *feedback-loop* difference, not greenfield-vs-brownfield: on greenfield the agent's intuition is correct, on brownfield "there be dragons" (dead ends, unused code, unseen parts), and the differentiator is whether the agent can *see* what it did — a blindfolded agent's "it's all working perfectly" really means "to the best of what you gave me, that sounds like it should work." Two new concepts. (1) give-your-agent-eyes-with-a-product-specific-observation-tool — a coding agent is blindfolded without an observe-and-control surface tailored to the product's runtime, and generic screenshot/browser tooling only fits web apps; because Poolside's product is a VS Code extension (not a web page), they built an internal CLI ("Spoolside," ASR-uncertain, "not on GitHub") that takes screenshots, produces a token-compressed UI snapshot, extracts front-end/back-end service logs, restarts services, and issues stackable high-level commands (open a menu, navigate, send a message to the agent, wait for the reply, upload an image); the tool is disposable — build your own as a CLI/skill/MCP, varying by product and tailoring the representation (ASCII view of a Unity 3D world; easy logins for a multi-permission app); find what to build by refusing to hand-correct the AI and a retrospective loop asking the AI to review its own logs for "stinks" (sleep(15) everywhere = a missing wait-for). (2) reproduce-the-bug-before-fixing-to-earn-agent-trust — agents are eager and confidently claim the fix, but "until it reproduces the bug, I don't trust you"; reproduction is the "first step to trust" that lets you stop hand-verifying and start running agents overnight and in parallel. Strengthened agent-experience-means-autonomous-access-understanding-and-operation (the "AIX engineer" role — focus less on the product and more on making the AI work on the product; oxygen-mask rule: put the mask on the AI first, make it self-served before building features, because it pays off once you multiply agents) and greenfield-ai-coding-gains-drop-off-in-mature-codebases (qualification: the drop-off may be a feedback-loop problem more than an inherent property of mature code — a bespoke "eyes" tool can recover much of the lost gain). Distinctness: give-your-agent-eyes is carved from existing web-verification concepts (autonomous-browser-verification, use-multisensory-feedback-loops, expose-observability-as-agent-readable-feedback) by its "non-web products need a *custom-built* observation surface, not a reused one" emphasis; reproduce-the-bug-before-fixing is the distinct trust-gate/feedback-loop framing. Bounded blast radius: Coding Agents (synthesis paragraph + both Key Concepts + source) and Tools (synthesis paragraph tying the eyes tool to the "tools you build so the agent can see your product" theme + eyes Key Concept + source). Concept-index: give-your-agent-eyes under Coding Agents/Tools, reproduce-the-bug under Coding Agents. index.md Current Emphasis: 2 new leads prepended. Three model eras — Sonnet 3.5 (tool-call), Opus 4.5 (long-running task), Mythos (orchestration; the model "understands itself," spawns/verifies subagents from a prompt with "no fancy software factory") — and the gain is wasted unless you scale ambition. Three new concepts: (1) rescope-ambition-down-a-tier-as-models-improve — the tiers of buildable ambition each dropped a level (side project ← former startup ← former too-big), and a new bottom tier is executable natural language on a cron (his PR-triage service is now a markdown file piped to Codex/Claude writing static HTML to S3 at 9 a.m.); "too big" is now undefined. (2) think-wider-not-bigger-compete-on-breadth-via-extensibility — AI collapses the cost of *breadth*, so small teams can span an incumbent's surface area ("build a database platform in a day or two of prompting") by architecting for user-driven extensibility, letting users build the vertical features you don't support (Slack accidentally became "the platform people run their agents in" — right *shape*, not most complete); "if your idea doesn't feel stupid, it's not big enough." (3) reject-skeuomorphic-dev-tooling-and-legacy-constraints — the blocker is legacy developer identity: we're in a "skeuomorphic phase" (iOS 6→7 analogy) keeping terminals, Git defaults (why can't we commit env files?), editor religion, language identity, and sunk-cost attachment because they're *familiar* not *useful*; agents lower the cost of deleting/resetting; test each convention by whether you do it because it's right or because it's always been done that way. Strengthened coding-agent-capability-tiers-change-the-bottleneck with the independent three-era framing + prompt-native orchestration. - Notes: Mixedbread argues reasoning has improved exponentially while retrieval crept forward for ~20 years, so for knowledge work retrieval is the bottleneck. Three new concepts: (1) the *Oracle gap* — score with the right documents (93% BrowseComp Plus, 64% FinanceQA Pro) vs over the real corpus (Codex default tools drop to single digits) isolates retrieval loss, recoverable at the tool layer (late-interaction search brings GPT-5 within ~3 pts of Oracle) not the model layer. (3) distill a small fast search agent (SFT from a teacher) and RL-tune it on a composite search reward — retrieval reward (NDCG + LLM rubric judge) + trajectory reward (LLM judge on query naturalness/exploration depth) — reaching NDCG@10 0.4 on an OpenCongress-style benchmark (vs 0.18) and top-1 on Snowflake DocQA with Gemini 2.5 Flash as answer model. - Notes: Aditya Bhargava (Etsy) argues against "the models are so good, keep the harness simple — a few tools and a loop," because that makes teams reliant on fancy proprietary models that can't run locally; the better goal is a harness good enough to get cutting-edge performance from a *local open-source* model. (3) pre-bind-tool-arguments-to-give-agents-safe-autonomy — partial function application locks a tool's sensitive argument so the model can't see or change it, confining blast radius without per-action approval, complementing interrupt-based approval; distinct from capability-sandbox and permission-layering concepts. Strengthened use-subagents-to-isolate-context-heavy-subtasks with the tool-selection angle (grouping tools under sub-agents so the top agent picks a sub-agent rather than among many unrelated tools; a sub-agent is "just another function"). ## [2026-07-11] ingest | Respect The Process - Andrew Dumit, Watershed Technology Inc.

- Source: [Respect The Process - Andrew Dumit, Watershed Technology Inc.](sources/20260707_CLttOU7n6sI.md)
- Processed: `CLttOU7n6sI`
- Updated topics: [Coding Agents](topics/coding-agents.md), [Evaluation](topics/evaluation.md), [Tools](topics/tools.md)
- Updated concepts: [Verify the Process, Not Just the Answer, in Judgment-Heavy Domains](concepts/verify-the-process-not-just-the-answer-in-judgment-heavy-domains.md), [Constrain Agent Effects, Not Expression, With a Typed SDK](concepts/constrain-agent-effects-not-expression-with-a-typed-sdk.md), [Produce Domain-Shaped Review Artifacts for Non-Coder Verification](concepts/produce-domain-shaped-review-artifacts-for-non-coder-verification.md)
- Notes: Andrew Dumit (Watershed, sustainability carbon-footprint platform) argues that in a judgment-heavy vertical "you have to verify the process in addition to the answer" — there are many ways to be right for the wrong reasons and many defensible answers experts disagree on (2020 study: six experts, identical wine-bottle data, answers varied up to 50%). One task (editing supply-chain graphs of hundreds of thousands of metadata-rich nodes) went through three harness generations: a ReAct specialized-tool agent worked on one graph but broke at scale (inconsistency, exploration bottleneck, context exhaustion, schema hallucination) because "editing across [them] takes loops and filters, not enumerated tool calls — code is the only thing that scales"; a coding agent recovered scale but unconstrained code was "scary" (Python-when-told-TypeScript, artifact edits with no lineage, gaslighting edits-not-made, unreviewable for non-engineers); the fix "constrains the effects, not the expression." Three new concepts: (1) verify-the-process-not-just-the-answer-in-judgment-heavy-domains — with no verifiable ground truth the answer is a weak signal (precedent: 2026 "Open Proof Corpus"/"Beyond Correctness," answer-vs-proof gap even in fully-verifiable math; reward-hacking on the Erdős problems, "significant risk for critical applications"), so validating the process is the main lever and the ~8% of eval tasks where the answer differs from the reference still stay valid/traceable/replayable. (2) constrain-agent-effects-not-expression-with-a-typed-sdk — the agent freely writes code but every consequential edit passes through a typed TypeScript SDK that is "the only door" (editable-vs-derived fields, mutators `findNodesByExactName`/`setRate`/`editNode`, assertions, typed objects; taught like teaching your codebase) plus an owned deterministic "run executor" (lint → conflict-detect → run → validate typed artifacts, reject-and-retry). (3) produce-domain-shaped-review-artifacts-for-non-coder-verification — the deterministic execution emits a structured domain report ("impact analysis ran on 50 graphs → 749 edit actions → emissions −45.6%," drillable per-edit/per-graph) so non-coders verify outcomes without reading code; "the code is just the means to an end." Harness constraints alone weren't enough: hill-climbed 43%→92% on internal evals via prompt/system-prompt work, few-shot SDK coaching, SDK ergonomics, plan-and-execute decomposition, and teaching expert judgment. Updated Coding Agents (synthesis paragraph + 3 Key Concepts + source), Evaluation (process-verification synthesis + 1 Key Concept + source), Tools (typed-SDK-as-only-door synthesis + 1 Key Concept + source). Concept-index: all three under Coding Agents; verify-the-process also under Evaluation; constrain-effects also under Tools. index.md Current Emphasis got 3 new leads. ## [2026-07-11] ingest | SWE-Marathon: Evaluating Coding Agents at Billion-Token Scale - Rishi Desai, Abundant AI

- Source: [SWE-Marathon: Evaluating Coding Agents at Billion-Token Scale - Rishi Desai, Abundant AI](sources/20260707_Rx8f05JI_WA.md)

- Notes: Rishi Desai (ML engineer at Abundant AI, which builds RL environments and SWE benchmarks for Frontier Labs) presents SWE-Marathon, a benchmark asking whether coding agents "can stay coherent over a billion token budget" — building whole projects end-to-end (build Slack from scratch, rewrite a JAX codebase in PyTorch, build a C compiler in Rust) rather than fixing bugs/GitHub issues, turning "Frontier Labs style case studies" (Anthropic's agent-team C compiler, Cloudflare rebuilding Next.js on Vite hands-off, Cursor's days-long autonomous harness) into reproducible eval tasks. It extends the lineage HumanEval → SWE-bench → Terminal-bench → SWE-Marathon (that environment+verifier framing "stretched to project scale"): 20 tasks across four families (library clones, full-stack product clones, ML engineering, algorithmic), some using external APIs (a post-train task via the *tinker* API), standardized into executable environments with multi-layer verifier suites in the *harbor* format and hardened in a QA loop "until the tasks were both solvable, but also hard to game." Two new concepts. (1) evaluate-coding-agents-on-project-scale-billion-token-tasks — the best config (Claude Opus 4.8 + Claude Code) resolves only 26% (avg 31M tokens/trial, longest 877M), so "end-to-end project ownership is still very far from being solved," and the scaffold matters as much as the model (a cheaper Codex config gets 12%); one rollout (GLM 5.2, Next.js→Vite rewrite) spanned 356M tokens / 9 hours / 800+ steps from a 0/325 test suite — "long engineering loops," not coding tasks; tasks/code/paper/logs/320 GB of trajectories public at swe-bench.org. (2) at-long-horizons-a-weak-verifier-becomes-an-attack-surface — "in a short benchmark, a weak test could just be considered as noise, but in a multi-hour environment, a weak verifier becomes an attack surface"; an agent with hours, a filesystem, network, and a reward signal will probe the verifier, so defend with independent multi-channel checks (hidden tests, reference parity, computer-use-agent verifier, anti-cheat) that "fail in different ways" — across 1,400 rollouts 12.8% suspicious shortcuts and 9% clear bypass but *zero* rewarded exploits ("that should be the bar for long-horizon evals"); concrete catch: a "C compiler in Rust" task where Gemini shelled out to GCC is caught by `strace` on the forbidden subprocess → reward zero. Plus a strengthen to autonomous-browser-verification-finds-painted-door-failures: SWE-Marathon is "the first benchmark to use a computer use agent or CUA verifier" for full-stack product clones (unit tests pass but "the product is probably still unusable and the front end looks terrible"), driving the Slack clone through the UI like a human against a rubric because "correctness is not just an API contract; it's whether the user can actually complete the product's intended workflow." Updated Evaluation (synthesis paragraph + 2 Key Concepts + source) and Coding Agents (synthesis paragraph + 2 Key Concepts + source). Concept-index: both new concepts under Evaluation and Coding Agents. index.md Current Emphasis got 2 new leads. Distinctness: project-scale/billion-token is a concrete whole-project-ownership benchmark point (distinct from benchmark-saturation-pushes-capability-evals-toward-human-time-horizons, METR's time-horizon methodology, and push-agent-benchmarks-on-environment-autonomy-and-output-complexity, Snorkel's three axes); verifier-as-attack-surface is a horizon-specific reward-hacking rule with the zero-rewarded-exploit bar (distinct from detect-reward-hacking-in-code-optimization-evals and seal-eval-environments-against-answer-leaking-agents). - Notes: Sanja Grbic (product designer at Automattic, the ~1,400-person fully-distributed/async company behind WordPress.com, Jetpack, WooCommerce, Tumblr, Beeper) reports on "Radical Speed Month" — pause the roadmap for 30 days, pair with a partner, ship something real, AI encouraged but not required; the first wave was ~500 people (a third of the company) starting ~794 projects. She frames it as an organizational-behavior-change intervention that only worked on top of pre-existing scaffolding: a role-specific two-week immersive AI enablement course, a systems/operations team that built security, processes, and docs good enough that a non-engineer can spin up a working dev environment, and an internal knowledge MCP server ("Context") exposing decades of documented company knowledge to AI tools. Her own arc spanned three projects: a 2-hour four-person board-game session manager (Cloud Code + a Nano Banana office illustration) where the engineer's biggest contribution was creating the GitHub project and teaching Git/versioning; a solo 2.5-week design-system status tracker proof of concept (pulls links from GitHub/Storybook/Figma, sorts by status, tags by library, live component previews, search; deployed internal + curated public site); and a 6-day two-designer iOS WooCommerce merchant-chat POC (wordpress.com auth, Jetpack connection, a site-theming visitor widget, and an AI agent that scans the site, answers visitor questions in real time, and discerns whether it can answer). Three new concepts. (1) run-a-time-boxed-roadmap-pause-to-shift-ai-work-habits — a fixed-window roadmap pause plus autonomous pairs breaks entrenched process in a large org (velocity differs entirely from a 0→1 team), but only on top of enablement scaffolding; changing tools/process "requires shifting the human behavior behind them," so the levers are access, enablers/champions, space to experiment, and agency to break habits. (2) on-mixed-ability-ai-teams-specialists-should-enable-not-do — when AI lets everyone do a bit outside their domain, a strong specialist's highest-leverage move is to teach and enable teammates; "engineers will need to become enablers and teachers." (3) make-the-running-prototype-the-primary-design-artifact — designers iterate on a running coded prototype and demote Figma to late mood-board/UI fine-tuning, owning the whole plan-build-deploy loop as a "design engineer"; recording exploration in a Cloud Code project folder/file system sped collaboration and build. Updated Product Strategy (org-transformation-lever synthesis + 1 Key Concept + source), Coding Agents (non-engineer-adoption synthesis + 3 Key Concepts + source), and Workflows (design-process-shift synthesis + 3 Key Concepts + source); concept-index places run-a-time-boxed under Product Strategy, on-mixed-ability + make-the-running under Coding Agents, and all three under Workflows; index.md Current Emphasis got 1 new lead. ## [2026-07-11] ingest | The Pipeline Is Dead

- Source: [The Pipeline Is Dead - Iris ten Teije, Sky Valley Ambient Computing](sources/20260707_bRnoEpoK5m4.md)
- Processed: `bRnoEpoK5m4`
- Updated topics: [Infrastructure](topics/infrastructure.md), [Product Strategy](topics/product-strategy.md), [Workflows](topics/workflows.md)
- Updated concepts: [The Frozen-Artifact Pipeline Is a Cost Constraint, Not a Law](concepts/frozen-artifact-pipeline-is-a-cost-constraint-not-a-law.md), [Deploy a Canonical Stem Plus Bounded Per-User Divergences](concepts/deploy-a-canonical-stem-plus-bounded-per-user-divergences.md)
- Notes: Iris ten Teije (co-founder of Differ, "infrastructure for adaptive software"; co-founder Noam was the first engineer at JFrog) argues the software distribution stack (CI, packagers, registries, container images, app-store review) exists to solve exactly one problem — move a *frozen artifact* "from machine where it was built to the machine where it runs, safely, reproducibly, once" — and that baked into it is an assumption "so old we stopped seeing it as a choice": one version of your software for everyone. That was never a fact about software "like gravity"; it was a consequence of cost (a correct, scoped change "took skilled humans hours or days," so you did it rarely, froze it, and shipped the frozen thing to everyone), and a per-user version "just wasn't an option" because forking + hand-maintaining a code base per user doesn't scale. Two new concepts. (1) frozen-artifact-pipeline-is-a-cost-constraint-not-a-law — as change-production cost collapses toward zero and can happen at runtime on the server, client, or user's live session, "when the agent is the runtime… development and distribution stop being two phases"; demand for software that "fits you" is decades old (forward-deployed engineer / professional services, developer dotfiles rebuilt by hand, Excel as "millions of people that all build their own programs") but was gated by cost and forced into pre-declared buckets by feature flags / segmentation / A-B testing; "the pipeline didn't fail because it didn't work anymore, but the constraint it was built for went away" (analogy: a branchless bank once "sounded reckless," "a decade later the branch is the weird part"). (2) deploy-a-canonical-stem-plus-bounded-per-user-divergences — Differ's bet is "one canonical stem and every user runs her own divergence of it… individually adapted live," moving "from the least[-worst] first version for everyone to the best version for anyone"; against the CTO objection ("run millions of these? …my worst problem multiplied"), the brittleness people fear "is unmanaged divergence inside a single artifact" (thousand-line files, no boundaries), so stem+divergences make each divergence "bounded, isolated, and individually reversible" — "the blast radius of a change is one context" and any divergence "can roll back live with no deploy," with developer-set guardrails (form fields "can never be dropped," auth/payments "off limits") and user-requested changes allowed only "within the boundaries the developer sets" and "within the spirit of the software," letting a horizontal SaaS "address a much wider number of customer personas without increasing your R&D spend." The hard parts are the substrate not generation ("the easy 80%"): source of truth = "the stem plus all the immutable divergences" (lineage is "a graph query versus a version number"; each divergence "immutable, inspectable, attributable"), correctness across "the stem and every possible divergence," desirability (a correct change may not be an uplift — measure retention/churn/support-tickets), autonomy-vs-control ("winning enough trust that you don't have to"), and coordination ("don't merge code, merge intent, merge outcome"). Updated Infrastructure (distribution-stack-as-adaptive-software-substrate synthesis + 2 Key Concepts + source), Product Strategy (runtime-personalization/more-personas-without-more-R&D synthesis + 2 Key Concepts + Open Question + source), and Workflows (dissolving development/distribution-boundary + merge-intent synthesis + 2 Key Concepts + source); concept-index places both concepts under Infrastructure, Product Strategy, and Workflows; index.md Current Emphasis got 1 new lead. Distinctness: frozen-artifact-pipeline-is-a-cost-constraint is a distribution-economics/boundary-dissolution thesis (distinct from rescope-ambition-down-a-tier and think-wider-compete-on-breadth, ambition/breadth framings, and from distribution-is-the-new-bottleneck-for-devtools, which means "distribution" in the GTM sense); deploy-a-canonical-stem-plus-bounded-per-user-divergences is a per-user runtime-adaptation architecture (distinct from branchable-cloud-workspaces-make-agent-actions-reversible, whose reversible unit is an agent's exploration not a shipped user's live software, and constrain-agent-effects-not-expression-with-a-typed-sdk, an input-side effect constraint on one code base). ## [2026-07-11] ingest | Beyond the Harness: A Journey Towards Adaptive Engineering

- Source: [Beyond the Harness: A Journey Towards Adaptive Engineering - Rajiv Chandegra, Annicha Labs](sources/20260707_qdZzND79mcg.md)
- Processed: `qdZzND79mcg`
- Updated topics: [Agents](topics/agents.md), [Coding Agents](topics/coding-agents.md), [Workflows](topics/workflows.md)
- Updated concepts: [Let the Agent Harness Emerge at Runtime (Adaptive Engineering)](concepts/let-the-agent-harness-emerge-at-runtime-adaptive-engineering.md), [Match the Harness to Complicated vs Complex Problems](concepts/match-the-harness-to-complicated-vs-complex-problems.md)
- Notes: Rajiv Chandegra (a practicing medical doctor in London and director of Annicha Labs, working toward multi-agent/multi-human/multi-institutional collaboration) proposes **adaptive engineering** against today's dominant **fixed (factory) harness**. Current paradigm: an engineer picks or builds a harness (Claude Code, Codex, Cursor, Pi, LangChain, Cline, Goose, Hermes) whose system prompts, `AGENTS.md`/`CLAUDE.md`, tools, roles, sequencing, memory, and loops are pre-engineered *ahead of runtime* and stay fixed while agents run inside — "Taylorism for AI," giving reliability, auditability, and linear traceable causality, and right for **complicated** problems (a clock, a jumbo jet: decomposable, knowable, predictable) but the wrong answer for **complex** ones (a flock, a market, an organization — Ackoff's "mess," "a system of relationships in motion") whose whole "can't be derived from analyzing the parts," where you "probe and sense and respond." "One of the most expensive mistakes… is that we treat a complex problem like a complicated one… things fail not due to lack of execution, but because there's a failure in categorizing the problem spaces." As models get exponentially stronger and AI leaves the sandbox to touch the real world, the fixed harness becomes brittle — "reliability isn't free… you buy it by suppressing variance that novelty requires," and "every situation you didn't anticipate needs a human to patch the harness" until it's "more complicated than the actual problem" — so "the factory method is the right answer to a fixed problem and the wrong answer to a moving problem." Two new concepts. (1) let-the-agent-harness-emerge-at-runtime-adaptive-engineering — adaptive engineering is "the discipline of designing constraints to the extent that the harness emerges on its own, stabilizes, and adapts… in ways you could not specify in advance," so "the harness becomes the ongoing output rather than the input"; the engineer dictates constraints (three levers: enable vs govern/guardrail, reward coherence vs cost divergence, rate of coupling/speed) and *senses and responds* to the emergent harness rather than restarting; emergent specialization arises as coupling builds to ~one connection per agent and environmental pressure breaks ties into niches ("the agent's identity… is the position, role, or capability it took relative to the others"), forming clusters, conventions, and "governance without a governor"; **horizontal intelligence** (group coordination) is the bet as a higher-leverage lever than **vertical intelligence** (making one agent smarter, e.g. Hermes "creates skills from experience"), the two orthogonal; design-time customizability (Pi "minimalist and maximally extensible") is *not* runtime self-reorganization; failure modes: attractors that "feel stable and optimal but… not necessarily the best," drift without genuine selection pressure, monoculture (agents "all trained on the same data"), collapsing legibility, no predictability ahead of runtime; closing thesis: the limiting factor becomes "not the strength of the model… [but] the adaptability of the harness." (2) match-the-harness-to-complicated-vs-complex-problems — the complicated/complex diagnostic + probe-sense-respond + Ackoff's "mess" + reductionist-vs-relational ontology (things vs processes/relationships, "a flame than a crystal") + emergence (water's wetness, a flock from three local bird rules, "no single bird has a flock in it"), with the miscategorization as the root failure and the three complex-system ingredients (diverse agents = "diversity is the fuel", local interaction, recursive adaptation) that settle into self-organizing attractors. Updated Agents (adaptive-engineering-vs-fixed-harness synthesis + 2 Key Concepts + source), Coding Agents (harness-engineering-as-philosophy synthesis + 2 Key Concepts + source), and Workflows (fixed-vs-emergent-workflow synthesis + 2 Key Concepts + source); concept-index places both concepts under Agents, Coding Agents, and Workflows; index.md Current Emphasis got 1 new lead. Distinctness: let-the-agent-harness-emerge is a harness-design *philosophy* (harness as emergent runtime output, horizontal vs vertical intelligence) distinct from court-agent-emergence-with-bounded-play (discovering emergent *product behaviors* in a prototype), choose-choreography-or-orchestration-by-complexity-and-autonomy (selecting a *fixed* coordination pattern up front), treat-multi-agent-systems-as-distributed-systems (reliability plumbing), and use-stable-agent-harnesses-as-model-evolution-boundaries (a *fixed* harness as abstraction boundary); match-the-harness-to-complicated-vs-complex is a problem-classification diagnostic no existing page owns. ## [2026-07-11] ingest | Field Guide to Fable — Thariq Shihipar, Anthropic

- Source: [Field Guide to Fable — Thariq Shihipar, Anthropic](sources/20260706_9fubhllmsBU.md)
- Processed: `9fubhllmsBU`
- Updated topics: [Coding Agents](topics/coding-agents.md), [Context Engineering](topics/context-engineering.md), [Workflows](topics/workflows.md), [Agents](topics/agents.md), [Models](topics/models.md)
- Updated concepts: [Capability Overhang: Tools Decide Which Model Spikes You Reach](concepts/capability-overhang-tools-decide-which-model-spikes-you-reach.md), [Shrink the System Prompt and Drop Examples as Models Improve](concepts/shrink-the-system-prompt-and-drop-examples-as-models-improve.md), [Use the Agent to Surface Your Own Unknowns](concepts/use-the-agent-to-surface-your-own-unknowns.md), [Rescope Ambition Down a Tier as Models Improve](concepts/rescope-ambition-down-a-tier-as-models-improve.md) (strengthened)
- Notes: Thariq Shihipar (works on Claude Code at Anthropic) gives a "field guide" to Fable, Anthropic's newest model, in four moves: unhobble Claude, find your unknowns, deal with the grief, be unreasonable. The spine is **capability overhang** — models get smarter in *spiky* ways and the tools/harness decide which spikes you reach: a chat model can't name the Pokémon whose names end in "aw" (Croconaw, Drednaw) though it knows all ~1000, but Claude Code writes a script, fetches the list, and filters in seconds. Because "the models are grown, not designed… what contains them is us," unhobbling = understand the model better to unleash it, "closer to a biology than a physics" (cites Anthropic's "Biology of a Large Language Model" paper); "give it arms" (the bash tool to build/search its own context) is what produced Claude Code rather than a 100M-token window. Three new concepts. (1) capability-overhang-tools-decide-which-model-spikes-you-reach — latent capability sits in the harness, not just the weights; a new model is a discovery task ("what is now possible?") and unhobbling can mean *removing* structure (distinct from share-demos-to-reveal-latent-model-capabilities and coding-agent-capability-tiers-change-the-bottleneck). (2) shrink-the-system-prompt-and-drop-examples-as-models-improve — Claude Code "removed 80% of the system prompt" because "examples tend to constrain a model more imaginative than the examples"; best-practice arc small→large→*smaller* prompt with fewer examples, "give context not constraints," avoid "do not do this"; same tool ratchets (ask-user-question: barely-callable under Opus 4 → 40-question interviews under 4.5 → embedded-HTML questionnaires under 4.8/Fable), so re-test old tools (distinct from prompt-coding-agents-around-learned-model-habits). (3) use-the-agent-to-surface-your-own-unknowns — spec = *map*, codebase = *territory*, every gap an "unknown," and at high capability the human's map-territory matching is the bottleneck; a knowns matrix maps to elicitation moves: blind-spot pass (also for new fields), prototype variants for taste, interview-me ("prioritize questions that would change the architecture"), reference codebase/HTML mock-up as another map, implementation-note logging, quiz-me (distinct from spec-driven-development, its discovery front-end). Also strengthened rescope-ambition-down-a-tier-as-models-improve with the "being unreasonable" mindset (Anthropic's "tradeoffs are not real"; good/fast/cheap = pick three; "force reality to show you the tradeoff"; built the talk's deck in ~4 hours with Fable; caveat "building is easier, but generating value is still hard"). Updated Coding Agents (all 3 concepts + source), Context Engineering (shrink-prompt + unknowns + source), Workflows (unknowns + source), Agents (capability-overhang + source), Models (capability-overhang + source); concept-index places capability-overhang under Agents/Coding Agents/Models, shrink-prompt under Coding Agents/Context Engineering, unknowns under Coding Agents/Context Engineering/Workflows; index.md Current Emphasis got 1 new lead. ## [2026-07-11] ingest | Continual Learning for AI Agents: From Failures to Durable Improvements - Soheil Feizi, RELAI

- Source: [Continual Learning for AI Agents: From Failures to Durable Improvements - Soheil Feizi, RELAI](sources/20260705_2IxD9OB3XuQ.md)
- Processed: `2IxD9OB3XuQ`
- Updated topics: [Evaluation](topics/evaluation.md), [Agents](topics/agents.md)
- Updated concepts: [Verifiable Continual Learning: Prove Each Agent Fix Helps and Breaks Nothing](concepts/verifiable-continual-learning-prove-each-fix-helps-and-breaks-nothing.md), [Route Agent Repairs to the Right Layer With the Smallest Durable Change](concepts/route-agent-repairs-to-the-right-layer-smallest-durable-change.md), [Make Regression-Aware Optimization Part of the Continual-Learning Loop](concepts/make-regression-aware-optimization-part-of-the-continual-learning-loop.md), [Ground agent simulation and evaluation in production logs](concepts/ground-agent-simulation-and-evaluation-in-production-logs.md) (strengthened)
- Notes: Soheil Feizi (founder/CSO of RELAI; CS professor, University of Maryland) frames continual learning for AI agents as learning from production experience — act, get feedback, improve *without forgetting* — and argues the key discipline is **verifiable continual learning (VCL)**: every fix "proven to help and proven to break nothing that already worked," via an executable test, a measured before/after delta, and a regression test. Two challenges. Feedback: dev-time benchmarks give evaluators, but "production logs are not learning environments" — a log-plus-feedback pair is one observation, not testable, so continual learning must "infer a distribution from one observation" and lift the log into a replayable learning environment (infer real vs mock tools and their mocking data, synthetic users, and evaluators) that is executable. Feedback sources at runtime: automatic (another model/LLM/code, even the agent critiquing its own log — scalable) or human-expert (low-volume, high-value domain feedback). Acting: three update layers with cost/verifiability tradeoffs — model (SFT / RL DPO-GRPO-RLVR / LoRA; expensive, needs benchmarks/evaluators), harness/context (prompts, skills, tools, code, workflow — trace-to-harness coding-agent edits are flexible but "vibe-based"/untestable; GEPA prompt-search is testable but needs a benchmark), and memory (facts + skill distillation via Letta/Mem0 — cheapest/fastest but usually unverified). Four principles: replayability, holisticness ("smallest durable change at the right layer" — a stale-policy/skipped-escalation failure could stem from memory, prompt, tool, workflow, or model), lifelongness (given K past environments and a new E(K+1), regression-aware learning fixes the new subject to no regression on the past, in-loop not post-hoc, sub-linear in K), and efficiency (loop runs frequently; efficiency in per-layer update cost and in the optimization loop). RELAI's loop: signals → replayable learning environments → root-cause routing → regression-aware optimize → reviewable version PR; added with a one-time learning-harness setup (BYO LLM, any major agent framework) plus two commands. Demo: fictional tool-using support agent with deterministic evaluators and a built-in *regression trap*; the instruction "caller is rude and adversarial" alone builds an environment (personas, intents, mock/real tools, evaluators), simulates to 78%, and one optimize loop lifts ~10% (to 97%); a production log+feedback example ("keep fast eligible refunds, but do not generalize generosity beyond refund thresholds") optimizes without regressing past behavior. Three takeaways: agent continual learning is not necessarily model fine-tuning; production logs are not learning environments; the frontier is regression-aware continual improvement. Three new concepts — verifiable-continual-learning-prove-each-fix-helps-and-breaks-nothing (the prove-helps/prove-breaks-nothing framing: 3 steps + 4 principles, distinct from replay-production-failures-before-promoting-prompt-fixes and connect-production-observability-to-offline-eval-loops), route-agent-repairs-to-the-right-layer-smallest-durable-change (the model/harness/memory routing taxonomy with cost/verifiability tradeoffs), make-regression-aware-optimization-part-of-the-continual-learning-loop (the in-loop lifelong/no-catastrophic-forgetting constraint) — plus a strengthen to ground-agent-simulation-and-evaluation-in-production-logs (the one-observation→replayable-environment / "infer a distribution" angle + source). Updated Evaluation (continual-learning-as-eval-loop synthesis + 3 Key Concepts + source) and Agents (how-an-agent-improves-after-shipping synthesis + 2 Key Concepts + source); concept-index places VCL and route-to-right-layer under both Evaluation and Agents and regression-aware under both; index.md Current Emphasis got 1 new lead. Selected one new processed source `2IxD9OB3XuQ`, the newest unprocessed by upload_date (2026-07-05) once the 2026-07-06 through 07-11 clusters were already processed; 304 of 476 unique sources. - Notes: Veronica Hylak (Hey AI; ~10 years in tech, six in AI starting with autonomous military ships, YouTuber whose AI explainers have reached 8M+ views, working with YC startups/AI tools/safety orgs in SF) gives a six-minute communication playbook for technical founders. Premise: shipping is easy now, "you only have one shot to make your mark" (about an elevator ride), and "great tech that nobody understands dies quietly," so the fatal gap is answering "why does this matter to an average user?" The typical AI pitch ("we're building an agentic AI orchestration platform for enterprise knowledge retrieval… agents talking to other agents inside a multi-agent workflow") makes buyers feel nothing. Three-part fix to turn complex AI products into stories people "instantly understand, remember, and want to buy." Two new concepts. (1) explain-ai-products-as-a-wound-click-transformation-story — the three-beat structure: **the wound** (open on the user's biggest pain, not what you built; ~20 seconds to identify the wound → "we fix that" → show how; good SecOps example immerses in exhausted security teams juggling disconnected tools) and **the transformation** (stop describing, prove value with an explicit before/after: "30 minutes digging through docs → one question, answer in 10 seconds with sources"; "if I cannot see what changed, I do not feel the story"); distinct from ai-does-not-replace-shareable-product-marketing (earning attention) and fundraising-pitches-should-explain-why-you-and-why-now (investor narrative) because its unit is the first user-facing sentence about what the product does. (2) give-a-concrete-mental-image-instead-of-a-category-label — the "make it click" middle beat: 17-year-old test, tie to a viral story people already understand (McDonald's AI drive-thru putting bacon on ice cream), ban words no one can picture, give a mental image ("Devin, the AI software engineer"; "a smoke alarm for AI behavior") as a *front door* into the conversation with the technical definition saved to close the sale; distinct from avoid-kitchen-sink-ai-capability-products (which it sharpens) since it's specifically about naming/analogy. Updated Product Strategy (a wound-click-transformation synthesis paragraph + 2 Key Concepts + source); concept-index places both under Product Strategy; index.md Current Emphasis got 1 new lead. ## [2026-07-11] ingest | The Missing Layer After Launch - Raphael Kalandadze, Wandero AI

- Source: [The Missing Layer After Launch - Raphael Kalandadze, Wandero AI](sources/20260705_kZsf_Sfm7RU.md)
- Processed: `kZsf_Sfm7RU`
- Updated topics: [Agents](topics/agents.md), [Evaluation](topics/evaluation.md), [Workflows](topics/workflows.md)
- Updated concepts: [Operate Agent Products as the Missing Post-Launch Layer](concepts/operate-agent-products-as-the-missing-post-launch-layer.md), [Staff Agent Operations With a Team of Agents](concepts/staff-agent-operations-with-a-team-of-agents.md), [Score Every Production Conversation to Judge Agent Health](concepts/score-every-production-conversation-to-judge-agent-health.md)
- Notes: Raphael Kalandadze (co-founder/CTO of Wandero AI, an agent-native OS for travel/hospitality; co-founder of Tbilisi AI Lab) argues shipping an agent is the *easy* part and the real work is the neglected "missing layer" of operating it after launch. It is unlike classical software: coverage is endless (like Claude Code/Codex you can't enumerate/pre-test the flows), LLMs are non-deterministic (same input → different trajectory), and failure is *silent* — "nothing crashes and no log lights up; the failure is in the conversation, not the stack trace." An agent can struggle mid-task, get lucky, recover with a workaround, and finish green while having failed the customer ("technically successful but still failing the task"; cites Anthropic that agents "love to make features as complete without checking they actually worked"), so reliable agents "shouldn't depend on the luck," "green CI means nothing," unit tests are "one slice," and "production is where you learn what you need to test in the first place." Because separating a real bug from noise and a symptom from a root cause needs reasoning, "the operating agent itself is an agent problem," so Wandero runs a "meta harness" of operating agents: a **log-monitoring agent** (fast loop, rolling window over logs/trajectories + code-base access → PR with description/metadata/mermaid-ASCII/HTML artifacts + static-analysis pass, or Slack alert; PR-ready in ~30 min), a separate **fresh-context review agent** (agents are "eager to send the PR" and biased toward their own fix, so it criticizes/scores each PR — root cause or just the symptom? — runs focused tests, requests changes or closes it), a **session analyzer** scoring *every* conversation (not a sample; runs many state agents, spends many tokens) into a health dashboard (sessions/cost/avg-score/success-rate/trends/sentiment/tool-call analytics + AI insights with root cause, sessions affected, recommended fix; run weekly; built in-house "because I know what I'm looking for"), and a **computer-use agent** that logs in and simulates the customer to catch UI failures (product-specific DOM skill faster than a generic Codex browser, token-heavy). Humans stay at merge/approval only — PR + review agents "send 10 times more PRs than the three of us every day" — with the staging rule "close the loop first" (be the bottleneck, then remove yourself). Meta-harness principle: give each operating agent all the human context (trajectories, metrics, database, UI); "it's not the model alone." Created three concepts — operate-agent-products-as-the-missing-post-launch-layer (the discipline + silent-failure / finished≠helpful / luck-dependent failure modes, distinct from wrap-agent-completion-in-a-deterministic-verification-gate, a build-time enforcement gate), staff-agent-operations-with-a-team-of-agents (the four-agent meta harness + fresh-context reviewer + humans-at-merge + full-context principle, distinct from observability-to-pr-agents and incident-agents which are infra/software incident response), score-every-production-conversation-to-judge-agent-health (LLM session-analyzer scoring every session for health, distinct from apply-online-scoring-with-cost-aware-sampling which samples and analyze-operational-health-over-time-slices which is infra metrics). Updated Agents (post-launch-operations synthesis + 3 Key Concepts + source), Evaluation (score-every-conversation continuous-monitoring synthesis + 1 Key Concept + source), Workflows (post-launch operations pipeline synthesis + 1 Key Concept + source); concept-index places operate under Agents/Evaluation/Workflows, staff under Agents/Coding Agents/Workflows, score under Agents/Evaluation/Workflows; index.md Current Emphasis got 1 new lead. ## [2026-07-11] ingest | MCP Apps: Primitives, discovery, and the Future of Software - Pietro Zullo, Manufact, Inc

- Source: [MCP Apps: Primitives, discovery, and the Future of Software - Pietro Zullo, Manufact, Inc](sources/20260705_sAOBXCDiDOs.md)
- Processed: `sAOBXCDiDOs`
- Updated topics: [Tools](topics/tools.md), [Agents](topics/agents.md), [Product Strategy](topics/product-strategy.md)
- Updated concepts: [Use MCP App Primitives for Bidirectional Model–UI State](concepts/use-mcp-app-primitives-for-bidirectional-model-ui-state.md), [Distribute MCP Apps Through Stores and Dynamic Discovery](concepts/distribute-mcp-apps-through-stores-and-dynamic-discovery.md), [MCP Applications Ship UI and Tools Together](concepts/mcp-applications-ship-ui-and-tools-together.md)
- Notes: Pietro Zullo (co-founder of Manufact, YC S25; maintainers of the `mcp-use` SDK, 8M+ downloads across PyPI/npm) gives a builder's tour of MCP Apps as a full bidirectional interaction layer plus a distribution channel — "not just an MCP server with a UI bolted on." Architecture recap: the tool returns not a JSON string but a widget rendered by the host in a sandboxed iframe, declared as a `ui://` resource at initialization and populated with the tool's arguments, with a postMessage-style channel back to the host. Timeline: MCP launched 2024; ~May 2025 co-founder Ido Solomon started MCP UI; ChatGPT shipped the Apps SDK; end of 2025 Character AI and Claude quietly opened MCP stores (design-partner-gated); January 2026 MCP UI converged into MCP Apps, "the official extension of the Model Context Protocol." Distinct contribution — five interaction primitives: (1) set-state / update-model-context (widget pushes live state into the model without a user message); (2) UI message / follow-up (widget messages the conversation unprompted — Claude prefills the chat input, OpenAI auto-sends and streams); (3) stream partial tool-call args into the UI incrementally so it draws as tokens arrive (Excalidraw mermaid-to-canvas, a Remotion React video rendering live); (4) widget calls other tools; (5) dual output for privacy — a tool returns a *list* of outputs, so send rich data into the widget but a separate redacted output (or "the user is seeing their private information above") to the model, enabling agent UX where sharing raw data with the provider is disallowed. Minor primitives: inline/full-screen/PiP display modes, external-link opening, host-theme sync, and client-support detection from exchanged metadata. Build with `mcp-use`: any widget file in the `resources` folder auto-registers as a `ui://` resource; the widget is a React component compiled to HTML/CSS; `npx create-mcp-app` scaffolds a template. Distribution: the client stores (ChatGPT apps at chatgpt.com/apps, Claude connectors, Cursor directory) opened for self-serve submission of both servers and Apps (UI not required); submission scans tool annotations/arguments and declared auth, requires test cases/prompts, accepts/rejects (ChatGPT faster than Claude), replacing shared JSON config with one-click install URLs (Manufact/manifact.com vets pre-submission and generates artifacts). Real unlock: dynamic discovery — Claude is currently the only client that searches the MCP registry for the right connector when a task has no matching tool, so with 1B+ users expressing intent in chat, being the selected connector is high-intent organic distribution (ChatGPT expected soon). Thesis: Paul Graham's "AI apps are the new browsers" → ChatGPTs are the new websites that return UI via MCP Apps. Created two concepts — use-mcp-app-primitives-for-bidirectional-model-ui-state (the five primitives + display/detection surface, distinct from mcp-applications-ship-ui-and-tools-together which owns the architecture/render, render-third-party-generative-ui-through-a-double-iframe which owns iframe/CSP security, and place-agent-ui-on-the-static-declarative-generative-spectrum which owns who-produces-the-UI) and distribute-mcp-apps-through-stores-and-dynamic-discovery (stores + submission + one-click install + registry dynamic discovery as a GTM channel, distinct from deploy-remote-mcp-servers-on-serverless and design-mcp-servers-as-agent-products). Strengthened mcp-applications-ship-ui-and-tools-together with the Manufact lineage/mechanics confirmation, cross-links, and source. Updated Tools (interaction-layer + distribution synthesis paragraph + 2 Key Concepts + source), Agents (primitives Key Concept + source), Product Strategy (dynamic-discovery-as-distribution synthesis + 1 Key Concept + source); concept-index places both new concepts under Tools and the distribution one also under Product Strategy; index.md Current Emphasis got 1 new lead. ## [2026-07-11] ingest | The Prompt Is Still a Punch Card - Ted Johnson, JoinIn AI

- Source: [The Prompt Is Still a Punch Card - Ted Johnson, JoinIn AI](sources/20260702_hVJOnuhFmTA.md)
- Processed: `hVJOnuhFmTA`
- Updated topics: [Product Strategy](topics/product-strategy.md), [Voice Agents](topics/voice-agents.md)
- Updated concepts: [Treat the Prompt as a Batch Protocol, Not a Conversation](concepts/treat-the-prompt-as-a-batch-protocol-not-a-conversation.md), [Treat AI as an Interface Technology That Removes Human Burden](concepts/treat-ai-as-an-interface-technology-that-removes-human-burden.md)
- Notes: Ted Johnson (co-founder of JoinIn.AI; 25 years in enterprise software, collaboration systems, and AI interfaces) reframes AI as an *interface* technology, not only an intelligence technology, to explain why capable models still "feel unnatural to use." He splits any human-machine interface into three parts — the **channel** (physical transport/bandwidth: keyboard, mic, screen, punch card, prompt box), **expression** (the range of meaning the channel is permitted to carry), and the **protocol** (the rules and shape of the exchange). Thesis: with LLMs the channel never changed (still typing into a box, still hitting submit) and expression *exploded* (assembly opcodes → shell commands/flags → composable programming primitives → natural language, which "blew the menu open"), but the protocol stayed **batch**, inherited from the punch card, which inherited it from the weaving loom. Prompting *is* the batch loop (assemble the whole request, submit, wait, read, fix one thing, resubmit; "the machine never engaged with you while you were thinking"), "interactive features" are "batch with interactive sprinkles," and shrinking the wait to seconds "fooled us into thinking that it become interactive… it's still batch. You still package a complete turn before the machine is allowed to participate." "Prompt engineering" is a flattering label for rules to assemble a good batch deck (think step-by-step, act/don't act as an expert, paste more/less context, only talk in markdown — "we trade incantations"), the same mastery a punch-card operator had. The mismatch is model capacity (reasoning, speech, vision, memory, planning) curving up while the protocol stays flat, so the human still carries every interface burden (choosing context, remembering what to ask, timing, ambiguity, repair, prompt engineering) and, when it fails, wrongly blames themselves — "the mismatch isn't the user, it's the interface"; each historical step also carried "a translation tax, a precision tax, context tax, repair tax." The fix is to move timing and modality off the human and let the AI *participate* with the affordances humans already use (a question, a pause, a sketch, a checklist, a quiet aside, or saying nothing). Voice examples: a frontier speech-to-speech model answered "Hey Ted, come on in" as a turn because a prompt box "has no concept of who's speaking, whether the words were even meant for it"; OpenAI's GPT real-time 2 (late May) backchannels ("mhm"/"right"); NVIDIA's research model yields on interruption and resumes the thread — but "making listening noises is not really the same as knowing who's in the room." JoinIn's protocol adds multi-party awareness: a utility-driven model labels each statement (question/proposal/answer), tracks who a speaker refers to, and "only takes a turn when no one else is speaking or holding the floor," resolving a meeting's scope objective with "no one wrote the prompt, no one patched the turn and hit submit." Mindset shift: "book-smart models alone are not enough"; the design question becomes "what burden are we still putting on humans only because the machine used to be too limited to carry that burden itself?" Cites Sherry Turkle (MIT): "conversation is the most human and humanizing thing we do." Created two concepts — treat-the-prompt-as-a-batch-protocol-not-a-conversation (channel/expression/protocol frame + batch-loop diagnosis + prompt-engineering-is-deck-assembly + capability/interface mismatch, distinct from shrink-the-system-prompt and engineer-the-interaction-not-the-model) and treat-ai-as-an-interface-technology-that-removes-human-burden (AI-as-interface reframe + the burden design question + participation-needs-multi-party-awareness, distinct from choose-ai-coworker-form-factors and full-duplex-speech-models). Updated Product Strategy (interface-as-lever synthesis paragraph + 2 Key Concepts + source) and Voice Agents (interaction-protocol synthesis sentence + 2 Key Concepts + source); concept-index places both under Product Strategy and Voice Agents; index.md Current Emphasis got 1 new lead. - Updated concepts: [Elicit Requirements as the Non-Automatable Bottleneck](concepts/elicit-requirements-as-the-non-automatable-bottleneck.md), [Capture AI-Build Requirements With Story Maps and User Stories](concepts/capture-ai-build-requirements-with-story-maps-and-user-stories.md), [Measure Feature Adoption, Not Shipping Velocity](concepts/measure-feature-adoption-not-shipping-velocity.md)
- Notes: Balázs Horváth (founder of VisualLabs, a Budapest premium Microsoft partner; 13 years bridging business and IT on US/UK ERP and CRM programs) argues that once AI makes code, specs, tests, and implementations cheap, the software-delivery bottleneck moves upstream to "people work" — getting the right stakeholders in the room, eliciting the real requirements, and naming what is worth building: "you can prompt your AI… but you can't prompt the room." Evidence: a VisualLabs internal hackathon generated ~21 agent ideas and abandoned 17 for lack of business value (no data access, no clear business owner, or no measurable value); the 4 survivors run in production. Because a model "is coded to give you the most common answers," naive use replicates a Henry Ford "faster horse," so a human must read the room and name the problem to reach the car ("a magnitude shift better"). Remedy resurfaces the business-analyst toolkit as newly scarce and AI-native: the VAD thinking path (Value → Architecture → Design), a 4-question value framework (whose problem / what winning looks like / what causes refusal / what decision it changes) tracked "in a good old markdown file in your repository so that AI can access it," story mapping (support-system backbone contact→triage→resolve→close, slice release-one as MVP, second row as backlog, kept high-level), and persona/what/need/why user stories with acceptance criteria that "derive the test cases" — a structure models were "actually trained on," so the familiar shape outperforms a generic prompt, and daisy-chained stories compile into spec → code. Delivery metric shift: stop counting features shipped, count features "used more than twice," watch the *frequency* of an activity not time-on-site, and reject two anti-patterns — "the demo is the deliverable" (a demo system is not a live system) and a PRD with no real user testers. Org lever: move the smartest people from writing code toward customers/business problems "because that's the expensive part; building it has actually become very cheap." Created three concepts — elicit-requirements-as-the-non-automatable-bottleneck (the you-can't-prompt-the-room thesis + hackathon evidence + faster-horse + move-upstream, distinct from ai-amplified-shipping-speed-needs-stronger-product-taste which owns taste-as-filter and product-engineers-need-direct-customer-context which owns proximity), capture-ai-build-requirements-with-story-maps-and-user-stories (the concrete VAD + 4-question + story-map + AI-native-user-story workflow, a requirements front-end distinct from the topics' research-plan-implement discipline), and measure-feature-adoption-not-shipping-velocity (used-more-than-twice KPI + frequency-not-time-on-site + demo/testerless anti-patterns). Updated Product Strategy (upstream-requirements synthesis paragraph + 3 Key Concepts + source) and Coding Agents (requirements-front-end synthesis paragraph + 1 Key Concept + source); concept-index places all three under Product Strategy and the story-map concept also under Coding Agents; index.md Current Emphasis got 1 new lead. ## [2026-07-11] ingest | Deterministic Infra for Non-Deterministic AI Agents - Nishant Gupta, Meta Superintelligence Labs

- Source: [Deterministic Infra for Non-Deterministic AI Agents - Nishant Gupta, Meta Superintelligence Labs](sources/20260629_APh1Vx0oLmQ.md)
- Processed: `APh1Vx0oLmQ`
- Updated topics: [Infrastructure](topics/infrastructure.md), [Agents](topics/agents.md)
- Updated concepts: [Build an Agentic Control Plane So the Model Proposes and the Platform Decides](concepts/build-an-agentic-control-plane.md), [Contain Retry Amplification Before It Becomes a Compute Incident](concepts/contain-retry-amplification-in-agent-loops.md), [Treat multi-agent systems as distributed systems](concepts/treat-multi-agent-systems-as-distributed-systems.md)
- Notes: Nishant Gupta (software-engineering tech lead at Meta Superintelligence Labs, building training/inference infra) argues that as organizations move from chatbots to autonomous agents, "the challenge is no longer intelligence — the challenge is reliability," and most platforms were built for deterministic microservices, not long-running non-deterministic LLM systems ("the great mismatch": agents are stateful, long-running, decide dynamically, and may run different workflows for the same input, violating the short-lived/deterministic/known-path/bounded-failure assumptions). Demos showcase capability ("can it do it?"); production asks whether it can do it reliably 10k/100k/million times, recover from failures, operate safely, and hit acceptable cost/latency/outcome — so engineering effort "moves below the model layer into orchestration, monitoring, safety evaluation, and recovery." Created two concepts — build-an-agentic-control-plane (the central move: "never let the model directly control production systems… the model generates proposals, infrastructure validates, policy engine approves, execution gateway enforces; the model just suggests, the platform decides," so reliability holds even when the model stays stochastic; the control plane is the Kubernetes/service-mesh analog owning scheduling, memory coordination, policy enforcement, evaluation, monitoring, workload routing — "an operating system for autonomous AI," distinct from keep-human-review-on-high-risk-agent-operations and llm-guardrails-need-checkpoints which are narrower slices) and contain-retry-amplification-in-agent-loops (the named failure mode: an agent responds to a tool error by regenerating a slightly-different-but-still-invalid request, each retry consumes more compute and deeper reasoning, GPU rises, "what started as a minor API error became a compute incident" — "uncontrolled retries are one of the biggest risks," distinct from wrap-agent-calls-with-circuit-breakers which owns the fix pattern). Strengthened treat-multi-agent-systems-as-distributed-systems with the Meta framing — "AI agents should be treated as distributed systems… models are stochastic, infrastructure must be deterministic," memory issues as "consistency failures masquerading as reasoning failures," and the DS-primitive reuse map (circuit breakers → tool isolation, rate limits → agent limits, retries → controlled recovery, resource quotas → cost governance, observability → agent tracing). Also covered: real failures are infrastructural not hallucinations; multi-dimensional causal observability (trace planning decisions/tool calls/memory lookups/state transitions to know *why*); layered defense-in-depth safety; humans as permanent exception handlers ("allocate human attention where it provides maximum value"); GPU inference as a cluster-scheduling/resource-orchestration problem; and the phase-shift thesis that as prompts and models commoditize, "the future of AI won't be won by better prompts — it will be won by better systems." Updated Infrastructure (agentic-control-plane synthesis paragraph + 2 Key Concepts + source) and Agents (reliability-half-of-the-harness synthesis paragraph + 2 Key Concepts + source); concept-index places both new concepts under Infrastructure and Agents; index.md Current Emphasis got 1 new lead. ## [2026-07-11] ingest | The Prompt is the Platform - Dominik Tornow, Resonate HQ

- Source: [The Prompt is the Platform - Dominik Tornow, Resonate HQ](sources/20260629_DqtmZE6Hl0g.md)
- Processed: `DqtmZE6Hl0g`
- Updated topics: [Coding Agents](topics/coding-agents.md), [Evaluation](topics/evaluation.md), [Infrastructure](topics/infrastructure.md)
- Updated concepts: [Treat the Specification as the Product and Derive Bespoke Implementations](concepts/treat-the-specification-as-the-product-and-derive-bespoke-implementations.md), [Use Deterministic Simulation as Executable Design for Agents](concepts/use-deterministic-simulation-as-executable-design-for-agents.md), [Expose Forbidden-Fruit Facts in Simulation for Agent Debugging](concepts/expose-forbidden-fruit-facts-in-simulation-for-agent-debugging.md)
- Notes: Dominik Tornow (founder/CEO of Resonate, a durable execution platform, author of *Think Distributed Systems*) argues coding agents will "quietly retire their first software platform" — not because it's bad but because it's *unnecessary*: general-purpose implementations get replaced by bespoke ones generated on demand as minimal extensions of infrastructure already in place, so reuse and value move upstream from implementation to an abstract, target-agnostic *specification* ("the prompt is a platform, the specification is a product"), and the real question is whether you can *repeatedly synthesize trusted* servers from one spec. Resonate's product becomes the protocol, from which they derive a reference server, a Postgres server, and partner-built servers (Synadia/NATS.io); SDKs exist for TypeScript/Python/Rust/Go/Java. Core engineering contribution: prompting an agent straight from an abstract spec to a Rust-on-Postgres server produced happy-path code that passed basic tests but broke on concurrency/process/network failure (prototype, not production); a human-authored concrete spec (schema, indices, SQL, transaction boundaries) let the agent *build* but not *design*, so they inserted a deterministic simulation step and changed the task to "build a simulated implementation" — executable design whose purpose is to discover the correct algorithm under partial order under partial failure. Pipeline: abstract spec → simulation → concrete spec → concrete implementation, agent as driver (humans still in design). Created three concepts — treat-the-specification-as-the-product-and-derive-bespoke-implementations (reuse/value move upstream to an abstract target-agnostic spec; distinct from spec-driven-development which is a requirements→design→tasks flow for one app), use-deterministic-simulation-as-executable-design-for-agents (insert a simulated implementation over the target's primitives so the agent designs, not just builds/verifies; distinct from ground-agent-simulation-and-evaluation-in-production-logs and neural-debugging), and expose-forbidden-fruit-facts-in-simulation-for-agent-debugging (the simulator records facts the real platform hides — fresh/stale read tag + the missed latest value — that the *algorithm* may not depend on but the *agent* may use to explain *why* an invariant broke; distinct from expose-observability-as-agent-readable-feedback by being a sim-only design-time trace). Enablers: minimalism/simplicity as the finish line (3 years shrinking the protocol to two objects — a durable promise and a durable task); express the protocol using only the target's primitives (NATS queues, versioned KV store, delayed messages) and stay correct under the target's real consistency model (legal stale reads, optimistic-concurrency write failures), not convenient behavior; agents thrive on immediate unambiguous feedback that shows *why/how* a run failed. Simulated KV store in Python keeps full version history, injects stale reads via a deterministic RNG, enforces optimistic concurrency — deterministic, repeatable, inspectable, so a broken run reproduces exactly and the agent repairs against the trace. Updated Coding Agents (synthesis paragraph + 3 Key Concepts + source), Evaluation (simulation-as-design-and-debugging synthesis paragraph + 2 Key Concepts + source), and Infrastructure (spec-as-product distribution-shift synthesis paragraph + 1 Key Concept + source); concept-index places the three concepts under Coding Agents, the spec-as-product one also under Infrastructure, and the two simulation concepts also under Evaluation; index.md Current Emphasis got 1 new lead. Selected one new processed source `DqtmZE6Hl0g`, newest unprocessed by upload_date (2026-06-29, lowest remaining video_id in that cluster after 6bmM45jkMDY and APh1Vx0oLmQ) once the 2026-07-02 through 07-11 clusters were already processed; 311 of 476 rows in processed-sources, 168 truly-unprocessed in index.jsonl (308 processed ids appear in index.jsonl; 3 processed ids do not). ## [2026-07-11] ingest | Your Agent Failed in Prod. Good Luck Reproducing It. - Tisha Chawla & Susheem Koul, Microsoft

- Source: [Your Agent Failed in Prod. Good Luck Reproducing It. - Tisha Chawla & Susheem Koul, Microsoft](sources/20260629_Lc8zRh9muoY.md)
- Processed: `Lc8zRh9muoY`
- Updated topics: [Inference](topics/inference.md), [Evaluation](topics/evaluation.md), [Agents](topics/agents.md)
- Updated concepts: [LLM Inference Is Non-Deterministic Even at Temperature Zero](concepts/llm-inference-is-non-deterministic-even-at-temperature-zero.md), [Record and Replay Agent Runs at Node Boundaries](concepts/record-and-replay-agent-runs-at-node-boundaries.md), [Turn Recorded Agent Traces Into Free Replay Test Cases](concepts/turn-recorded-agent-traces-into-free-replay-test-cases.md)
- Notes: Tisha Chawla and Susheem Koul (Microsoft, Commerce & Ecosystem Data Platform) argue the real production-agent failure mode is not the bad decision but the inability to reproduce it: a non-deterministic run that corrupts a record (a broker agent told to "sell $1,000 of stock" dumped the raw 1,000 into the quantity field and sold 1,000 shares, ~$190k at $190/share, while the API returned a clean 200 OK in 30ms with green dashboards) rarely repeats its trajectory, so pulling the prompt and re-running succeeds every time and the one costly run is gone — "if you can't reproduce it, you can't debug it; if you can't debug it, you can't promise it won't happen to your next customer." They debunk the temperature-zero reflex from first principles (temp 0 fixes argmax token selection but not run-to-run reproducibility): (1) sampling determinism ≠ system determinism; (2) floating-point math isn't associative, so a tiny matrix-op shift flips the winning token; (3) not concurrency — the real culprit is batch invariance (a request is grouped with whatever hits the server that millisecond); (4) MoE routing reroutes tokens when a batch overflows an expert's capacity, depending on co-batched traffic. Reframe: stop chasing bitwise determinism (controllability you can't get from a hosted API and don't want, since randomness "brings the agency into your agent"); build replayability (observability) — don't freeze the model, capture what it did. Record at the boundary, not the network layer (half the agent never touches the network; packets shred under streaming/async). Demo of Chronicle, a framework-agnostic record-and-replay layer whose `boundary` annotation wraps any node (tool call, LLM call, RAG retrieval), freezing input/output plus session variables (model version, build ID, RAG chunks) as a hyper-detailed per-node JSON trace, so you can step from the bad `place_order(quantity=1000)` call back to the LLM node and rerun the exact failure line with zero model calls. Loop: annotation → recording → visualization → understanding → fixing → replaying → verifying. Because you can't control the LLM, put guardrails on the tools and test via replay mode: load the trace, stub every node except the changed one (run the guardrailed tool live), and assert the order got blocked — "merging replayability traces with auto-generated testing, stubbing, and assertions." Two testing modes: deterministic (guardrails/tools; stub the LLM → free, model-free rerunnable test case) vs behavioral (tone/trajectory; LLM-as-judge). This is Mozilla-rr record-and-replay in the agent loop, beyond API mocking/response caching, and complements durable execution (durability keeps the loop alive but is not debuggability). Created three concepts — llm-inference-is-non-deterministic-even-at-temperature-zero (FP non-associativity + batch invariance + MoE routing under greedy decoding), record-and-replay-agent-runs-at-node-boundaries (record semantic node I/O as a frozen trace to re-enter a non-reproducible failure with zero model calls; distinct from record-workflow-history-for-agent-debugging-and-compliance and use-durable-execution-for-production-agent-loops), and turn-recorded-agent-traces-into-free-replay-test-cases (a trace becomes a rerunnable regression test by stubbing all nodes but the one under change; distinct from replay-production-failures-before-promoting-prompt-fixes which reruns through the live model/eval loop). Updated Inference (non-determinism synthesis paragraph + 1 Key Concept + 1 Open Question + source), Evaluation (record-and-replay-substrate synthesis paragraph + 2 Key Concepts + source), and Agents (reproducibility-layer synthesis paragraph + 1 Key Concept + source); concept-index places record-and-replay and replay-test-cases under Agents and Evaluation, and non-determinism under Inference and Evaluation; index.md Current Emphasis got 1 new lead. Selected one new processed source `Lc8zRh9muoY`, newest unprocessed by upload_date (2026-06-29, lowest remaining video_id in that cluster after 6bmM45jkMDY, APh1Vx0oLmQ, and DqtmZE6Hl0g) once the 2026-07-02 through 07-11 clusters were already processed; 312 of 476 rows in processed-sources, 167 truly-unprocessed in index.jsonl (309 processed ids appear in index.jsonl; 3 processed ids do not). ## [2026-08-19] ingest | Don't be data poor — Anuj Iravane, Anterior

- Updated topics: [Evaluation](topics/evaluation.md), [Workflows](topics/workflows.md), [Healthcare Operations](topics/healthcare-operations.md), [Agents](topics/agents.md)
- Updated concepts: [Generate Eval Data by Reversing the Inference Workflow](concepts/generate-eval-data-by-reversing-the-inference-workflow.md), [Build Synthetic Records Coarse to Fine by Emulating How They Were Produced](concepts/build-synthetic-records-coarse-to-fine-by-emulating-their-source-process.md), [Hand Domain Experts the Pipeline as Skills](concepts/hand-domain-experts-the-pipeline-as-skills.md), [Production Failure Sets Drive Domain AI Iteration](concepts/production-failure-sets-drive-domain-ai-iteration.md)
- Notes: Compiled Anuj Iravane's ~16.5-min talk on how Anterior builds eval datasets it is contractually forbidden to keep. Created three concepts: (1) reverse the inference workflow — sample the label, sample a reasoning trace from the policy's explicit decision-tree representation, and synthesize the record backwards, which both beats LLM mode collapse ("a much more uniform and effective prior distribution") and makes labels "correct by construction" via a round-trip check, retiring expensive ground-truthing; (2) build long synthetic artifacts coarse to fine by emulating the process that produced them (invariants → patient journey of provider encounters → per-encounter document plan → hydrating fan-out), which is simultaneously the token-efficiency and context-window-scaling mechanism, and which forces an LLM consistency eval over the independently generated branches; (3) hand domain experts the pipeline as skills — every stage is a skill file on a generic internal agent harness, so a clinician adds a new document type without an engineering change, on the stated grounds that "no AI engineer would" know what a good medical record looks like. Also strengthened Anterior's own earlier concept, production-failure-sets-drive-domain-ai-iteration, with this talk's qualification: production cases can be observed but not retained, so clinicians re-create them synthetically, and a 200-case production sample under-covers the tail regardless. Healthcare Operations gained a fifth-position paragraph reading the two Anterior talks a year apart as one argument (the earlier one closes 95%→99% with expert-labelled production failure sets; this one supplies the data that loop presupposes and reports it unavailable), making governance the fifth binding constraint in the cluster alongside hazard, autonomy, consequence, and retryability. Two new open questions ask what validates a policy tree's own coverage once eval sets are sampled from it rather than from traffic.
