- Updated concepts: [Run Must-Not-Fail Decisions in a Code Layer Above the Model](concepts/run-must-not-fail-decisions-in-code-above-the-model.md), [Make Regulated-Data Failures Architecturally Impossible](concepts/make-regulated-data-failures-architecturally-impossible.md), [Decide Ship-or-Hold With Explicit Severity Rules](concepts/decide-ship-or-hold-with-explicit-severity-rules.md), [Check Whether the Judge Is Right Before Changing the Agent](concepts/check-the-judge-before-changing-the-agent.md), [Score Every Production Conversation to Judge Agent Health](concepts/score-every-production-conversation-to-judge-agent-health.md)
- Notes: Compiled Rashi Agrawal's ~21.5-min talk on guardrails for member-facing health AI at Hinge Health. Created four concepts against a saturated deterministic-guardrail neighborhood, each staking out a position the existing pages do not hold: (1) must-not-fail decisions run in a code layer that executes *before* the model on every turn — a third position distinct from the post-generation veto and the tool-call wrapper, with escalation, high-stakes intent routing, and identity as its members, and the frontier labs' published authority hierarchy read as an admission that a prompt is not a security boundary; (2) make regulated-data failures architecturally impossible by stripping PHI at the ingestion boundary, cutting every production-to-dev pipe, and scoping access by region as well as role, so the system is "incapable of certain failures" rather than merely governed — the wiki's first page on regulated data-plane architecture; (3) five rules for resolving a launch-blocker dispute among clinical, legal, compliance, product, and engineering, whose transferable core is that worst plausible outcome sets severity (a move frequency-weighted metrics structurally cannot make), severity is independent of capacity so the only legal moves are fix/delay/accept-with-sign-off, and the launch bar is the org's revealed rather than stated tolerance; (4) when a live judge score drops, test the judge before editing the agent, with a caffeine-guidance pair showing an over-calling judge and a genuine agent error that are indistinguishable from the score alone — the operational counterpart to the wiki's offline judge-calibration thread. The layer-three material (judges on live traffic rather than a golden set, three signal sources, 100% sampling on high-stakes cases, "a new failure… means you now have a new judge," and human reading capacity as the ceiling) strengthened the existing score-every-production-conversation page rather than becoming a fifth concept. Healthcare Operations gained a sixth-position pair of paragraphs positioning Hinge Health as the consumer-scale member-facing tier whose risk reference is an industry baseline rather than a regulator's hazard list, and reading rule five ("judges scale, pattern interpretation doesn't") as the shared constraint behind Hippocratic AI's 7,000 clinician evaluators, Ufonia's 10-specialty panel, Maven's spot-check group, and Anterior's clinician skill-authors. Two new open questions ask when a judge stops earning human attention, and whether an org's revealed risk tolerance is the right reference point when the industry baseline is itself the thing the talk indicts.

## [2026-08-19] ingest | Trading Desks to Clinical Trials: Parallels in Applied Vertical AI — Ayush Bhardwaj, Allos AI

- Source: [Trading Desks to Clinical Trials: Parallels in Applied Vertical AI — Ayush Bhardwaj, Allos AI](sources/20260819_Yphdry8ttAQ.md)
- Processed: `Yphdry8ttAQ`
- Updated topics: [Product Strategy](topics/product-strategy.md), [Evaluation](topics/evaluation.md), [Workflows](topics/workflows.md), [Agents](topics/agents.md), [Models](topics/models.md)
- Updated concepts: [You Cannot Iterate on Output You Cannot Judge](concepts/you-cannot-iterate-on-output-you-cannot-judge.md), [High-Value Vertical Data Is Withheld by Design](concepts/high-value-vertical-data-is-withheld-by-design.md), [Hire the User to Own the Judgment Loop](concepts/hire-the-user-to-own-the-judgment-loop.md), [Keep the Expert as Decider With AI in Their Loop](concepts/keep-the-expert-as-decider-with-ai-in-their-loop.md)
- Notes: Compiled Ayush Bhardwaj's ~20-min talk comparing applied AI at a hedge fund with applied AI at a pharma-tech startup, which he reports as the same job. Created four concepts. (1) The judgment gap: the first four build steps "fit one screen" and are not the moat, so a team reaches shippable-looking output long before it can evaluate it, and vertical AI projects "quietly die" there — a silent failure, since nothing errors and the demo simply does not sell. LLM-as-judge is rejected from experience ("a really, really stupid mistake") on the grounds that verifiable rewards work in math and code "because you have answer keys" that these domains lack; the page also carries his ROI reframing of the Stanford AI Index 89%-never-reach-production stat ("every AI reaches production, but it just fails to work or justify its own cost"). (2) The structural-data argument, which is the wiki's first page on domain corpora that are withheld rather than merely scarce: 13F quarterly filings degrade returns once reverse-engineered, ~30% of firms never disclose clinical trials despite the legal requirement (FDA publicly reminding ~2,000 sponsors in 2026), so "neither OpenAI nor Anthropic has this data," and NDAs plus practitioner pay make annotation unbuyable — with the trade thesis and failed-experiment data named as the private halves worth curating. (3) Hire the user: the fix is a hire rather than a tool, with a four-rung expert loop (prompts → source curation → encoded decision ordering → judgment) and a cost-ordered improvement ladder starting at error analysis ("the cheapest of all, and I think the highest ROI") before rubrics-as-reward, SFT, and RLHF, plus the fine-tuning treadmill caveat. This sits one step earlier than the wiki's existing Anterior expert-review-tooling and skill-authorship pages, which presuppose experts the company already employs. (4) AI-in-the-loop rather than human-in-the-loop for irreversible verticals: the system offers five candidate trade theses and the trader decides, which sidesteps the automation-bias failure by keeping the decision upstream instead of adding an approval checkbox downstream. Three new open questions ask when the cheapest improvement rung stops paying, what makes an expert-generated data moat durable against a competitor who hires their own practitioner, and what operational test would justify moving a vertical workflow off AI-in-the-loop.

Healthcare Operations gained a seventh-position synthesis that reads equivalency against the cluster's five other human-in-the-loop arrangements — all of which place the human outside the run as reviewer or calibrator — and against Anterior's own two earlier talks; four new open questions across Healthcare Operations, Agents, Security, Infrastructure, and Evaluation record what the source leaves unresolved (how a human's authority is represented when humans and models are interchangeable executors, how escalated-tail human labels combine with full-traffic judge scores, what the event log's read cost becomes at agent event volumes, and where process segregation stops containing exfiltration).

- Updated concepts: [Size Your Eval Effort to the Generator–Verifier Gap](concepts/size-your-eval-effort-to-the-generator-verifier-gap.md), [Build Judge References From Independently Written, Adjudicated Expert Rubrics](concepts/build-judge-references-from-adjudicated-expert-rubrics.md), [Decompose the Deliverable and Post-Train a Small Model per Section](concepts/decompose-the-deliverable-and-post-train-a-model-per-section.md), [Train Your Own Models Only Where You Have a Right to Win](concepts/train-your-own-models-only-where-you-have-a-right-to-win.md), [Gate Always-On Listening With Cheap Event Detectors](concepts/gate-always-on-listening-with-cheap-event-detectors.md)
- Notes: Compiled Asawa's ~21-min talk on Abridge's move from ambient documentation to clinical intelligence, adding five concepts. On evaluation: the generator–verifier gap is a property of the *task* that decides how expensive evals will be, and when it collapses ("if I had a really really good generator verifier, then that would just be my generator itself") the answers are coverage by partial judges (quality, safety, boundary/adversarial, tone) and a manufactured reference — two physicians independently writing element rubrics, a third adjudicating, a fourth QAing, then an LLM judge doing semantic match. On cost at ~100M medical conversations a year: decompose the clinical note along its own sections and post-train a small model per section, gate always-on listening with cheap event detectors so in-visit order capture never polls a heavy model, and treat post-training as justified only by a "right to win" (saturated quality → train for cost/latency; unsaturated → only with unique data plus a problem the frontier labs are not focused on, betting on rate of change). Positioned the eval pages explicitly against the wiki's existing judgment-gap material: Allos AI locates the gap in the team and prescribes a hire, Abridge locates it in the task and prescribes a construction procedure, with clinicians already on staff. Eighth entry in the 2026-08-19 healthcare cluster and the first whose binding constraint is unit economics rather than risk governance.

- Source: [Training Krea 2: What matters in generative model training — Sangwu Lee, Krea.ai](sources/20260818_-tviRdpmHvs.md)
- Processed: `-tviRdpmHvs`
- Updated topics: [Generative Media](topics/generative-media.md), [Models](topics/models.md), [Vision AI](topics/vision-ai.md), [Product Strategy](topics/product-strategy.md)
- Updated concepts: [Reliability and Stylistic Range Are Opposite Model Positions](concepts/reliability-and-stylistic-range-are-opposite-model-positions.md), [Refuse AI-Generated Training Images When Style Is the Product](concepts/refuse-ai-generated-images-when-style-is-the-product.md), [Filter Training Images Your Captioner Systematically Mis-Describes](concepts/filter-images-your-captioner-systematically-mis-describes.md), [Order Billion-Scale Data Filters by Cost Per Sample](concepts/order-billion-scale-data-filters-by-cost-per-sample.md), [Use Sparse Autoencoder Features as an Unsupervised Data Tagger](concepts/use-sparse-autoencoder-features-as-an-unsupervised-data-tagger.md), [Run the LLM Post-Training Ladder on Diffusion Models](concepts/run-the-llm-post-training-ladder-on-diffusion-models.md), [Curate generative-media data before tuning model internals](concepts/curate-generative-media-data-before-tuning-model-internals.md)
- Notes: First 2026-08-18 source and the wiki's first end-to-end account of training an image foundation model, which converts a standing placeholder into a procedure — the existing "curate data before tuning model internals" page explicitly recorded that its source "does not disclose detailed curation recipes," and this talk supplies them, so that page was strengthened rather than duplicated. Six new concepts. Two are positioning: production image models buy reliability by mode-collapsing ("render the most boring average person… and put it in a center frame"), which makes reliability and stylistic range opposite ends of one dial selected by whether the user already knows what they want, and choosing diversity then forbids generic aesthetic-score filtering; and AI-generated images are refused outright because "synthetic data is like so sticky" that distillation ships a detectable copy of ChatGPT's or Nano Banana's look. Four are technique: a captioner's *systematic* omissions are a data-selection problem, since captions that never mention the frame and white wall teach the model to hang every painting it generates; billion-scale curation is a cost-ordered cascade (pHash/MD5 → SSCD/SigLIP embeddings → a large VLM's judgment distilled into a SigLIP-sized classifier, with Essential Web's ~500M-parameter text analogue and a PageRank-over-Wikipedia coverage sweep under the same budget); sparse autoencoders trained for interpretability double as an unsupervised tagger for watermarks, signatures, and border artifacts, inverting this wiki's existing SAE pages from model analysis to data infrastructure; and the training ladder is borrowed wholesale from LLM practice (256→1K resolution curriculum, mid-training/SFT to mold the distribution, preference pairs, GRPO-style RL against reward servers, plus a prompt expander that is "almost essential" because long prompts sit closer to the training distribution). Four open questions record what the source leaves unmeasured: the tolerable AI-image mixing ratio, the distilled filter's agreement with its teacher VLM, whether the PageRank coverage sweep helps at all (the speaker says he does not know), and whether a curation-discipline differentiator is defensible or only early.

- Notes: Third 2026-08-18 source and the wiki's first hobby-scale build, useful because the model layer is explicitly interchangeable (Piper or Apple TTS, Whisper, "any local model") while every hard problem is the non-model signal path. Four new concepts. Delivery: a JUCE plugin loaded into Logic Pro inherits routing, chaining, and the transport, on the framing that "your DAW is effectively your IDE but for musicians and music producers," so the plugin's UI carries only the new decisions. Segmentation: slicing synthesized speech into words defeats energy-gap segmentation because running speech often has no silence between words, a sonority-peak syllabifier adds an independent cue, and the residual after combining both was cheaper to fix in a drag-to-edit boundary UI than with a third heuristic. Real-time budget: WORLD pitch-shifting of VocalSet samples is "a very heavy process… I can't really do that live," so the transform runs once per fret offline and the runtime becomes a lookup — the cheapest of the wiki's three answers to a latency bar, licensed by an enumerable control surface and paid for in coverage (five vowels only). Control surface: decoupling what generated audio says from when it plays gives a performer timing, emphasis, and melody while taking generation latency off the live path entirely, which is a cheap version of the steerability Reactor's real-time-video talk argues requires new infrastructure — placing the two side by side is what made this ingest synthesis rather than a novelty summary. Caveats recorded on the pages: no accuracy numbers for segmentation, no comparison against forced alignment, no latency measurements, and the demo output is openly "choppy."

- Notes: Added eight concepts from the wiki's first controlled memory-harness ablation: memory as a write–manage–read control loop decomposed into core/recall/archival blocks; the recall ladder run with the model held fixed (no recall → vector RAG → ranked decisions ledger → oracle) plus adversarial wrong-memory rungs and a second model and benchmark; the measured null result when the corpus fits in the window against the step-124-answer/step-500-question case where the harness is the only route; the oracle ceiling as the instrument that partitions error between retrieval failure and use failure ("it doesn't force it to use it"); the ranked decisions ledger beating similarity, recency, and no-recall; the failed self-gating condition; the cost-quality coupling ("bad memory is expensive because it spends more token and it can send the agent the wrong way," yet the best policy was also the cheapest); and local inference as an experiment rig whose price is serial-only execution and multi-day thermal load. Strengthened eight existing pages rather than duplicating them: recorded an independent convergence on the similarity-is-the-wrong-signal claim while separating the two fixes (change the unit versus learn the ranking from outcomes), extended the do-nothing-baseline discipline with an oracle rung and adversarial rungs, gave the long-context page its boundary measured from both sides in one study, supplied the offloading page with its sharpest task shape plus the context-rot symptom triad, added a component-level worked instance of harness engineering on local models, showed how the METR time-horizon trend paired with "fewer and fewer model releases" becomes a planning input for harness investment, and gave the memory-budget page its read-side cost law. Provenance is carried onto every concept: single-author unpublished work in a 13-minute talk by a Sakana AI researcher that closes on the company's sovereign-AI positioning and a hiring call, run entirely on local quantized models, and reporting **no effect sizes anywhere** — only orderings, with no variance or significance test across 68 questions. Caption artifacts recorded on the source note: "Meter" resolved to METR and "X-Bench" to xbench; the model captioned "Qwen 27B" and the cookbook repository attributed to "Diamond" are flagged as unresolved, and no claim depends on either.

- Notes: Added nine concepts from the wiki's second Jack Morris talk, eight months after the first: the structural argument that two of the three scaling axes are spent before a private-corpus project starts, leaving compute alone; the definitional claim that purchased post-training annotation is public by construction "because it's something that the model could tell to a user," which closes the last escape hatch without needing the contested data-exhaustion premise; near-zero training loss with collapsed generation as the wrong success signal, carrying two independent failures (bounded axis, and no generalization inside the bound); KV compaction's two limits (only reaches what fits in context, skips gradients) as the line between a serving technique and a learning one; on-policy distillation reframed as transferring in-context *behavior*, which is why raw documents are the wrong target and self-study generates Q/A pairs conditioned on the corpus; the three costs of continued pre-training including the base-model prerequisite most teams cannot meet; the synthetic data wall shared by all four families and the unanswered "what's this second stage of training look like?"; the AlphaGo property as a cheap design test for whether a learning loop compounds; and the aside that the corpus boundary is a modeling assumption rather than a fact. Strengthened eight existing pages: bounded the wiki's curated-synthetic-data recommendation without withdrawing it (the repair fixes generalization, not scaling) and added the same speaker's restatement of his own earlier memorization result with the new diagnostic that the metric peaks as behavior collapses; reframed the earlier context-versus-RAG-versus-weights page around which axis is available at all; recorded Terence Tao's breadth-versus-depth framing as an independent convergence on the intelligence/expertise split with a different cause assigned; gave the private-microworlds page a stronger argument that does not need public-data exhaustion; supplied the continual-learning taxonomy with the field's naming problem itemized from the same event plus Morris's own instantiation read through the four axes; separated a third distillation shape (no teacher until you build one) on the trace-distillation page; drew a scope boundary around coding-agent compaction so its results are not read as corpus acquisition; and added a fourth readiness item to the fine-tuning signals (a base checkpoint you can post-train). Provenance travels on every page: a framing talk explicitly "not… a super detail-oriented talk," by a researcher at a startup launched the week before and selling this problem's solution, closing on job postings, reporting **no results for any method including his own** — the single Engram datapoint is a plateau. Caption artifacts resolved on the source note ("N gram"/"Ngram" is Engram, "Meter" is METR, "archive" is arXiv, "GBT" is GPT, "open code" is OpenCode); flagged and depended on by nothing: "10K financial reports" (ten thousand reports or SEC 10-K filings), the loss value (captions 0.0001 versus the description's 0.00001), a first-name-only attribution of on-policy distillation to another speaker, and a skipped slide ("let's skip Andre").

- Notes: Fourth talk from the 2026-08-12 event to enter the wiki, and the first with a second talk from the same company already in it — [Applied Compute's December 2025 pipeline-RL talk](sources/20251209_o15AaYl7Wu0.md), which graded final answers. Reading them together is the highest-value edit of the iteration: the same vendor eight months later is designing around *not* having an answer to grade, and the "unified engine of putting inference and training together" that Denton calls the holy grail is exactly the zero-staleness limit of the asynchronous loop the earlier talk described, so the staleness page now carries both directions of the same quantity. Denton's 2x2 also subdivides a single cell of Yu Su's four-axis continual-learning taxonomy (experience/compression/structure/use), which is recorded on that page rather than duplicated. The two axes were the organizing choice: keeping trace policyness and hint provenance separate is what let the material split into a cheap corner (a trace dump plus a static prior, no environment), a middle rung the wiki did not previously have (regenerate exactly one step from the current policy against a stale prefix), and an online corner, instead of collapsing into one "continual learning" page. Three findings generalize past the vendor. Behavior can be installed from traces that never contain it, because the conditioning happens on deliberation rather than on the target tokens. Placement, not content, carried the formatting result — the identical instruction applied uniformly improved things only "a little bit," which is a clean natural experiment separating *what* the supervision says from *where* it lands, and the three nested localization filters (judge picks the step, distill a few steps forward, mask which tokens are learned from) all say the default distillation objective is far too broad for a targeted change. And catastrophic degradation looks at least partly like an aperture problem rather than a fundamental capability/behavior tradeoff, since the two approaches that could not narrow their aperture — a format reward and SFT on correct traces — were the two that degraded the base model. Provenance is recorded inline on every affected page, not only the source note: a vendor talk closing on a hiring address, all numbers from unpublished internal customer runs with no seeds, variance, or sample counts, the quadrant-3 claim delivered against a chart with no numbers spoken, both failed baselines reported without magnitudes, no cost accounting anywhere for the online loop, and the relevance-masking mechanism deferred to an uncited blog post. Caption artifacts resolved and recorded ("hardens" → harness, "sweet bench" → SWE-bench, and the model identification, where the captions say "Qwen 3.5 thinking model" but the video description says only "A Qwen thinking model" — the source note keeps the description's weaker form); two left explicitly unresolved ("climate overall emails" at 08:39, "loss reports" at 04:29) with no claim depending on either. Noted but deliberately not acted on, to keep the iteration to one video: the Engram source note's flagged first-name-only attribution of on-policy distillation resolves to "Ronak," likely a speaker from an unprocessed same-day video.

[Benchmark Context-Management Presets Against a Do-Nothing Baseline](concepts/benchmark-context-management-presets-against-a-do-nothing-baseline.md), [A Memory Harness Adds Only Cost When the Task Fits in Context](concepts/a-memory-harness-adds-only-cost-when-the-task-fits-in-context.md), [Judge Benchmark Quality by Task Quality, Diversity, Headroom, and Methodology](concepts/judge-benchmark-quality-by-task-diversity-headroom-and-methodology.md), [Reliability and Plasticity Conflict in Continually Learning Agents](concepts/reliability-and-plasticity-conflict-in-continually-learning-agents.md), [Define Continual Learning as Adaptive Compression of Experience](concepts/define-continual-learning-as-adaptive-compression-of-experience.md), [Do Not Gate Memory Use on the Agent's Own Judgment](concepts/do-not-gate-memory-use-on-the-agents-own-judgment.md), [Select State of the Art on a Quality-Efficiency Pareto Front](concepts/select-state-of-the-art-on-a-quality-efficiency-pareto-front.md), [Design Benchmarks as Forward Bets That Shape the Field](concepts/design-benchmarks-as-forward-bets-that-shape-the-field.md), [Do Not Treat Long Context as Durable Model Memory](concepts/do-not-treat-long-context-as-durable-model-memory.md) (strengthened)
- Notes: Fifth talk from the 2026-08-12 event to enter the wiki, and the one that supplies a measurement the previous four all needed. The durable item is not Continual Learning Bench 1.0 but **gain** — stateful reward minus stateless reward, computed by running each system through the benchmark twice, once carrying state and once reset between every instance. Two properties make it worth the doubled eval cost: it removes the confound that "total reward alone might confound continual learning ability with base model strength," and it is defined over any mechanism for carrying state ("it might mean that it's updating its notes. It might mean that it's just growing its context length"), which puts a notepad, a summarizer, a retriever, and a raw growing window on one axis. That directly answers two Open Questions this wiki was already carrying on [Evaluation](topics/evaluation.md) — the instrument for gain and the instrument for accumulated expertise — both of which were rewritten rather than left stale. The headline result is the third independent convergence on the same uncomfortable finding: plain in-context accumulation beat the memory and context-management systems on reward, on gain, and on both cost frontiers, after Towards AI's compaction bake-off and Druga's memory-harness ablation. Three teams, three benchmarks, no shared mechanism — and all three name the same variable as the one that should flip it, whether the material still fits, which is why the ICL page records Asawa's own caveat ("medium horizon tasks" that "didn't push the frontiers of the in-context learning systems enough") in his words rather than the wiki's. Deliberate non-merges: the stability/plasticity *diagnostic* was kept separate from the existing [reliability-and-plasticity page](concepts/reliability-and-plasticity-conflict-in-continually-learning-agents.md), because that page states an unresolved design tension while this one supplies two trace signatures you can grep for, and the two are cross-linked in both directions; Asawa's one-sentence definition ("sample efficient online learning that is stable over long horizons") was filed alongside Yu Su's adaptive-compression definition rather than replacing it, since they carve the same word differently. The plasticity example also caught a previously abstract failure in the act — a notepad agent writing "These seem to be cohort definitions from a different study schema that doesn't apply here" about a schema that did apply — which is the read-side twin of the memory-gating result already in the wiki, so [Do Not Gate Memory Use on the Agent's Own Judgment](concepts/do-not-gate-memory-use-on-the-agents-own-judgment.md) got the transcript quote rather than a new page. Held in tension rather than resolved: the sunk-cost argument says every system measured here is bolted onto a checkpoint "never designed to be [a] continual learner," while the leaderboard says the least invasive of those systems won — the pages record both and claim no reconciliation. Provenance is recorded inline on every affected page, not only the source note: an academic talk presenting the speaker's own benchmark, funded in part by a Snorkel AI open-benchmarks grant (Snorkel's own benchmarking talk is already a source here, so the funding is noted where the two are compared) and the [Laude] Institute slingshots program; **no numbers are spoken anywhere in the talk** — not the leaderboard, not gain values, not the cost axis — and the competing systems are never named, so the headline result is uncheckable and unattributable; the doubled evaluation cost of the two-run protocol goes unmentioned; parametric methods are only partially represented; expert validation is asserted without describing the process; and each failure mode rests on a single anecdote. Eight caption artifacts resolved and recorded ("Partasawa" → Parth Asawa, "Amy data sets"/"AM problems" → AIME, "per room" → headroom, "Parto"/"PTO Frontiers" → Pareto frontiers, "RHF" → RLHF, "multi-teer" → multi-teacher, "Udub" → UW, "know which is" → "now which is"); two left explicitly unresolved ("LOD institute", rendered as [Laude] Institute but unconfirmed, and "blind spectrum monitoring" as a domain name) with no claim depending on either.

- Notes: Sixth talk from the 2026-08-12 event, and the first in this batch that is about operating an agent rather than training one. The organizing move is a two-axis score — **ceiling** ("the craziest capability, emergent capability that your product or agent is capable of") versus **floor** ("what is the worst thing your agent can do?") — with the argument that published eval effort almost all measures the first while trust is lost on the second. What makes it more than a slogan is that the floor examples are product-shaped rather than catastrophic ("recommend a competitor," "delete a bunch of data," an "AI slop email to a customer because it like technically had access"), and two of the three follow from access the agent legitimately held, so they are product decisions rather than model defects. The floor is also not removable: agents that hit roadblocks "start getting really creative… that's what makes agents really powerful, but like that's also what makes them like catastrophic." Two same-speaker reconciliations were needed. Hylak appeared in the wiki thirteen months earlier recommending clustering, and now says "clusters are not issues"; this was recorded as a refinement — the clustered object moved from intent-annotated signals to raw traces — on both the old and the new page rather than as a reversal. Separately, the objection was reconciled with the wiki's promote-clusters-to-golden-dataset pipeline, which survives precisely because its human validation step converts a cluster into a named artifact with stable identity; the rule extracted is that clustering is a discovery pass whose output must be promoted into something you named before it can be tracked. Two topic pages outside the source note's original Topic Links (Tools, Infrastructure) were added during the reciprocal-link sweep because the code-mode-for-traces concept declares them, and the source note's Topic Links were extended to match. Provenance is on every affected page: a vendor talk delivered to an audience that included competitors, with no figures from Raindrop's book of business anywhere in it — the "80%" eval-invalidation number is an explicit estimate with no methodology, the anomaly-detection claim names no model and no task, no failure rate is offered for clustering, the market claims about prompt playgrounds are impressions from a self-selected customer base, and the talk was cut short with the Q&A dropped. Five caption artifacts were resolved (Century→Sentry, tenants→tenets, peak→peek, psychophancy→sycophancy, "400"→4o) and two left unresolved (a company rendered as "Speak," and unnamed prompt-playground vendors), with no claim depending on either.

- Notes: Seventh talk from the 2026-08-12 event, and the second independent account of hint-based on-policy self-distillation after Applied Compute's. Because the method itself was already documented, the split was made on what is genuinely new: a comparison rubric, a mechanism argument, and two failure modes with mitigations — with the shared technique recorded as corroboration on the existing pages rather than duplicated into new ones. The **four-property scorecard** (online task distribution, on-policy sampling, parallelism of one, per-token reward) is the most portable item, and its parallelism row is the one that translates into a budget: group rollouts are what force environments that are "one-to-one copies of the real world," so the environment fleet is a consequence of one algorithmic choice rather than a fixed cost of post-training. The mechanism argument is that a distillation loss matches the teacher's full next-token distribution rather than reweighting the sampled token — "we're not just taking now a distribution like RL and slightly sharpening it, but we're instead actually shifting entire distributions" — carrying a checkable corollary that tokens-to-solve collapses where RL inflates it, which is why the concept declares Inference and why that topic gained a paragraph, a Key Concept, an Open Question, and a Sources row this iteration (the source note's Topic Links were extended from four topics to five to match). The failure half is the durable contribution: over 50-100 tool calls the trained model's output concentrates on "wait," "but," "maybe," attributed to per-step teachers each nudging toward reconsideration until reconsideration is the policy; the mitigation weights each step's loss by the student/teacher KL divergence — the same quantity Applied Compute uses to cut a hard window around a judge-placed hint, used here as a continuous per-step weight, recorded on both pages as complementary with the observation that both "efficiency" measures are probably drift defenses. **Hint leakage** is named as the reward-hacking analogue, with a trace-recognizable signature ("It's 000. So, let me go back and put that into my reasoning trace") and two mitigations: an LLM rewriter that supplies signals rather than resolutions, and residual guidance blending a partial-hint teacher to measure and clamp the distribution shift. Q&A deflates the talk's own vision slide — the field is in "pseudo continual learning," batch updates and a re-upload, with merging 10,000 concurrent rollouts unsolved — and independently converges with Sara Hooker on joint model/harness optimization as "completely unexplored territory." Provenance is recorded inline on every affected page because concept pages are read standalone: a founder talk for a company selling this pipeline, with **no measured number anywhere in it** — "87 out of 100" is an essay analogy, "65k" is illustrative, both headline results are stated without values, the two failure modes rest on a word cloud and one rollout excerpt, hyperparameters are withheld for both mitigations, and the teacher forward passes that full-vocabulary matching requires are never priced against GRPO. Ten caption artifacts were resolved and recorded, two left unresolved; the transcript states both 12B and 120B for the scaled result, so no model size is cited for it anywhere in the wiki.

- Notes: First 2026-08-11 talk processed, and the first source in a while that is an infrastructure account rather than a training one. The concept split was made on what the wiki did not already have. Its harness-strategy pages ("thin it as the model improves," "the harness is the model-evolution boundary") were arguments; what was missing was a **worked instance with a cost on both sides**, which the context-anxiety story supplies — a compensating layer added for a real Sonnet 4.5 behavior that "became pure overhead, adding things like latency and causing issues with the cache being discarded incorrectly at times" once Opus 4.5 no longer needed it. The distinguishing observation recorded on the new page is that a stale workaround is not inert scaffolding: it keeps *executing*, so look for staleness wherever the harness intervenes on a threshold or schedule rather than merely offering a capability. **Decoupling the loop from the tool container** earned a separate page from the existing security-grounded split because Anthropic's three reasons are independent of trust — first-token blocking, shared blast radius, and eager container allocation — and two independent arguments landing on the same boundary is itself the finding; both pages now cross-reference, and the cost the talk never prices (a network round trip on every tool call thereafter) is recorded on the new one and as an Infrastructure open question. **Keeping the session log separate from the context window** is the highest-value item for Context Engineering because it names an assumption the topic had been working around without stating: where "the context window and the session are one in the same," every management operation is destructive, and the fix makes eviction a retrieval decision rather than a deletion — which is why a sliceable log answers questions a good summarizer cannot. The "one artifact serves observability, memory, and self-improvement" argument folded in there as a detail rather than becoming its own page. The two frontier features were recorded with their gaps as the substance: **dreaming** is filed as the *manage* phase of the wiki's existing write–manage–read loop moved offline (which is what buys cross-session visibility and standing to delete), with conflict resolution, rollback, cost, and evaluation all named as unaddressed; **outcomes** is filed under Evaluation because the artifact is a rubric and only the consumer changed, with the two missing bounds stated plainly — no iteration cap on "keep trying until it reaches that success criteria," and a retry loop whose exit condition is the judge is by construction optimizing against the judge. On Security, the credential page's contribution is the precondition rather than the practice: "the model never sees your security tokens" is achievable only because tool execution left the model's process, which makes a guarantee usually framed as discipline downstream of an architecture decision made for latency reasons; it is filed alongside the existing scope-exchange page as complementary (who can read the secret vs. what the secret can do). The environment egress allowlist was deliberately *not* given a page — it went onto the two existing network-layer pages, including on the wire-protocol page as the concrete instance of the control Ryan Dahl argues is insufficient, since allowed-hosts decides destination and not content. Likewise the "harnesses are the limiting factor" thesis strengthened the existing scarcity page rather than duplicating it, with the note that a model lab is the one making the argument. Provenance is recorded inline on every affected page because concept pages are read standalone: an Anthropic vendor talk for Claude managed agents, no competitor comparison and no pricing anywhere, and the two latency percentages ("60%" P50, "over 90%" P95) are the only numbers in it — no workload, sample size, absolute values, or description of the coupled baseline. Context anxiety is anecdotal with the resulting overhead unquantified; the demo is described by the presenters as "semi-interactive"; dreaming and outcomes are announced, not evaluated. Seven caption artifacts resolved (including the pervasive "Cloud" → Claude and "section management" → session management), two left unresolved — "Claude Tag" and "Claude Opus 48," where 4.5 vs 4.8 is illegible, so no page states a behavior claim tied to a specific release. Validation: 25,820 relative links resolve with zero broken; 377 processed-source rows with no duplicate IDs matching 377 log entries; the per-concept reciprocal-link sweep is clean for all nine new pages against all declared topics; the wiki diff is +167/-2, with both deletions being in-place extensions of existing caveat lines. - Notes: The second 2026-08-11 talk, and the first source in many iterations that is about running a team rather than building a system. The concept split was made on where the talk is structural rather than motivational. Its load-bearing argument is that agent adoption cannot be delegated to individuals because the two changes that move it — reorganizing the codebase for progressive disclosure and converging everyone onto one setup — are out of an IC's reach: "if you want to change the way your code base is organized, you can't do that as an IC." That earned its own page against the wiki's existing champions-and-AI-ready-repos and psychological-safety material, because those describe rollout mechanics while this names the authority the mechanics require. The review-burden page was kept separate from the existing aggregate review-capacity page on a distributional rather than a volume argument: the ten-PR engineers generate the diffs the one-PR engineers must read, so "not only can they not ship, they're going to actually see bad code and then curse the agents" — the aggregate can be within capacity while the load falls entirely on the people least able to convert it into leverage, and their skepticism then reads as attitude rather than queueing. Two diagnostics were recorded as instruments rather than observations: the fear × utilization map, whose value is the phase where fear *falls* because expectations fell ("I'm not really that scared cuz… it just ships slop"), so a fear survey alone cannot separate confidence from resignation; and the five-symptom checklist, whose sharpest entry — today's model "being really dumb" when "the model didn't change, right? The [harness] may have changed underneath" — was deliberately filed as the inverse of the wiki's existing stale-harness page (there the model moved and the harness did not; here the harness moved and the model did not), with both pages cross-linked. An eleventh planned page for that inversion was merged into the checklist rather than split, and the nightly code gardener was folded into two existing pages on the same grounds. The context-engineering half supplied the numbers this wiki's progressive-disclosure material has consistently lacked: a ~100-line `SKILL.md` cap (recorded as a mechanism — a stated cap makes each addition a trade against an existing line — rather than as a validated threshold), an `AGENTS.md` that is "a thin index," and first-prompt token burn as the verification (20–25K unavoidable, 40–50K "something's wrong"), which is a one-session check where an eval would be a project. Its retrieval counterpart generalizes past this talk: an agent's entry point is wherever its search matched, so a doc reachable only from a docs directory is unreachable in practice — with the unpriced cost, a rename that breaks the pointer with no test to fail, recorded on the page. The failure list contributed two pages: unscoped issue-filing rights as the internal-facing twin of the wiki's clusters-are-not-issues caution (both hand a detector's output straight to the artifact humans work from), and a declared exemption lane for prototype code, which is what keeps a self-healing loop from filing work items against code nobody intends to fix. Created a source note and 10 new concept pages, strengthened 13 existing pages, updated 4 topic pages with Overview paragraphs, Key Concepts bullets, Open Questions and Sources rows (Context Engineering has no Open Questions section and was left that way), inserted 21 concept-index entries across 4 groups, appended one processed-sources row, prepended one Current Emphasis bullet, and appended exactly one log entry. Provenance is recorded inline on every affected page because concept pages are read standalone: a ~17-minute practitioner talk about one team of about ten over a few months, with no before/after measurement of anything — the PR counts are illustrative, the context thresholds are rules of thumb with no derivation, the gardener and agentic reviews are reported with no figure for what they caught, and the `ship it` skill is described but never shown. The issue count is stated as 400–500 in the talk and "roughly 4,500" in the video description; the discrepancy is recorded inline wherever the number is used rather than resolved. The "500k… 750k [or a] million" figures are cumulative session spend against auto-compaction, not a window size, and are described that way. Nine caption artifacts resolved, including the pervasive "hardness" → harness and "gigantic reviews" → agentic reviews (confirmed against the description); two items left unresolved. Validation: 26,136 relative links resolve with zero broken; 378 processed-source rows with no duplicate IDs matching 378 log entries; the per-concept reciprocal-link sweep is clean for all 10 new pages against every declared topic; the wiki diff is +128/-0 across 21 tracked files plus 11 new ones, purely additive. No git commits; no background processes started; notes.md untouched. 247 videos remain unprocessed.

- Notes: The next unprocessed video by upload_date desc / video_id asc, read directly in five chunks of its 4,024-line WebVTT transcript with no extraction script. This is the most numerically specific infrastructure talk the wiki has taken in, and the concept split was made on which claims a reader can act on separately. The organizing cut is not "training versus inference" but **the absence of a global all-reduce**: back-propagation has collectives on every step and stays colocated, while a rollout serving island — a coherent endpoint serving one policy version, still permitted intra-island tensor parallelism and prefill/decode disaggregation — is the unit that can leave the datacenter. That leaves exactly one link to solve, and the talk's contribution is that the link is far cheaper than it looks. Two planned pages ("the checkpoint is the wrong unit" and "the bitwise-lossless patch") were **merged**, because each was thin alone and they are one argument: a ≈500 GB checkpoint takes "multiple minutes to hours" to move while async training wants seconds, so the sync object becomes changed positions plus replacement bits, applied as overrides or XOR so that "it's not a floating point addition. So there's no additive delta drift." Two pages were deliberately **kept apart**. [Adam absorption](concepts/adam-absorption-hides-most-weight-updates-from-the-served-model.md) is the mechanism — the visibility boundary is about half a ULP and scales with weight magnitude (θ/256 in BF16), while an Adam step is bounded at B×LR because the optimizer divides by running gradient statistics, giving ≈3 millionths at RL learning rates, so a flat push meets a sloping floor and large weights freeze. [A sparse served-weight delta is not gradient sparsity](concepts/a-sparse-served-weight-delta-is-not-gradient-sparsity.md) is the misconception Jiang stops on stage to correct, and it earns its own page because it licenses and forbids different optimizations: ~99% of parameters do receive non-zero gradients, the FP32 master update is dense, and LoRA is small by construction where this is small by absorption. [Lower serving precision shrinks the patch](concepts/lower-serving-precision-shrinks-the-weight-sync-patch.md) stayed separate because it inverts the usual reading of quantization and carries its own caveat about group-scaled formats. The bulletin board and the sidecar are likewise separate: one is a distribution model (immutable numbered safetensors versions pulled by engines with no RDMA to the trainer), the other an atomic operational pattern that adds version correctness beside a stock SGLang or vLLM server via three cases — proxy, apply the missing transitions, or answer "not ready." The sharpest integration is on [Distributable Compute Lowers the Barrier to Frontier Work](concepts/distributable-compute-lowers-the-barrier-to-frontier-work.md), where Sara Hooker's assertion that post-training compute distributes finally gets a mechanism **and** a qualification: only the sampling half distributes, the colocated core keeps pre-training-like requirements, and distributability is contingent on the optimizer rather than being a property of the workload. Two observations are recorded as explicitly derived rather than stated: that the push/floor margin makes absorption weakest at high learning rates (supported by the talk's own 0.15%-first-step versus 0.05%-steady-state split), and that a shared group scale can re-encode a whole group at once, so group-scaled formats may produce correlated bursts of changed positions. Provenance is on every page because concept pages are read standalone: a vendor talk whose load-bearing evidence (the Adam bound, ~99% bit-identical, ~99% dense gradients) is attributed to a paper the talk never names; the only first-party measurement is one internal GLM 4.7 Air FP8 run; there is no end-to-end result anywhere — no reward curve, wall-clock, or cost comparison against a colocated baseline; "extremely fast, in seconds" is derived from the 500 GB → 500 MB ratio, not measured; patch construction, storage and retention are unpriced; and Jiang names the scope limit himself, since everything assumes Adam while Moonshot and DeepSeek adopt Muon. No trust or threat model is offered for running rollouts on third-party capacity, and there was no Q&A. Twenty-six caption artifacts resolved, four left unresolved with no claim depending on any of them. Validation after this entry: 26,371 relative links resolve with zero broken; 379 processed-source rows with no duplicate IDs matching 379 log entries; the per-concept reciprocal-link sweep is clean for all 8 new pages against every declared topic; the wiki diff over tracked files is +137/-0, purely additive, alongside 9 new files (the source note and 8 concept pages).

- Notes: The next unprocessed video by upload_date desc / video_id asc, read directly in five chunks of its 4,752-line WebVTT transcript with no extraction script. The talk is a harness tour, so the risk was ten pages that only restate one vendor's implementation; the split was made on which claims survive being lifted out of Codex. The organizing frame recorded on the source note is that two protocols bracket the harness — the app server between UI and harness, the responses API between harness and inference — and the second of those turns out to be where several pages point. The single most load-bearing finding is that harness behavior can be **trained**: apply patch is "a format that we trained the model on starting with GPT-5," ripgrep ships in the binary "because we know the model has been trained on using ripgrep," PowerShell likewise, and auto compaction runs server-side "in a way that the model got trained with." That was kept as one page rather than four because the corollary is what matters and it is shared: a capability that depends on training is not portable to another model behind the same harness, and once the provider moves it server-side it is not yours to modify. It is also the sharpest qualification this wiki's harness-as-stable-boundary and thin-the-harness-as-the-model-improves pages have received — the thinning is real, but the thinned-away logic reappears behind an API on the provider's schedule rather than becoming nothing, which is now recorded on both pages. The context half answers one problem twice and the split is on visibility: tool definitions need not be resident, so they are deferred and retrieved by a model-called tool search; a skills list must be resident or nothing triggers, so it gets a hard budget of 2% of the maximum context window with descriptions trimmed as the catalog grows. Those stayed as two pages because a reader can adopt either without the other, and both were cross-written against the wiki's existing retrieval-based tool routing, where the genuine fork is *who* selects — a retriever running ahead of the model on the first message, or the model mid-trajectory at the cost of a round trip. Neither side has published an accuracy comparison, and that is now an open question on Tools. The safety pair was deliberately kept apart on the mechanism-versus-rubric line this wiki has used before: the review subagent is the mechanism (spawned separately, cannot spawn others, read-only, fed the pending command plus the transcript), while authorization explicitness is the reusable criterion, and only the second transfers to a product with no subagents. What makes the pair worth recording at all is the premise — approval fatigue is reported as observed rather than theorized ("we saw people would just start clicking yes"; "people started to just use full access mode more"), which reframes a per-action human gate as a control that degrades with use, and that is now the qualifying bullet on the human-approval-gates page. The unresolved risk is recorded on both new pages and as a Security open question: the reviewer's evidence includes the conversation transcript, which is the prompt-injection surface, and no false-approval rate exists. Two pages were kept despite thin evidence because the *observation* is structural rather than empirical: the network becoming an agent's bottleneck at 1,000 tokens per second (the resend cost is superlinear in trajectory length under a stateless protocol, which is why agents hit it before chat does), and per-OS sandbox primitives, which is the first source in the wiki to qualify "do not roll your own sandbox" with a case where the team had to — the scope note recorded is that the existing advice presumes execution can leave the user's machine. One planned eleventh page was dropped: the high-agency-overshoot failure mode was folded into the existing floor-raising page instead, where it adds what that page lacked — a named prompt that produces the floor ("push the model through prompting to really have high agency"), from a lab rather than a vendor selling floor detection. Provenance is recorded inline on every affected page because concept pages are read standalone. Almost nothing in this talk is measured: the WebSocket result is "speeds up things quite significantly" with no latency figure, workload, or baseline, and its only concrete comparison is nine payload items versus one; the live demo of that feature crashed on stage and was replaced with a pre-recorded backup; the 2% cap is a chosen constant with no derivation; auto review is self-described as "a gross oversimplification" with no error rates and no adversarial test; the Windows sandbox rationale is deferred to an external article; and the whole talk is version-bound by the speaker's own opening disclaimer that "this is a current state of affairs." There was no Q&A. Nine caption artifacts resolved (including "cashability" → cachability, "service-side events" → server-sent events, "fiable" → verifiable, and "MIT it's Apache 2" → Apache 2); two left unresolved — the spellings of the two named third-party app-server consumers, and one garbled adverb — with no claim depending on either. Validation after this entry: 26,756 relative links resolve with zero broken; 380 processed-source rows with no duplicate IDs matching 380 log entries; the per-concept reciprocal-link sweep is clean for all 10 new pages against every declared topic, and every list-item link matches its target's H1 (three mismatches were caught and fixed mid-iteration); the wiki diff over tracked files is +142/-0 across 26 files, purely additive, alongside 11 new files (the source note and 10 concept pages). No git commits; no background processes started; notes.md untouched. 245 videos remain unprocessed.

- Notes: The next unprocessed video by upload_date desc / video_id asc, read directly in five chunks of its 5,704-line WebVTT transcript with no extraction script. This is the wiki's second Sonar source, so the first decision was what is genuinely additive rather than a restatement of the May 2026 talk, whose ACDC loop and LLM leaderboard are already recorded. Five things survived that test. The Carnegie Mellon longitudinal result is the only evidence anywhere in this wiki that puts a productivity metric and a quality metric on the same projects over the same period — "a temporary spike in productivity… it lasted about 3 months and then it went back down" against "a persistent increase in static analysis warnings and code complexity" that "persisted well into the future" — which is why it became a page rather than a bullet: the two halves decay at different rates, so reading either alone gives the opposite conclusion depending on when you look. Zero trust was split from the wiki's existing separate-prompt and separate-provider pages on the method-class line, because "use a different methodology to review the code that was used to write the code" is a strictly stronger claim than varying the model, and because the payoff named is provability rather than detection rate — a property an LLM reviewer structurally cannot offer, which is what makes the layer usable as compliance evidence. Defect-class layering was kept apart from the wiki's hierarchical-verification page on the grounds that ordering layers by signal strength and choosing them by class of defect reached are independent questions; a strong signal about the wrong class is still a gap. The in-loop page exists because the argument changed: the earlier Sonar talk moved checks left on latency grounds (1-5 seconds pre-commit versus 1-5 minutes in CI), and this one moves them on propagation grounds — defects "fixed immediately by the agent so they don't propagate into future agentic loops" — which is the argument that survives if CI ever becomes fast. Per-dimension tier routing earned a page because it inverts a decision teams already make silently: the Claude tier toggle operated to control token burn is trading maintainability, security, and complexity against correctness, not buying less of one ladder. A sixth planned page on the autonomy trust ramp was dropped as too thin and folded into the zero-trust page plus the existing human-gate page. Two conflicts were recorded rather than resolved. This talk contradicts the earlier Sonar talk on whole-codebase context — "you can't just throw your entire code base at the agent up front" against "Sonar Context Augmentation pushes the entire codebase into the LLM as context" — with the later, narrower position taken as current and the change noted as unacknowledged by either talk. And the Wharton study is now cited twice in the wiki in different units: "80% of participants" via Duolingo (a share of people) against "nearly 80% of the time" here (a share of instances), which is recorded on the source note, on the automation-bias page, and as an Evaluation open question, because the two readings support different corrections. Provenance is carried inline on every affected page because concept pages are read standalone: a product marketer's vendor talk in which nothing about Sonar's own loop is measured — no defect-rate baseline, no false-positive rate for either layer, no latency or cost for in-loop verification — where both cited studies lack a link and a sample size, a results slide was skipped for time, the demo was pre-recorded, and the closing "standardize on a single… platform" sits against the diversity argument the talk had just made. The sharpest tension is internal and is recorded as an open question on two topic pages: the product offered as the independent zero-trust check can also "write fixes and… approve those fixes and merge those PRs completely automatically," which retires the independence for exactly the changes it touches, and the ramp to that state is "as you use it more and more and gain confidence" — elapsed usage, not a criterion. Eight caption artifacts resolved (SonarQube, Gitar, Antigravity among them), two left unresolved, neither load-bearing. - Notes: The next unprocessed video by upload_date desc / video_id asc, read directly in five chunks of its 5,120-line WebVTT transcript with no extraction script. This is the first source in a long run that is about the *direction* of a fast team rather than the quality of its output, and the split test was whether each idea survives being lifted out of a founder's pitch for his own product category. Five did. Velocity sickness became a page rather than a bullet because its four symptoms are disjoint from the wiki's existing broken-setup checklist — that page reads an individual's misconfigured agent from babysitting, context burn, and slop, while this one reads a team from a merge queue that breaks, work sprinting in incompatible directions, agent bankruptcy each morning, and decisions made by agents — and because three of the four are invisible to any shipping metric, which is the whole argument. The ownership page was kept apart from the wiki's PR-routing page on a boundary that matters: routing decides whose name is on a change, and this decides who chose the approach. "If you as an engineer are letting an agent make a critical decision, you are [ceding] control of your code… you are no longer the owner of that code" is a loss that correct routing does not prevent and that reading every line does not undo, because the transfer already happened at a "this is the recommended option" prompt — the same in-session gate collapse OpenAI reports as approval fatigue and Duolingo as automation bias, now with a third independent observer and a different remedy, which is why it was cross-written onto both of those pages instead of restating them. The decision-layer page earned its own entry over the wiki's plan-first material because the claim is not "plan more" but that the deciding half has inverted requirements — "creative and collaborative" against "individual" and "heads down" — so the IDE is the wrong instrument for it, chat is worse ("a brain off medium… built for execution"), and plan mode and spec-driven development are ruled out by name as, respectively, a chat that slides into implementation and a factory that presumes the decision is already made. Its most usable part is the smallest: drift from planning into polish inside one session is a check you can run today without adopting anything. Doc-as-state was recorded with its conflict intact rather than resolved — it directly opposes Jain's position that the session transcript is the intent record because the decisive moments happen mid-implementation, and both pages now say so — and its unpaid bill was written onto the doc-rot page, since a living state document is meant to be read as current and therefore rots more expensively than a completed PRD, with no owner, versioning rule, or retirement policy offered anywhere in the talk. Unimplemented plans earned a page because the signal inverts: plans written and never built are reported as evidence the layer works, with prototype gravity ("once you have a prototype, it's really hard to throw that away") as the force it escapes — which sits against the wiki's existing argument that parallel prototypes are how design choices become measurements, a tension now recorded on both. Three planned pages were dropped and folded into existing ones instead: the "recommended option" collapse into the automation-bias and Codex review-subagent pages, "hand the plan to a teammate before the agent" into the collaborative-plans page, and "move the review point earlier" into the plan-review and code-review-alignment pages, where it supplies a second, alignment-based reason for a conclusion the wiki had reached only from throughput. Provenance is carried inline on every affected page because concept pages are read standalone: a ~20-minute founder's talk for Ref, a product in exactly the category being argued for, with no Q&A and no demo; nothing measured anywhere — no before/after on any symptom, no team size, no adoption data; the one supporting anecdote is second-hand and unnamed; "agent bankruptcy is just not a thing" once state lives in a doc is asserted without support; and no model, harness, or protocol is named. Four caption artifacts resolved, two left unresolved, neither load-bearing. Six lessons from a team operating at "99.9% of our PRs are heavily agent generated" and "everything's human reviewed," which made the main risk a source note of vendor claims plus six thin pages; the split was made on which ideas are distinct from the nearest existing page rather than on how the talk is organized. Session reachability was kept apart from the wiki's shared-cloud-workspace page on the addressing/environment line, because the objection is that a shared runtime still leaves one place you have to go look — "a lot of work happened in Slack, so that's better than nothing, but certainly not all work happens in Slack" — and its payoff is one the environment page does not produce: a reviewer interrogating the session instead of waiting for the author ("I don't need to wait for Sergey… the answer to the question is almost certainly in this thread"), which is why the intent-record page was cross-written rather than restated. The ambient-credential page exists because the wiki's sandbox material covers authority you *hand* an agent, and this is the case where it was handed nothing: "it finds a token on your laptop that it can use and it thinks it's working with staging, but actually it's production." Every step there is the agent doing its job, so no approval prompt catches it and no per-tool scope covers it — which is also the argument that separates it from the two egress pages it strengthens. Environment isolation earned its own page over the wiki's natural-work-artifacts page on the substrate/steering split: the artifact page says how a non-engineer expresses a change, and this says why expressing it produced nothing until the codebase ran somewhere. Unfiled conversation was kept separate from the observability-to-PR page because the inputs differ in kind — machine signals arrive with structure, a timestamp, and an implicit claim that something is wrong, while human speech has none of those — so the hard step moves from grouping and weighting to noticing that a remark was a product idea, and the dedup rule ("if it finds existing work, it'll link to it") does the job grouping does there. PR replay was split from the wiki's adapt-an-open-harness page because it skips the harness entirely: the task corpus already exists as merged pull requests, and the objection to public boards is subject matter rather than aggregation — "SWE-bench is all in Python, we're Ruby on Rails" — which is a fourth failure mode alongside the leaderboard page's existing three. Three planned pages were dropped and folded in as strengthening bullets: model/harness agnosticism with the model-withdrawal risk (the Fable anecdote) and the token-seller incentive argument onto the stable-harness page, the open-weight quality/latency reading onto the invest-in-the-harness page, and the operating-cost figures onto the eval-infrastructure page. Two source-internal discrepancies were recorded rather than resolved: "1 and 1/2 billion tokens over the past month" against the video description's 10.5 billion, and "3,300 Claude code runs that cost $10,000 in tokens daily" inside a paragraph otherwise framed monthly. Provenance is carried inline on every affected page because concept pages are read standalone: a ~19-minute vendor talk with no Q&A whose live demo failed on conference Wi-Fi; the benchmark plots are the strongest artifact and the least reproducible from the talk, with no task count, scoring rubric, definition of "quality," axis units, error bars, or harness versions, and no account of how a replayed attempt is judged against the original PR; the security argument rests on one hypothetical with an explicitly weak frequency claim ("I'm not trying to say this is happening constantly, but it still happens"); the egress sandbox prompts on each new destination, which is the interactive-approval pattern the same talk rejects two minutes earlier; the remedy inverts the trust boundary to the vendor with no threat model for third-party capacity; the meeting-bot yield has no denominator and no review-cost accounting; and multiplayer contention — two people steering one session from different surfaces — is never addressed, which became a Coding Agents open question. Seven caption artifacts resolved, two left unresolved, neither load-bearing. One invented detail was caught and removed during self-review: an LLM judge was attributed to their benchmark, which the talk never describes. This is the wiki's first Resolve AI source and its first talk arguing that the AI coding wave's next constraint is operational rather than editorial, so the concept split was made on which claims survive being lifted out of one vendor's product tour. Six did. The execution/production-context page was kept as the anchor because it is the only formulation in this wiki that separates *reaching* a signal from *judging* it — "the execution is can load the dashboard. It's the production context that's going to say, this feels wrong" — and because what the second half returns is a priority, not a fact, which is what the wiki's agent-readable-observability material assumes rather than supplies. It was cross-written onto the last-mile-domain-context page with the difference stated: that page's context converges under an expert loop, while an environment "evolving faster and faster" decays continuously, so the artifact is a maintained system rather than a corpus. The trigger page was kept apart from the wiki's unfiled-conversation page on a line that matters for the remedy: there the work was never noticed, here everyone knows about the work and nothing fires for it — a page has a receiver, an incident has a bridge, and a returning P99 drift has neither, deliberately, "because we're not going to alert on everything." Deployment monitoring was split into two pages rather than one because the source names two independent CI/CD failures: thin coverage on gated changes (the derived check plan, which reads the diff, walks the causal chain to the Kafka pipeline downstream of the changed service, and picks its own recheck interval up to three days) and *no* coverage on ungated ones (feature flags and infra changes, whose current detector is an alert waking an on-caller who asks "who changed what?"). Fixing a pipeline's assertions does nothing for the second, which is why they are not one page. The confidence-gate page earned an entry over the wiki's proactive-agent material because it adds a failure mode that framing does not have — not interrupting at all — and a control the wiki had not recorded: an agent that declines, with a private DM confirmation for the middle band so a wrong guess costs one DM instead of a public wrong answer. The task-amendment page was kept separate from the thread-to-task-ID page because that one continues a run and this one edits a persistent specification: "the agent is able to update its task underneath… so that the next time it fires, it's not going to be as verbose." One genuine disagreement was recorded on both sides rather than resolved: Smith's "surface area being where you live" (Slack or MS Teams) is the trapped-in-one-product position Singh's session-addressability page argues against, and the likely reason — operational reports originate in chat while coding sessions must touch GitHub and an editor — is stated on the Superconductor page. A second tension was written onto the staged-proactivity page: that page ramps from local code maintenance to production consequences, while this source starts at deployment monitoring, which suggests consequence awareness and autonomy are separable axes since reading production is safe long before acting on it. Provenance is carried inline on every affected page because concept pages are read standalone: a ~25-minute vendor talk with no Q&A; nothing measured anywhere (no deploy count, catch rate, false-positive rate, baseline, cost for open-ended re-checking, or customer figure); an explicitly "fake environment" demo whose amendment segment never completed on stage ("no time to watch this actually go, but this works") and whose final section was skipped for time; a 70% premise attributed only as "this was a survey study done"; a learning system named as the hard part four times with no storage, retrieval, staleness, or evaluation description; a confidence gate with no threshold, calibration, or error rate; no security, access-control, retention, or prompt-injection discussion at all despite agents reading critical Slack channels, holding DM access, and triggering on GitHub tags and messages anyone can post; and no model, harness, or provider named. Three new Open Questions were filed on Agents (what is behind a confidence gate, who bounds a self-scheduling agent, what the trust boundary is when the trigger is a public message), two on Workflows (when a standing job stops, whether reply-to-amend needs a permission model), and two on Infrastructure (what a derived check actually catches, what continuous change-watching costs). Five caption artifacts resolved, two left unresolved and neither load-bearing. Created a source note and 6 new concept pages, strengthened 19 existing pages, updated 4 topic pages, inserted 16 concept-index entries across 4 groups, appended one processed-sources row, prepended one Current Emphasis bullet, appended exactly one log entry.

This is a retrospective practitioner talk rather than a product tour, so the concept split was made on which ideas are distinct from the nearest existing page and survive being lifted out of one company's refactor story. Five did. The refactor-timing page is the anchor because the wiki has plenty of material on *how* to run a large migration and nothing on whether to run it now, and because Linkov argues the deferral case honestly before rejecting it — "taking on technical debt and refactoring later is getting exponentially easier as the days go by" is the same capability trend his own re-run benchmark measures. What defeats it is not a forecast but an accrual: AI-native development manufactures illegible code faster than the discount arrives, and illegible is the specific property that makes code expensive to change on a customer's timeline, which is why that quote was cross-written onto the human-understanding page as a schedule problem rather than only a risk one. The N-of-1 re-run page was kept apart from the wiki's PR-replay page on the measurement unit: this one counts human intervention — hours of back-and-forth, corrective iterations, major mistakes caught — which dissolves the scorer problem the replay page names as unsolved, because the person grading personally solved the task. Both pages now say the two instruments answer different questions, one cross-sectional to move a team default and one longitudinal to update an individual's operating model. The task-length-curve page exists because the argument is about which percentile of someone else's published fit to quote, and the payoff is decision-theoretic rather than empirical: 50% is adequate on a benchmark and a coin flip in a workday, since a failed unattended run costs the wall-clock and the attention block. Its caveat is recorded on the page and raised as an Evaluation open question — a fitted curve is least stable in exactly the tail he prefers, and nobody publishes intervals there. The multi-repo page was created rather than folded into the existing Jellyfish page because it qualifies it in a way that changes the remedy: same drag observed a model generation later, different mechanism named (verification and sandbox provisioning rather than cross-repo context), and documentation, indexes, and retrieval fix one and do nothing for the other. That fork is now recorded on both pages and as a Coding Agents open question. "AI psychosis" and the GPT 5.5 zero-shot failure were merged into one page instead of two thin ones because they are the same failure at different stages — a fluent artifact asserting completion, a cheap size sanity check ("it only wrote 2,000 lines of code, which was a little bit fishy"), and a confession nobody read. Two planned pages were dropped and folded in as strengthening bullets: PR review as context distribution onto the review-alignment page, and developers volunteering into the clean repo onto the codebase-hygiene page. The provenance limit is stated inline on the refactor-timing page and on both affected topic pages: the talk argues AI-era teams ship faster with degrading quality, then measures its own rebuild only in velocity, reach, and cost — no defect rate, incident rate, or static-analysis figure — and never prices the six months in engineer-months, tokens, or unshipped work, which is the denominator another team would need. Validation: 27,897 relative links resolve with zero broken; 385 processed-source rows with no duplicate IDs matching 385 log entries; reciprocal-link sweep clean for all 5 new pages against every declared topic and index group, with 12 index entries landing in the 6 correct groups; of the 32 unique concept links added anywhere in the diff, 28 match their targets' H1s exactly and the remaining 4 are inline prose links inside the index.md emphasis bullet, lowercased mid-sentence per that section's existing convention; the tracked wiki diff is +125/-0 across 28 files, purely additive, alongside 6 new files. 240 videos remain unprocessed.
