- Notes: The next unprocessed video by upload_date desc / video_id asc, read directly in six chunks of its 4,064-line WebVTT transcript with no extraction script. This source is unlike anything else in the wiki: not a practitioner report, a product tour, or a research result, but a professor reading a vendor's certification blueprint and treating the exam weights as evidence about how the harness fails in production. That made provenance the first editorial decision rather than the last — the inference "Anthropic knows how people are using their system and what the issues are going to be" is the load-bearing claim behind every recommendation here, Anthropic publishes no weighting methodology, and nothing in the talk is measured, so the limit is stated inline on all four new pages, on all six topic pages, and raised as a Coding Agents open question that names the alternative reading (a blueprint may reflect what is convenient to test). Four pages earned entries out of a larger candidate list. The `stop_reason` page is the anchor because it is a control-flow correction, not advice: the wiki's existing agent-loop page describes a loop that runs "until the model stops requesting tools" without ever saying what field carries that signal, and the third branch — `max_tokens`, a response cut mid-sentence that is syntactically indistinguishable from a short finished one — is a failure no success-rate metric in this wiki would surface, since the harness parses a fragment as an answer. Böhm–Jacopini (1966) was folded into that same page rather than given its own, because "the agent loop is `while` with the model supplying the branch condition" is a framing of the same mechanism. The critic-redaction page was kept apart from the wiki's separate-context, separate-prompt-or-provider, and separate-method-class pages on the axis it varies: hold the model, prompt, and vendor fixed and change only what the reviewer is shown. That makes it the fourth independence axis and the cheapest, and it produces a direct conflict with harnesses whose review subagent is fed the parent transcript by design — recorded on both sides and reconciled editorially, not by either source, as authorization review (needs the transcript to judge what the user asked for) versus correctness review (compromised by it). The unmeasured cost is now an Evaluation open question: a critic denied the chain of thought also cannot locate where a derivation went wrong, and nobody has run both configurations on the same defects. The double-bound context page exists because forking and compaction are usually argued separately here, and the argument for pairing them is that they catch different traffic — forking cannot stop the parent's own conversation from growing, and compaction cannot refund tokens a subtask already spent in the shared window; the threshold itself is unnamed, which leaves it in tension with the wiki's prompt-caching break-even material, now an Agents open question. The certification-blueprint page is the meta-move and was written as a method with its limits attached, since its usable output is a checklist shape — scenario plus named wrong answer — that most enablement advice lacks. Six candidate pages were dropped and folded in as strengthening bullets instead: batch-mode latency arbitrage onto the token-spend page (the only cost lever in the wiki that trades wall-clock rather than quality), non-interactive CI onto the permission-wrapper page (a wrapper cuts prompt count in an attended session; a pipeline needs the prompt path gone), the one-or-two-tool subagent bound onto the tool-set and subagent-customization pages, hierarchical `CLAUDE.md` onto the repository-instructions page as the structural counterpart to the thin-index discipline, the million-token caution onto the small-context page, and the blueprint-as-syllabus reading onto the team-upskilling and champions pages. Caption artifacts were resolved in the source note where the intent is unambiguous ("Cloud Code" → Claude Code, "three levels of Claude" → `CLAUDE.md`, "Boris Cherney" → Boris Cherny, "contact management" → context management) and left unresolved where it is not: two proper nouns that could not be verified and the ambiguous scoping of the first two `CLAUDE.md` levels, which the repository-instructions page now flags as something to check against current documentation rather than take from this source. "Open Claw" was checked and left alone as a real project name. The dominant editorial risk was duplication rather than thinness: this is the *second* GitHub Next talk in the wiki about the ACE prototype, after Maggie Appleton's, so every substrate claim — micro-VMs, session-as-branch, shared terminals and previews, co-edited plans — had to become a strengthening bullet on the existing pages rather than a new one, and only the idea Appleton's talk does not contain earned its own page. That idea is the residue rule, stated as a subtraction: "anything that's in code, any fact that's in code, the agents can figure out by reading the code. What's left are the things that are not in code." It is filed apart from the wiki's align-before-implementing material because that page says *where* shared context should live and this says *what* is worth putting there, and it was written with its own expiry noted — the residue shrinks as retrieval improves, which puts it in direct tension with instruction-file practice that restates architecture the agent could read for itself, and with this wiki's own retrieval sources reporting agents missing cross-repo context. That tension is now a Coding Agents open question rather than a resolved position. The anchor page is the output-side bound, and it is the anchor because every existing control in the wiki bounds what an agent may *reach* while this bounds what a run may *produce* — by kind, by count ("Pull request single… because I don't want the agent to get prompt injected to create 500 pull requests"), and with silence made a legal terminal state ("I explicitly said you're allowed to do nothing… I don't want the agents denial of servicing me"). The emit-nothing half is what makes it more than a rate limit: it closes an availability hole no page here had named, since an automation with no permission to stay quiet will manufacture output to justify its run. Its unexamined edge — whether an output cap means anything while the agent still holds write-capable tools, and what happens on a breach — is recorded as a Security open question. The Markdown-as-source page was kept separate from the wiki's two neighbouring Markdown-substrate patterns on lifecycle: repo-local task files hold work items that are created and archived, this holds a standing job that fires repeatedly, and the analytics-widget page makes the opposite bet about which artifact is canonical (there the deterministic output is kept and edited; here it is regenerated and never read). Its cost is stated on the page and raised as a Workflows open question — "you never look at it" means the reviewable surface is a diff of English plus trust in a non-deterministic compiler, which sits badly against this wiki's argument that the enforceable layer should be hand-written and unit-tested. The judgment-versus-heuristics page earned a slot over the existing maintenance-ROI page because it supplies the per-task test rather than the category, and because it comes with a cheap candidate-finder (the reflex you are embarrassed by: rerunning a red CI job) and an honest missing half, since Home Assistant's workflow closes real users' bug reports and no accuracy figure is given for it anywhere. Two planned pages — "declare guardrails in front matter" and "keep secrets outside the agent's jail" — were dropped after reading the existing must-not-fail-decisions and credential-decryption pages, which already cover both; they became strengthening bullets instead, the first as a fourth independent source and a fourth enforcement position. The dominant editorial risk here was provenance rather than duplication: this is a founder keynote delivered three days after the speaker's company launched an open-weights subscription, and every claim in it — the subsidy figures, the commoditization thesis, the harness position, the model head-to-head — points at that product. So the limits were written inline on all four new pages, on all seven topic paragraphs, and in a dedicated `Provenance and Limits` section of the source note, rather than being left for the reader to infer. The anchor page is the LiteLLM compromise, and it earned the slot because of where the blast radius landed rather than how the intrusion worked. Self-hosting an LLM gateway is the recommended posture across this wiki's infrastructure and cost material — it is how teams centralize keys, meter spend, and avoid a hosted router — and that is exactly what made the victim population dense in credentials: "enterprise customers and developers that have their own internal gateways." Centralizing keys to control them and centralizing keys to lose them are the same act. The detection path is the part worth remembering: the malware was found because it crashed Cursor when someone ran the LiteLLM MCP server, so a well-written payload in the same position leaves nothing in this wiki that would have caught it, which is now a Security open question. The lock-in page was kept apart from the wiki's existing token-cost material on what it measures — those pages price usage, this prices the *gap* between the price you pay and the price the usage would cost, and argues the exposure accrues in harness configuration, rules files, and habits rather than in the invoice. Its instrument is the cheapest one recorded here: during a provider outage, ask a room how many people cannot work. The commoditization page was filed under Product Strategy and Models rather than under the wiki's open-weights adoption pages because those all argue from the adopter's side and this argues from the releaser's, and because the interesting content is the applicability test rather than the conclusion — publishing a layer is a good trade only when you do not intend to win on it and do intend to win downstream of it. Its weakest joint is stated on the page and raised as a Product Strategy open question: Open Compute published designs for hardware Facebook bought, while weights are closer to what a lab sells, and no source here supplies a test for that boundary. The contribution-channel page was kept separate from the wiki's existing effort-filter and maintainer-triage pages on the shape of the argument: those describe filters, this describes the escalation those filters sit on and names its terminal state, with the apprenticeship path as the cost and no replacement proposed. Two planned pages were dropped after reading their nearest neighbours — a "step count is not efficiency" page, because the existing agent-loop evaluation page already carries the Gemini case and the GLM/Opus inversion fits better as a bullet there, and a "system over model" page, because the verification-guardrails page covers it. Three tensions were recorded rather than resolved: whether distillation from released weights can ever surpass its source, whether models differ systematically in self-verification effort (one bug, one run per model), and how much of the maintainer flood is actually agent-generated, since no source here attributes it. The editorial risk here was that a seventeen-minute talk with no numbers in it can be lifted into wiki voice as fact, so the unmeasured status is written inline on all four new pages, in all four topic paragraphs, and in a dedicated Provenance and Limits section of the source note. The anchor page earns its slot by decomposing a claim this wiki repeats without examining: that background coding agents are the template for background agents generally. Shenoy separates the two properties that make the code case work and shows that neither is about agents — the sandbox-and-PR loop is a *forking substrate*, and the willingness to accept job seven before job three is a *professional habit*, contrasted against the inbox cleared one email at a time. Read that way, the wiki's worktree, VM-queue, swim-lane, and agent-manager pages become a portability checklist ("what plays the role of the branch here, and is anyone downstream already working out of order?") rather than designs to port, which is why the bullet was written onto five of them rather than one. His own sidestep — represent knowledge work as code so it inherits diffs, branches, and review — was folded into the output-medium page rather than given a page, because he offers no mechanism, example, or result for it. The eval page was kept apart from the existing outcome-verifier page on who writes the verifier: that page argues for testing outcomes instead of paths, while this one describes a setting where nobody writes the test at all because a physical event settles it, and the price is a one-bit label arriving weeks after the decision it grades, which is now an Evaluation open question. The implicit-feedback signal (the diff between what the agent produced and what was actually submitted) was folded into the existing implicit-edit-feedback page as a generalization beyond code rather than split off. The enablement page was kept separate from this wiki's dense adoption material on direction of causation: those pages ask how to get adoption, this one says adoption is the input the improvement loop consumes, so a continual-learning roadmap in a low-usage deployment is starved by construction — which is a qualification of the trace-substrate argument and was written onto that page. The co-design page was separated from the embed-in-existing-surfaces page on what it governs: that page says where the tool lives, this one says where the requirements come from and why a remote channel filters them, since a ticket is written by someone who already framed their problem as a request. One real disagreement was recorded rather than smoothed: Klein locates the diffusion blocker in computer-use reliability, an engineering problem; Shenoy locates it in retraining and process replacement, which no model improvement removes, and both sides are now stated on the form-filling page. Two candidates were dropped as pages after reading their neighbours — the five-rung autonomy ladder, which the existing autonomy-slider and delegation-maturity pages already carry, and "the exceptions are the job," which fits as the sharpest available statement of the last-mile argument. Three tensions were recorded rather than resolved: what plays the role of the branch outside software, how to combine a slow ground-truth label with a fast proxy, and whether in-person co-design beats a disciplined remote process or only a passive one. Created a source note and 4 new concept pages, strengthened 25 existing pages, updated 4 topic pages, inserted 9 concept-index entries across 4 groups, appended one processed-sources row, prepended one Current Emphasis bullet, appended exactly one log entry. No git commits; no background processes; notes.md untouched.

- Notes: The next unprocessed video by upload_date desc / video_id asc, read directly in six chunks of its 3,144-line WebVTT transcript with no extraction script. This source lands in the densest neighbourhood in the wiki — there are already a dozen pages on review capacity, review gates, and turning review lessons into guardrails — so the editorial question was not what the talk says about code review but which of its claims those pages cannot already answer. Four survived. The anchor is the audience inversion, which is the only place in this wiki where the two failure modes of an automated reviewer are priced separately per consumer: a wrong comment is cheap for a human who ignores it and expensive for an agent that obeys it and reworks backwards, while a nit is the reverse. That flips the existing false-positive page's mechanism rather than repeating it — alert fatigue is self-limiting because a person stops reading, and obedient rework is not — and it yields a per-surface filtering policy instead of one confidence bar, which is written onto both pages. The measurement page earns its slot on a coverage argument the wiki's other implicit-feedback pages do not make: at 25,000 comments a week only 10% draw any response, so anything requiring a person to answer is measured on a self-selected tenth, while whether the code changed is computable for all of it; the compliance-versus-correctness limit is stated on the page and raised as an Evaluation open question, since a developer under review-capacity pressure may comply with a wrong comment for exactly the reasons this wiki documents elsewhere. The rule-authoring page was kept apart from the existing comment-mining page on which half is expensive: that page says the corpus of past reviews already contains the rules, and Uber confirms the mining takes minutes, which makes the durable content the four platform obligations that follow — ownership binding, co-location, deterministic routing, and returning per-rule telemetry to the author so a distributed rule library has any garbage collection at all. The pipeline page was written because comment volume is the complaint everyone has about AI reviewers and no page here located it correctly: with several generators over one diff it belongs to no generator, so the filtering waist is the only place the question is answerable, and the third-party comparison arms ride on the same structure as a standing build-versus-buy measurement. Three candidates were folded in as strengthening bullets rather than split off: the three-to-nine-hour time-to-first-review metric, the negative turn budget under a review deadline, and the expanding-outer-loop position. Four tensions were recorded as open questions rather than resolved, including one internal to the talk — automatic approvals on a percentage of code and expanded human review are both presented as the destination with no boundary between them. Created a source note and 4 new concept pages, strengthened 25 existing pages, updated 4 topic pages, inserted 12 concept-index entries across 4 groups, appended one processed-sources row, prepended one Current Emphasis bullet, appended exactly one log entry. No git commits; no background processes; notes.md untouched.

This is the wiki's first source in which the verification question is answered by a proof rather than by a better checker, and the page-worthiness test was which *guarantee* each idea changes rather than which topic it covers. The anchor is an asymmetry, not rigour: proof search cost a week and 32,000 lines while the checker stays a small kernel with open-source implementations "in C++, Rust, Lean" that "you yourself can actually go write," which is the only verification property in this wiki whose cost does not grow with agent output — and it is a strictly stronger form of the existing method-independence rule, since the checker can be eliminated as a shared point of failure rather than merely diversified. The second page carries what the proof does not: with "humans own the specification and machines own the code and proof," a wrong specification is not merely unhelpful but certified, auto-formalization adds an unchecked link between the sentence and the formal spec, and testing survives by changing target from implementation to specification. The third page separates what is proved from what is tested in AWS's Cedar — the Lean model is proved, the shipping Rust binary's conformance is sampled by "about 100 million differential random tests run nightly" — a boundary the talk does not mark and a copying team should. The fourth reorganizes the tool landscape (Lean, Verus on Z3, Aeneas over Rust's MIR, AWS's work-in-progress Strata) by which translation into the prover's logic each one performs, which is the cost that actually decides adoption. Every new page, every topic paragraph, and the processed-sources row records that nothing in the ten-minute talk is measured, that proof maintenance under change is never raised, and that three of the five named artifacts are the speaker's employer's.

This is one of the few sources in the wiki that reports a measured organizational result, so the page-worthiness test applied here was which *claim about the evidence* each idea changes rather than which practice it recommends — the five habits themselves are largely already in this wiki, and three of the four new pages come from material around them. The anchor page is the evidence design: three internal studies run in sequence, with Liguori naming the confound in each (elite staffing and greenfield, then protected conditions and a pre-decomposed backlog) and the third holding the tool roughly constant at 90% adoption while the outcome still split in half, which supports "the tool is not sufficient" but not "the tool does not matter" — a distinction the talk's own "it wasn't about the tools" phrasing blurs and the page states explicitly. The second page extracts the arithmetic behind a symptom this wiki already records: babysitting caps the multiplier because a human waiting out 30-second turns is *serialized* and cannot occupy a second loop, so faster models only shorten the wait, and the exit is a payload change (task plus self-validation criteria, promoted to the steering file) rather than a harness change. The third records the bottleneck leaving engineering altogether — a two-month build decision and a two-month launch approval that were rounding errors against a 9-12 month build are now the long pole — which explains how an engineering-side rollout can succeed on every internal metric and change nothing a customer sees. The fourth is the transition cost that the wiki's existing cross-sectional hygiene page omits: "in almost every team that was interviewed, they reported that their productivity actually went down," with the real failure being a leadership layer that reads the dip as underperformance, a failure Liguori attributes to herself and her peers. Two candidate pages — pruning steering files as models improve, and the frontier-developer behavioural definition — were dropped after reading their nearest neighbours (`a-harness-fix-becomes-overhead-when-the-model-outgrows-it`, `measure-ai-transformation-by-outcomes-instead-of-adoption`) and folded in as strengthening bullets instead. Every figure in the talk is self-reported internal Amazon data measured on output proxies (commits, then deployment velocity) with no defect, revert, rework, or incident adjustment, no control arm, no per-group sample size, and no team reported to have changed how it worked and failed; that limit is written inline on all four new pages, in all four topic paragraphs, in the processed-sources row, and in a dedicated Provenance and Limits section.

The anchor page is the layer map, because the talk's actual contribution is disambiguation rather than advocacy — MCP, A2A, ACP, UCP, and AP2 are usually met as competitors and four of them compose, which the demo makes visible by showing an A2A message and an MCP `product_search` call inside one user turn. The second page is the sentence the whole protocol argument rests on, generalized off commerce: a user interface collapses distinctions the counterparty's systems treat as structurally different, so a UI-shaped integration passes its own tests and corrupts the other side's state. The feed page contradicts an existing wiki position — [Expose Commerce Data Through Agent-Native Product APIs](concepts/expose-commerce-data-through-agent-native-product-apis.md) treats per-merchant feeds as the legacy path a unified API replaces, and Prio reports the two largest surfaces requiring exactly that, for a reason an aggregation layer does not remove; the reconciliation (aggregation is valuable to the merchant as a fan-out point, not as a substitute for feeds) is written on both pages. The checkout-state-machine and protocol-compliance pages carry the two most reusable implementation facts: one canonical session model plus per-spec serializers, and an eval whose oracle is owned by your distribution channel and whose failure mode is silent de-listing. The abuse page keeps the Chipotle story with the speaker's own disclaimer attached and puts the weight on the merchant-specific leaks instead — discount codes and other customers' checkout activity, neither of which a generic PII or toxicity filter models. The talk contains no measurement of anything: Best Buy's implementation is never described, the 45%-of-sessions and $7B-to-$65B figures are unattributed, latency is recommended as an eval axis with no figure reported, and every protocol support claim is present-tense; that limit is written inline on all six new pages, in all five topic paragraphs, in the processed-sources row, and in a twelve-item Provenance and Limits section.

The wiki had a large inference and infrastructure cluster with nothing underneath it about the interconnect, and this source supplies both the trend (7.2x compute against 3x and 2x communication from A100 to B200) and the measured headroom (a PyTorch-plus-NCCL baseline below 50% of its communication-aware roofline on most of 87 problems), which reframes several existing pages: fabric counters are load-bearing for an arithmetic reason, disaggregating across hardware backends is another state transfer, and a precision change is a five-to-six-month kernel port. Three of the new pages are the systems tutorial the talk gives before its results — transfer mechanism as a three-column ledger, overlap schedule chosen by data alignment, and the case against bulk-collective orchestration — and they are the material the model half of the talk grades against. On the evaluation side the durable finding is that correctness and quality do not scale together: parallel sampling took correct solutions from 28 to 36 of 87 while the correct-and-faster share stalled near 31%, and a mini-SWE-agent plus bash harness took Gemini 3 Pro from 24 to 35 and then plateaued, with the cause being that a slow-but-correct artifact returns no error to branch away from. The strongest negative result is recorded in its precise form — the gap survives supplying the hand-derived principles in context — and the comparison caveat (the 28 figure is the best model's, the 24-to-35 gain is within Gemini 3 Pro) is written on the pages rather than smoothed. No absolute ParallelKittens performance numbers, no roofline derivation, no model list or sample counts, and no benchmark of the three net-new kernels appear in the talk; everything concrete is NVIDIA-specific, which is written into the Inference topic's new open question about cross-vendor portability.

- Notes: The next unprocessed video by upload_date desc / video_id asc, read directly from its WebVTT transcript with no extraction script. This is the first source in the wiki whose subject is go-to-market as an engineering surface rather than as marketing advice, so it opened a `go-to-market` topic and pulled five existing GTM concepts into it that had been scattered across Product Strategy. The anchor addition is the artifact the talk asks for — "go-to-market is a data problem" whose deliverable is "a live model of your world that agents can act on" — which reorganizes a pile of GTM automations into readers of one substrate. Two surfaces over it got their own pages: exhaustive market classification, which is a genuinely different operation from ranked retrieval and is what an embedding index over a corpus makes affordable (filed against the wiki's existing Exa neural-search page and its index-versus-rediscover economics page), and an account event layer whose most valuable trigger is an absence — a customer who stops produces no rows, so the detector has to run over expected activity rather than observed activity. The clone project split into two methods that fail differently: a persona measured off 760 of the speaker's own emails (18-word average, "best" not "sincerely"), which is checkable on generated output, and judgment evals mined from hundreds of past decisions recovered from Slack and email, which is the strongest methodological claim in the talk and carries no pass rate, baseline, or held-out split. Its security answer generalized better than expected: bind the capability set to the *caller* rather than to the agent — owner gets reads, writes, and all tools; everyone else gets drafts and fewer tools — which is a third option next to the wiki's existing personal-versus-team scoping rule, and the page records what drafting does not fix (the draft is produced from the owner's full context). Also filed: buy-versus-build replaced by a single customizability test that a bought system with an MCP server passes, the learnability argument against generating every interface (a fourth axis the static/declarative/generative spectrum was missing), and a forward-deployed org that runs deals and builds the deal tooling because AI merged two jobs. Nothing in the talk is measured and the classification substrate is the speaker's own product, so every page carries the mechanism and the limits rather than a result.

- Updated concepts: new — [Reduce Every Workflow to Know, Decide, Act, and Learn](concepts/reduce-every-workflow-to-know-decide-act-and-learn.md), [Put Humans and Agents on the Same Substrate Instead of an AI Layer on Top](concepts/put-humans-and-agents-on-the-same-substrate-instead-of-an-ai-layer-on-top.md), [Compute Truth in the Warehouse and Serve It as a Denormalized Profile](concepts/compute-truth-in-the-warehouse-and-serve-it-as-a-denormalized-profile.md), [Make Routing and Eligibility a Shared First-Class Primitive](concepts/make-routing-and-eligibility-a-shared-first-class-primitive.md), [Keep Agents Off the Customer Channel and Treat Inbound Forms as Untrusted Input](concepts/keep-agents-off-the-customer-channel-and-treat-inbound-forms-as-untrusted-input.md), [Emit Owner-Assigned Tasks From Signals, With a Marketing Default When None Fire](concepts/emit-owner-assigned-tasks-from-signals-with-a-marketing-default-when-none-fire.md), [Shadow Your Best Human Before Encoding the Workflow](concepts/shadow-your-best-human-before-encoding-the-workflow.md), [Own the Context Layer and Rent Every Other Layer](concepts/own-the-context-layer-and-rent-every-other-layer.md), [Thread Every Outcome Back to the Decision That Caused It](concepts/thread-every-outcome-back-to-the-decision-that-caused-it.md); strengthened 35 existing pages across the go-to-market world model and data serving (5), untrusted input and approval boundaries (7), signals and proactive work routing (4), durable execution and multi-agent reliability (4), build-versus-buy and layer ownership (3), expert capture and floor-raising (4), outcome-driven evaluation and decision logs (5), and shared substrates for humans and agents (3)
- Notes: the wiki's third go-to-market source and the first that is a system design rather than a program or a product tour. Its anchor contribution is the four-question decomposition (know / decide / act / learn) plus the correction Liu makes to her own diagram — humans and agents must read one displayed substrate, or two systems drift apart. Underneath it, three components had no page in the wiki: a compute/serve split where the warehouse produces modeled versioned entities and a key-value store publishes a join-free profile with agent-generated artifacts keyed alike; a signal service that assigns ownership and routes the no-signal majority to predictive lifecycle marketing rather than dropping it; and eligibility as a shared, versioned primitive with one routing classifier in front. Two decisions generalize past go-to-market: an inbound contact-sales form classified as untrusted input because an agent sits in the middle (the injection trifecta assembled with no browser anywhere, mitigated by cutting the outbound leg rather than filtering the input), and build-versus-buy resolved per layer with debuggability as the second reason to keep the context layer. The method pages are shadowing the best performer ("that was chaos, but it was also the spec") with the floor-raising payoff, and outcome-to-decision threading as the only thing that turns "did it work" into an input. The talk is unfinished and almost unmeasured — two figures, both without baselines — so every page carries the mechanism plus what was not measured.

- Updated concepts: new — [Measure Agent Recommendations on Pain Prompts, Not Comparison Prompts](concepts/measure-agent-recommendations-on-pain-prompts-not-comparison-prompts.md), [Benchmark Your Own Tool by Running Agents With and Without It](concepts/benchmark-your-tool-by-running-agents-with-and-without-it.md), [Count Burned Turns, Because Agent Self-Recovery Hides Tool Defects](concepts/count-burned-turns-because-agent-self-recovery-hides-tool-defects.md), [Stale Product Content Compounds Through Newer Models](concepts/stale-product-content-compounds-through-newer-models.md), [Self-Serve Onboarding Is a Precondition for Agent Recommendation](concepts/self-serve-onboarding-is-a-precondition-for-agent-recommendation.md), [Human and Agent Credibility Reward Opposite Writing Styles](concepts/human-and-agent-credibility-reward-opposite-writing-styles.md), [Split Agent Advocacy Into Engineering, Product, and Marketing Flavors](concepts/split-agent-advocacy-into-engineering-product-and-marketing-flavors.md), [Treat Agent Experience as a Curb Cut](concepts/treat-agent-experience-as-a-curb-cut.md), [Agents Widen the Dev-Tool ICP Beyond Engineers](concepts/agents-widen-the-dev-tool-icp-beyond-engineers.md); strengthened 28 existing pages across agent-experience and agent-as-user framing (5), tool descriptions and training priors (4), trace reading and benchmark controls (5), assistant discoverability and content freshness (5), onboarding and distribution (5), and the widened dev-tool persona (4)
- Notes: the wiki's first source that treats the agent as a *recommender* rather than only as a product, buyer, or user, and the first that measures it. Two instruments arrive together: a with-and-without benchmark over software-lifecycle tasks whose traces expose defects no pass rate reports (a model guessing `read line` where the tool wanted `start line`, recovering from the error text, and burning a turn on a run that still succeeded), and a GEO prompt set whose partition by intent produced 65% mentions on comparison prompts against zero on the pain prompt the product exists for. Three findings underneath generalize past developer relations: stale claims about a product compound through newer models rather than decaying, so a model upgrade is not a remediation plan; human-gated onboarding disqualifies you from being recommended, one step earlier in the funnel than conversion; and credibility bifurcates, with developers penalizing recognizable model cadence while assistants favor their own content shape. The talk is a self-reported vendor pilot with no aggregate benchmark result and single-condition point estimates, so every page carries the mechanism plus what was not measured.

- Source: [How AI Agents Let GTM Teams Scale — Justin Joyce, Cloudflare](sources/20260826_Qw_tC68KKes.md)
- Processed: `Qw_tC68KKes`
- Updated topics: [Go To Market](topics/go-to-market.md), [Business Intelligence](topics/business-intelligence.md), [Workflows](topics/workflows.md), [Agents](topics/agents.md), [Context Engineering](topics/context-engineering.md), [Tools](topics/tools.md), [Evaluation](topics/evaluation.md), [Product Strategy](topics/product-strategy.md), [Security](topics/security.md)
- Updated concepts: new — [Split a Generated Narrative Into Drafter, Fact-Checker, and Tone Agents](concepts/split-generated-narrative-into-drafter-checker-and-tone-agents.md), [Read Every Run for Months Before Trusting an Unevaluatable Narrative](concepts/read-every-run-for-months-before-trusting-an-unevaluatable-narrative.md), [Pre-Shape Analytics Data by Time, Slice, and Metric Before the Agent Reads It](concepts/pre-shape-analytics-data-by-time-slice-and-metric-before-the-agent-reads-it.md), [Put the Business Question Set Inside the Skill File, Not Just the Schema](concepts/put-the-business-question-set-inside-the-skill-file-not-just-the-schema.md), [Push the Narrative Because Dashboard Adoption Is Always Uneven](concepts/push-the-narrative-because-dashboard-adoption-is-always-uneven.md), [Layer Ask, Push, and Self-Serve Because Teams Interface Differently](concepts/layer-ask-push-and-self-serve-because-teams-interface-differently.md), [Run a Submission-and-Review Alias for Shared Skills](concepts/run-a-submission-and-review-alias-for-shared-skills.md), [Separate the Context Gap From the Expert Gap](concepts/separate-the-context-gap-from-the-expert-gap.md), [Read-Side Agents Scale First Because the Write Side Needs Approvals](concepts/read-side-agents-scale-first-because-the-write-side-needs-approvals.md); strengthened 36 existing pages across multi-agent review pipelines and per-call observability (5), skills as business semantics (6), BI and text-to-SQL determinism (5), proactive push surfaces and eval-to-action (4), skill governance and registries (4), self-serve workspaces and form factors (4), the write path into systems of record (4), and floor-raising and adoption measurement (4)
- Notes: the wiki's fourth go-to-market source and the first told from the back office of a large sales organization, which is why its contributions are about delivery and determinism rather than about agent design. Four things had no page. A recurring generated narrative gets three agents where the third optimizes framing rather than truth, because a fact-checked summary can still bury the bad news. Its reliability comes from the data — filters, aggregations, and trend detection engineered up front into tables keyed by time, organizational slice, and metric — which relocates the silently-omitted-filter bug from the model into a transform where it is testable once and shared by every consumer. Its acceptance came from a human reading every single run for two to three months, a defensible burn-in at weekly cadence that left no defect taxonomy, exit criterion, or re-run policy behind. And the delivery argument is distributional: metric adoption inside a company has a tail that will never open a dashboard, so a request queue, a pushed digest, and a self-serve workspace run concurrently rather than as rungs. Two organizational entries follow — the business's real question set shipped inside the skill file, whose second consumer turns out to be internal app-building, and a central submission alias reviewed by two constituencies against a "Cambrian stage" of skill proliferation — plus the frontline diagnosis that separates the context gap from the expert gap, and the honest limit that every deployed use case is a read while quoting, approvals, and CRM writes are still ahead. Nothing in the talk is measured with a stated method, including the closing "2x our efficiency," so every page carries the mechanism plus what was not measured.

strengthened 37 existing pages across the world-model data layer and its economics (7), orchestration and durable execution (6), long-horizon and per-entity agents (5), the write path into systems of record (5), the human-agent boundary (4), build-versus-buy and staffing (4), signals and triggers (3), and channel base rates and distribution (3)
- Notes: the wiki's fifth go-to-market source, and the first that treats the live world model as something you buy rather than something you build — which is what makes it additive to four accounts that already describe the same architecture. Three mechanics had no page: coverage by waterfalling data vendors with per-provider evals as the ordering mechanism, freshness as a per-field budget ranked by volatility because purchased refresh is metered, and entity resolution as an admission requirement rather than a cleanup pass. Orchestration is reclassified as distributed data engineering across 10-30 tools, with one failure that is not a bug in your code: the vendors sync to each other outside your orchestrator, so a record you just created is not yet actionable elsewhere and the workflow has to poll for readiness. Two positions cut against the rest of the cluster and were recorded as such. Berry's agents write to the CRM with no approval gate, contained instead by owning their own columns — a third option between read-only and human-approved that bounds the damage without bounding the error. And asked for the hardest problem across his own four-part stack, he names none of them: the interface between the human and the agent, split into a rep who disagrees with a decision and a rep who never learns one was made, which is the failure a shared substrate makes knowable without making known. The execution section supplies the wiki's first channel base rates (0.5-1% replies) and the deliverability externality that shapes agentic outbound. Everything architectural is unmeasured, the two quantitative claims are second-hand and partly discounted by the speaker himself, and the learning loop is explicitly unsolved, so every page carries the mechanism plus what was not measured.

- Processed: `V_5bn4q-vAI`
- Updated topics: [Tools](topics/tools.md), [Coding Agents](topics/coding-agents.md), [Product Strategy](topics/product-strategy.md), [Go To Market](topics/go-to-market.md), [Context Engineering](topics/context-engineering.md), [Evaluation](topics/evaluation.md)
- Updated concepts: new — [Ship Bundled Docs and an AGENTS.md Inside the Published Package](concepts/ship-bundled-docs-and-an-agents-md-inside-the-published-package.md), [Hand-Write llms.txt and Index the Rest for Fetching](concepts/hand-write-llms-txt-and-index-the-rest-for-fetching.md), [Serve Markdown Through Three Redundant Paths](concepts/serve-markdown-through-three-redundant-paths.md), [Generate Agent-Facing Docs Artifacts From One Markdown Source](concepts/generate-agent-facing-docs-artifacts-from-one-markdown-source.md), [Score Agent-Readiness Against a Moving Baseline](concepts/score-agent-readiness-against-a-moving-baseline.md), [Attribute LLM-Sourced Inbound With a How-Did-You-Hear Field](concepts/attribute-llm-sourced-inbound-with-a-how-did-you-hear-field.md), [The Install Handoff Is Now a Prompt](concepts/the-install-handoff-is-now-a-prompt.md); strengthened 16 existing pages across agent-readable web surfaces and Markdown delivery (4), the docs-freshness and skills-pointer cluster (4), agent-experience framing and distribution (4), and agent-experience measurement (4)
- Notes: the wiki's agent-experience cluster had a thesis, two measurement instruments, and no shipped outcome; this source supplies one, plus the tactics under it. Four things had no page. Coding agents working on a library mostly never reach the documentation site — they read the repository and `node_modules` and reconstruct the API from compiled source — so the highest-leverage placement is inside the published package next to an `AGENTS.md`, which is also the only agent-experience surface in the wiki that costs the consumer nothing to adopt and is version-pinned by the lockfile. The orientation file should be hand-written rather than generated, with the density claim stated as forty good lines against a thousand lines of noise, and its companion built as a link index because agents fetch rather than browse — which surfaced a naming conflict with an earlier source that describes `llms-full.txt` as the whole corpus in one file, recorded on the page as two opposite artifacts rather than one convention. Markdown is served three ways because the standards-clean `Accept`-header route assumes a client capability many agents lack, and ordering fallbacks by required capability is the generalizable part. And agent-readiness graders now exist but move under you: a score fell from substantially higher to 59 in three weeks with no site change, which makes the delta informative and the level meaningless. Two distribution pages complete the pair with the wiki's prompt-harness method — a free-text signup question is the only place a referrer-less assistant recommendation surfaces, and the install handoff compressing into a pasted prompt is why prior DX work converts rather than being superseded. Nothing in the talk is measured with a stated method, including the ~50% token saving and the inbound ranking, so every page carries the mechanism plus what was not measured.
